# Edit page behaviour spec

What a user coming from DaVinci Resolve's Edit page expects Kinoklip's Edit page to do, written
in our own words from general knowledge of how NLEs behave (docs/archive/plan.md 5.10). Nothing here is copied
from Resolve documentation. Every item has the expected behaviour and Kinoklip's status, found
by testing it (unit / gesture tests, or the running app).

Status legend:
- **OK**: already behaved like this; verified.
- **Fixed**: differed or was missing; implemented on the `parity` branch.
- **Decision**: the convention is ambiguous or we deliberately differ; the reason is given and
  the behaviour is switchable where it is convention-dependent.
- **Deferred**: not done yet, with the reason (usually: needs a new subsystem).

Tests: `tp` = `tests/timeline/test_parity.cpp` (test names start with the item number),
`tc` = `test_timelinecontroller`, `tw` = `test_timelinewidget`, `pe` = `test_playbackengine`,
`vp` = `test_viewerpanel`, `eo` = `test_editops` / `test_editops_timeline`, `in` =
`test_inspector`, `pe2` = `tests/timeline/test_parity_edit.cpp` (resolveedit branch), `er` =
`tests/core/test_editops_ripple.cpp`, `kp` = `tests/app/test_keyframespanel.cpp` (kfwindow branch), `ar` = `test_actionregistry`, `ke` =
`tests/timeline/test_keyframeeditor.cpp`, `ko` = `tests/core/test_keyframeops.cpp`, `an` =
`tests/core/test_animation.cpp`, `am` = `test_audiomixer` (keyframes branch), `ts` = `tests/timeline/test_transitionselect.cpp`, `ek` = `tests/app/test_editkeys.cpp` (editfix branch), `ea` = `tests/timeline/test_editaids.cpp`, `vs` =
`tests/playback/test_varispeed.cpp` (editpolish branch), `p2` = `tests/timeline/test_polish2.cpp` (polish2 branch), `p3` = `tests/timeline/test_polish3.cpp` (polish3 branch).

Shortcuts are data: the built-in keymap profile `resources/keymaps/resolve-windows.json`
(Resolve's Windows defaults, generated by `scripts/keymap-resolve-windows.py`; every key with a
confidence rating in `docs/keymap-resolve-windows.md`), remappable per profile in Keyboard
Customization;
convention switches live in `PlaybackBehaviour` (`src/playback/PlaybackEngine.h`, QSettings
`playback/*`) and `EditBehaviour` (`src/core/EditBehaviour.h`, QSettings `edit/*`).

---

## 1. Transport and J/K/L

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 1.1 | Space toggles play/pause of the active viewer (the one used last: source or timeline). | OK (pe stateMachineAndShuttle, vp transportFollowsActiveViewer) |
| 1.2 | L plays forward at 1x; each further L doubles the speed (2x, 4x, 8x, capped). | OK (pe stateMachineAndShuttle) |
| 1.3 | J plays backwards the same way; pressing the opposite key while shuttling restarts that direction at 1x. | OK (pe) |
| 1.4 | K pauses. | OK (pe) |
| 1.5 | K held + a tap on L or J steps one frame forward or back. | OK (pe kHeldStepsAndSlowPlays) |
| 1.6 | K held + L or J held plays slowly forward / backward until a key is released. | OK (pe kHeldStepsAndSlowPlays; slow play after 300 ms at 0.5x) |
| 1.7 | Play pressed on the last frame starts over from the beginning instead of doing nothing. | OK (pe stopsAtEndAndRestarts; `PlaybackBehaviour::restartAtEnd`) |
| 1.8 | Playback stops on the last frame of the timeline when loop is off. | OK (pe stopsAtEndAndRestarts) |
| 1.9 | Shift+Space plays in reverse at 1x (again: pause). | Fixed (`transport.play_reverse`; tp spec1_keymapMatchesSpec) |
| 1.10 | Loop toggle: repeats in..out if both marks exist, else the whole timeline, seamlessly. | OK (pe loopsOverInOut; Ctrl+/) |
| 1.11 | Play in to out plays the marked range once. | OK (pe playInToOutStopsAtOut; Alt+/) |
| 1.12 | "Play around current frame" (/) plays a pre-roll before to a post-roll after the playhead and returns to it. | Fixed (key was Alt+Space, now /; pre/post-roll in `PlaybackBehaviour`) |
| 1.13 | Left / Right step one frame and pause; holding repeats. | OK (pe stepAndSeconds) |
| 1.14 | Shift+Left / Shift+Right move one second. | OK (pe stepAndSeconds) |
| 1.15 | Transport keys act on the source viewer when it has focus, on the timeline otherwise. | OK (vp transportFollowsActiveViewer) |
| 1.16 | Audio is heard while shuttling, in reverse and in slow play (pitched). | Fixed (editpolish: AudioFeeder varispeed, the device stays the clock at every rate; vs spec1_16_*). Decision: the pitch follows the speed like a tape, also at 8x; Pitch Correction of retimed clips is not applied to shuttling. Polish round 2: the resampler is a Kaiser-windowed sinc whose cutoff follows the speed (`VarispeedResampler`), so 2x - 16x no longer alias (linear interpolation folded everything above 24 kHz / rate back as hiss); the mixed window slides forwards so EQ and dynamics run on unbroken (vs spec1_16_shuttleDoesNotAlias). |
| 1.17 | J/K/L ignore keyboard auto-repeat (holding L does not ramp to 8x). | OK (actions have auto-repeat off) |
| 1.18 | The first play after an import or a load starts picture and sound together: a play from rest whose start frame is not on screen yet holds the clock and the sound until the first frame is ready (at most 800 ms), instead of starting the sound while the picture catches up with dropped frames. The hardware decoding device is opened in the background at start-up. | Fixed (polish round 2; pe playFromRestWaitsForTheFirstFrame, tests/app/test_firstplay.cpp measures import to first frame and first sound). |
| 1.19 | Space right after an import plays without dropping frames: the media cache's thumbnail decoders for the new filmstrip no longer open hardware decoders next to the playback's own (software up to 1920x1200, hardware for larger pictures). | Fixed (polish round 3; test_firstplay: 720p / 1080p 0 dropped, was 3 - 6; 2160p HEVC 1 - 2, was 6; numbers in polish-v3.md) |
| 1.20 | Preferences > System > Audio I/O "Automatic" plays on the desktop's current output device and follows it: headphones connected (which the desktop makes the default), disconnected, or a switch in the desktop's audio applet move the sound of every viewer and page at once, also in the middle of a play, without stopping it. | Fixed (audiodevice branch, 0.4.0 owner report "only this app plays through the speakers"; ar = tests/playback/test_audiorouting.cpp: automaticFollowsTheSystemDefault; manual check below). Cause: the device was resolved once per output on first use and Qt opens a stream on a named device (also for the default), so it never followed the desktop. |
| 1.21 | A device chosen in Preferences applies when Preferences is saved, without a restart, to every viewer and page, also while playing. While it is not connected the system's device plays; when it is connected again the sound goes back to it. | Fixed (ar chosenDeviceWins, missingChosenDeviceFallsBackAndComesBack; `AudioDeviceMonitor::reloadPreference()`) |
| 1.22 | A device switch during a play keeps the picture in sync and running: only the stream is replaced, the buffered sound, its position and the playback clock continue (the clock steps forwards over the few ms the old device still held, never backwards). | Fixed (ar switchKeepsTheRingAndTheClock, trackerNeverGoesBackwards, int16AfterSwitch) |
| 1.23 | Each device switch is logged in kk.playback with the old device, the new one and why ("audio device switch: Speakers -> Bluetooth Headphones (the system default output changed)"), and each moved stream once more. | Fixed (`AudioDeviceMonitor::refresh()`) |
| 1.24 | The Audio I/O device list is updated while Preferences is open when devices are connected or removed; its first entry names what Automatic currently plays on ("Automatic (Bluetooth Headphones)"); a chosen device that is not connected stays in the list as "(not connected)". | Fixed (`AudioIoPage`). Unverified with real devices in tests. |
| 1.25 | Manual check (real devices, not testable here): play the timeline on the speakers, connect Bluetooth headphones: the sound moves to them within about a second and the picture keeps playing; switch the output back in the Plasma audio applet while playing: the sound follows; disconnect the headphones: the speakers take over. Started from a terminal (QT_LOGGING_RULES="kk.playback.info=true" if info messages are filtered), each step prints a "kk.playback: audio device switch" line. | Unverified (owner) |
| 1.26 | Where no video clip covers the playhead the timeline viewer shows black, while playing, paused and scrubbing, also after many edits: playing across a clip's end into a gap, seeking into it, compound and retimed clips, cached chunks, gaps made by lifts and by ripple trims (paused on the edit point and while playing). A change inside a compound clip's timeline that leaves the parent's own clips as they are (Clip Attributes of media used inside it, its Timeline Settings, a clip dropped into it in the stacked timeline) shows in the parent's playback at once. A frame on screen that an edit made outdated is rendered again when playback starts or resumes on it. Export renders gaps black as well. | Fixed (0.4.1, owner report "no video on the timeline but the previous clips' video is still playing"; pg = tests/playback/test_playbackgap.cpp: playAcrossClipEndIntoGap, seekIntoGap, gapMadeByEditing, cachedCompound, compoundLongerThanContent, manyRippleTrims, nestedChangeReachesParent). Cause found: the engine treats an edit as sound-only (keeps rendering the previous pictures) when the video tracks are unchanged, and nested timelines always compared equal (`NestedTimelines::operator==`), so a changed compound clip kept playing its old content; `render::sameNestedTimelines()` now compares the nested maps. The owner's exact sequence was not reproduced; the gap and ripple trim cases above pass on 0.4.0 too and guard against regressions. |
| 1.27 | Audio scrubbing plays only while the playhead is moved by its head, in a ruler: a press or drag in the Edit page ruler (on the head or anywhere along the ruler, which jumps the head there) scrubs with sound. The line through the tracks is no drag handle: a click or drag on it acts on what is under it (selects or moves the clip, selects the gap, draws a rubber band) and neither moves the playhead nor plays scrub audio. Frame steps and navigation keep their snippet (23.7). | Fixed (redditfb, Reddit feedback on 1.0.0-beta.1; `PlaybackEngine::SilentPlayheadMove`; tests/timeline/test_playheadscrub.cpp spec1_27_*). Decision: as in Resolve's Edit page the ruler, the viewer's scrub bar and the keyframe ruler are the playhead's handles and scrub audibly; only moves made by the line itself are silent. |
| 1.28 | The Cut page's upper timeline draws a head on its playhead in the ruler strip at the top. A press or drag there scrubs with sound; a press or drag below it (over the clips) still moves the playhead line for navigation, but silently. | Fixed (redditfb; `CutOverview`; test_playheadscrub spec1_28_cutOverviewScrubsOnlyFromTheRuler) |
| 1.29 | Playing across a cut shows the first frame of the next clip on time, also when the clip starts far from a keyframe in a long-GOP file: while playing, the next clip's decoder is opened, seeked and decoded forward to its first frame on a worker thread about 1 second ahead, and the render thread finds it ready (before: it did all that itself when the frame was already due, the picture held for 300 ms and about 15 frames were lost). | Fixed (cutstutter; `test_cutlookahead` preparedDecoderIsReadyBeforeTheCut, requestDuringPreparationWaits, reservationKeptAndIdleRecycled, compositorPrepare, engineDeliversCutFramesOnTime: margin 3 frames, median of the cuts; numbers from `test_cutperf`, src/playback/readme.md) |
| 1.30 | The look-ahead sees every clip coming into view within that second: past the next clip in a row of short clips, on every enabled video track, inside compound clips, at a transition's start, at a loop's jump back, and through a proxy when one stands in. A razor cut or a jump of a few frames in the same file needs no preparation (the playing decoder gets there). | Fixed (`test_cutlookahead` decodeTargets, manyShortClips, twoTracksCutTogether, loopWrapIsPrepared) |
| 1.31 | A seek or an edit while the look-ahead works stops it within one decoded frame and starts over from the new position or the new clips; the stale work never holds up the frame that is due. | Fixed (`test_cutlookahead` cancelStopsPreparation, prepareFrameStopsMidway, schedulerRanges, seekAndEditCancel) |
| 1.32 | Reverse play and J/K/L at 4x and above do not use the look-ahead and behave as before; forward slow play, 1x and 2x use it. | Decision (in reverse every frame seeks anyway; at 4x+ the frame after a cut is not known in advance; `test_cutlookahead` shuttleSpeeds) |
| 1.33 | The look-ahead's memory is bounded however many cuts are ahead: at most 3 decoders held for it and 2 working, on the existing decode threads; cuts that cannot be prepared in time simply decode at the cut. A decoder nobody came for is released after its due time. | Fixed (`test_cutlookahead` queuedWorkStaysBounded, overdueReservationExpires, missingFile) |
| 1.34 | The sound has no gap at cuts: the mixer opens the next clip's audio on its own thread, inside the 170 ms ring it keeps ahead of the device (open + seek + first samples: 6 - 38 ms measured). | OK (measured, nothing changed; 0 underrun frames with a fake sound card, `test_cutlookahead` engineDeliversCutFramesOnTime, `test_cutperf`; a real sound card is untested) |
| 1.35 | While the program plays or shuttles, no new thumbnail, poster or waveform job starts: they are parked (newest 256 kept) so the playback decoder keeps all cores, and start, newest first, when playback stops. Jobs already running finish. (GitHub issue 2: FPS dropped when the view paged during playback.) | Fixed (`test_mediacache` jobsWaitWhilePlaybackIsActive, parkedJobsAreBounded) |
| 1.36 | Supersedes 1.35: while the program plays, parked thumbnail / poster / waveform jobs still run, but at most one image job and one waveform job at a time, newest first, at the lowest thread priority, so a filmstrip fills slowly instead of staying blank. When playback stops everything parked is released. The cap of 256 parked jobs stays. | Fixed (`test_mediacache` parkedJobsTrickleWhilePlaybackIsActive, jobsWaitWhilePlaybackIsActive, parkedJobsAreBounded) |

## 2. Playhead and navigation

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 2.1 | Clicking / dragging in the ruler moves the playhead; with snapping on it snaps to edit points, marks and markers. | OK (tw scrubRulerMovesPlayhead) |
| 2.2 | Up / Down jump to the previous / next edit point (clip start or end). | OK (tc navigation) |
| 2.3 | Only tracks whose auto track selector is on (and that are not locked) count for Up / Down. | Fixed (tp spec2_3_upDownOnlyAutoSelectTracks) |
| 2.4 | Home goes to the first frame, End to the frame after the last clip. | OK (tp spec2_4_homeEnd) |
| 2.5 | The playhead never goes before frame 0. | OK (tc navigation) |
| 2.6 | Clicking a clip with the selection tool selects it without moving the playhead. | OK (tw clickAndRubberBandSelection) |
| 2.7 | Shift+I / Shift+O go to the in / out mark (out: the last marked frame). | OK (tc marksAndMarkers) |
| 2.8 | Keyboard navigation reveals the playhead if it is outside the view. | OK (tw followPlayheadPageScrolls) |
| 2.9 | Shift+Up / Shift+Down jump to the previous / next marker. | Fixed (tp spec2_9_markerNavigation) |
| 2.10 | With the timeline or the timeline viewer focused, typing digits enters a timecode, shown in the timeline viewer's timecode display: the digits fill HH:MM:SS:FF from the right ("1000" = 00:00:10:00), "." adds "00", Backspace removes a digit, Enter goes there, Esc (or any other key) cancels. A leading + / - makes it an offset: it moves the selected clips (or trims the selected edit point, like the nudge keys; one undo step), else the playhead. | Fixed (new; ea spec2_10_*). Decision: an absolute entry fills the missing high fields with zeros (timelines start at 00:00:00:00); the number keys stay multicam angle keys while the multicam viewer is shown. |

## 3. Marking

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 3.1 | I / O mark the viewer that has focus: the source clip in the source viewer, the timeline otherwise. | OK (vp sourceViewerLoadsClipAndMarks) |
| 3.2 | The out mark includes the frame under the playhead. | OK (tp spec3_2_3_outIncludesFrameAndInAfterOutClears) |
| 3.3 | Setting an in after the out (or an out before the in) clears the other mark. | OK (tp spec3_2_3) |
| 3.4 | Alt+I, Alt+O, Alt+X clear in, out, both. | OK (tc marksAndMarkers) |
| 3.5 | X marks in/out around the clip under the playhead (lowest auto-select video track first; a selected clip wins). | Fixed: used every track, now only auto-select tracks (tp spec3_5_markClipUsesAutoSelectTracks) |
| 3.6 | Shift+A marks in/out around the selected clips. | Fixed (tp spec3_6_markSelection) |
| 3.7 | The marked range is shown in the ruler and in the viewer's scrub bar. | OK (painter, ScrubBar; vp) |
| 3.8 | Timeline mark changes are undoable steps. | Decision: kept undoable (marks are saved with the timeline, so undo restoring them is the safer choice). |
| 3.9 | Source marks are remembered per clip. | OK (vp sourceViewerLoadsClipAndMarks) |
| 3.10 | The Deliver page render range follows the timeline In / Out: with both set (or moved) it becomes In/Out Range, cleared it goes back to Entire Timeline; a manual choice stays until the marks change (owner report on 0.4.0: the whole timeline was rendered). | Fixed (deliverpage renderRangeFollowsInOutMarks) |

## 4. Source edits (insert, overwrite, ...)

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 4.1 | F9 inserts the source range on the destination tracks, rippling every unlocked auto-select track by its length. | Fixed: rippled tracks whose auto-select was off too (tp spec4_1_insertRipplesOnlyAutoSelectTracks) |
| 4.2 | Three-point editing: with a timeline in mark the edit lands at the in mark, not at the playhead. | Fixed (tp spec4_2_editAtTimelineIn; `EditBehaviour::threePointEditing`) |
| 4.3 | With timeline in and out marks the timeline range sets the length (the source out is ignored; a shorter source makes a shorter clip). | Fixed (tp spec4_3_timelineInOutSetsDuration) |
| 4.4 | With only a timeline out mark the clip is backtimed: it ends at the out mark. | Fixed (tp spec4_4_timelineOutOnlyBacktimes) |
| 4.5 | F10 overwrites what is under the new clip. | OK (tc insertSourceOnTargets) |
| 4.6 | F12 place on top: the first free track above the destination, adding a track if needed. | OK (tc insertSourceOnTargets, eo placeOnTop*) |
| 4.7 | F11 replace: the clip under the playhead (destination video track first) is replaced by the source from its in point, keeping position and length. | Fixed (new; tp spec4_7_replaceKeepsPlaceAndLength). Decision: with a source in mark the source is aligned on it (the in point at the clip start); without one the source viewer's playhead frame lines up with the timeline playhead (editpolish; ea spec4_7_replaceLinesUpTheSourcePlayhead). |
| 4.8 | Shift+F11 fit to fill: source in..out is retimed (speed change) to fill timeline in..out. Needs both ranges. | Fixed (new; tp spec4_8_fitToFillRetimes) |
| 4.9 | Shift+F12 append at end: overwrite after the last clip of the timeline. | Fixed (new; tp spec4_9_appendAtEnd) |
| 4.10 | After a source edit the playhead sits after the new clip and the new clips are selected. | OK (tc insertSourceOnTargets, tp spec4_1) |
| 4.11 | Shift+F10 ripple overwrite: the timeline in..out range (else the clip under the playhead) is replaced by the source at its own length; everything after it ripples by the difference (other auto-select tracks follow, when shorter only where they have room). | Fixed (new `editops::rippleOverwriteClips`; pe2 spec4_11, er rippleOverwrite*) |
| 4.12 | Video and audio of one source are placed together and linked. | OK (tc insertSourceOnTargets) |
| 4.13 | Stills get the standard still duration. | OK (tc stillsAndGenerators; `EditBehaviour::stillSeconds`) |
| 4.14 | Dragging a clip onto the timeline viewer shows an edit-type overlay (insert, overwrite, replace, fit to fill, place on top, append at end, ripple overwrite; each tile shows its key); dropping on a tile performs that source edit at the playhead with the usual destination rules (4.1 - 4.11), several media pool items one after the other as one undo step. | Fixed (editpolish; ea spec4_14_*). Decision: tiles in two rows over the whole viewer; Replace, Fit to Fill and Ripple Overwrite use only the first of several dropped items; media pool items bring the whole clip. |

## 5. Drag and drop to the timeline

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 5.1 | Dragging media or a marked source range onto a track overwrites there, linked V+A, with a ghost preview. | OK (tw dropMediaOverwritesAndCtrlInserts) |
| 5.2 | A modifier during the drop inserts instead of overwriting. | Decision: Ctrl inserts (no single convention is universal). |
| 5.3 | Effects dropped on a clip are applied to it; transitions dropped near an edge go to that edge. | OK (tw dropEffectsTransitionsAndTitles) |
| 5.4 | Titles / generators dropped on a video track create a clip of the standard generator length. | OK (tw dropEffectsTransitionsAndTitles) |
| 5.5 | A drop in the empty space above the top video track or below the last audio track creates only the tracks its clips land on: a video-only clip makes a new video track and no empty audio track, an audio-only clip a new audio track and no empty video track. | Fixed (bughunt10: a video-only clip dropped above V1 also created an empty A2; `planDrop()` keeps only the needed new tracks; test_timelinedrop spec5_5_videoOnlyAboveCreatesNoAudioTrack, spec5_5_audioOnlyBelowCreatesNoVideoTrack) |
| 5.6 | Files dragged onto the timeline from a file manager (Edit page, Cut page lower timeline) show the same ghost as a Media Pool drag while the drag goes on: one clip outline per file at the cursor time, named after the file, with its real length (probed once on DragEnter on worker threads and kept in a cache; a placeholder of the still duration until the probe answers), video on the video track under the cursor and its audio on the matching audio track, stills at the Preferences still duration, several files one after another, snapping and Ctrl insert as for a Media Pool drag. A file that cannot be decoded shows no ghost; dropping it places nothing and shows the usual "could not be imported" message. Leaving the timeline (or Escape) removes the ghost. The drop imports into the Media Pool's current bin, reuses the pool item of a file already imported (no second item, no message), and places the clips where the ghost was. Importing and placing are two undo steps, as when importing first and dragging from the pool: one Undo takes the clips off the timeline, the pool item stays. The first import's frame rate question (25.18) is still asked. | Fixed (extdrop: only the file manager's icon followed the cursor, the length was known only after the drop, the files went to the Master bin, and a file already in the pool was refused with a message; `DroppedFileProbe`, `platform::draggedLocalFiles()`, `TimelineWidget::planDrop()` / `importDroppedFiles()`; test_timelinedrop spec5_6_*). Decision: the drag's paths are read from text/uri-list or application/x-kde4-urilist only; a drag that offers nothing but a document-portal key has no ghost (its paths need a blocking D-Bus call) and still imports on drop. |

## 6. Destination controls and auto track selectors

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 6.1 | Each track header has a destination patch (V1, A1, ...); clicking it targets that track for source edits. | OK (tc insertSourceOnTargets) |
| 6.2 | Untargeting video or audio leaves that part out of source edits. | OK (tc insertSourceOnTargets) |
| 6.3 | Each track has an auto track selector, on by default, saved with the project. | Fixed (new `TrackData::autoSelect`; tp spec6_3_autoSelectDefaultOnAndSaved) |
| 6.4 | Commands without an explicit target use the auto-select tracks: Up/Down (2.3), split at playhead without selection (9.2), mark clip (3.5), default transition (13.2), trim to playhead (8.10), and the all-track ripple of insert (4.1) and gap removal (10.5). | Fixed |
| 6.5 | The selector is a header button; clicking toggles it (one undo step). | Fixed (tp spec6_5_headerButtonTogglesAutoSelect) |
| 6.6 | Alt-click on a selector makes it the only one on of its kind. | Fixed (new; tp spec6_6_altClickSoloesAutoSelect) |
| 6.7 | Locked tracks are never changed by any edit. | OK (eo, `checkInvariants`; tw headerButtonsToggleTrackState) |

## 7. Selection

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 7.1 | Clicking a clip selects it (and its linked clips when linked selection is on). | OK (tw clickAndRubberBandSelection) |
| 7.2 | Ctrl- (or Shift-) click adds or removes a clip. | OK (tw clickAndRubberBandSelection). Decision: Shift does the same as Ctrl (no range-select semantics). |
| 7.3 | Alt-click selects only the clicked part of a linked pair. | Fixed (tp spec7_3_altClickSelectsOneSide). Owner request on 0.4.0: an Alt-drag duplicates the clip with its linked partners (Alt-click without a drag still selects one side; tp spec7_3_altDragDuplicatesTheLinkedPair). |
| 7.4 | Linked selection toggles with Ctrl+Shift+L (and the toolbar button). | OK (tw linkedClipsMoveTogether) |
| 7.5 | Dragging in an empty area draws a rubber band that selects every touched clip; with Ctrl/Shift it adds. | OK (tw clickAndRubberBandSelection) |
| 7.6 | Clicking a clip edge selects that edit point (highlighted); at a cut the centre selects both sides (roll). | Fixed (new; tp spec7_6_clickEdgeSelectsEditPoint) |
| 7.7 | Ctrl+A selects all clips, Ctrl+Shift+A deselects. | OK (tc selectAllAndDeselect) |
| 7.8 | Clicking empty track area deselects. | OK (tw clickAndRubberBandSelection) |
| 7.9 | Clip, edit point and gap selections are exclusive: selecting one clears the others. | Fixed (tp spec7_6, spec10_5) |
| 7.10 | Pressing on a clip inside a multi-selection keeps the group for dragging; a click without drag reduces the selection to that clip. | OK (tw) |
| 7.11 | Y / Ctrl+Y select the clips forward / backward from the playhead on the auto-select tracks; Alt+Y / Ctrl+Alt+Y on all tracks. | Fixed (new; pe2 spec7_11) |
| 7.12 | V selects the edit point nearest to the playhead (video tracks first); at a cut both sides (roll). | Fixed (new; pe2 spec7_12) |
| 7.13 | Shift+V selects the clip under the playhead, else the gap under it. | Fixed (new; pe2 spec7_13) |
| 7.14 | An Alt-drag duplicate takes the clips' transitions along: one between two duplicated clips is copied as it is, one at the head or tail cut of a duplicated clip whose neighbour stays behind becomes the copy's head / tail transition, shortened to fit the copy's new neighbour (a fade from / to black without one, 13.6) and dropped where nothing fits or (at the tail) where the clip after the copy already has its own transition at that cut. The same rule as Ctrl+C / Ctrl+V (16.6). | Fixed (owner request on 0.4.0; tp spec7_3_altDragCarriesTransitions) |
| 7.15 | The rubber band (7.5) also starts in the empty space above the top track and below the bottom track (owner request); a plain click there deselects. | Fixed (tw rubberBandFromAboveTheTracks) |

## 8. Trimming

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 8.1 | Selection tool, dragging a clip edge: trims without ripple (leaves a gap; cannot overlap a neighbour). | OK (tw trimWithGapInSelectTool) |
| 8.2 | Selection tool, dragging the exact centre of a cut between two touching clips: roll. | Fixed (tp spec8_2_selectToolRollsAtCutCentre; `EditBehaviour::selectToolRollsAtCut`) |
| 8.3 | Trim tool, dragging an edge (either side of a cut): ripple trim of that side, later clips follow. | Fixed: the trim tool used to roll at every cut (tp spec8_3_trimToolRipplesEitherSideOfCut) |
| 8.4 | Trims are limited by the media handles and a minimum length of one frame. | OK (eo trim tests, rollClampedByMediaAndDuration) |
| 8.5 | Trim tool, centre of a cut: roll. | OK (tw rollAtEditPointInTrimTool) |
| 8.6 | Trim tool on a clip body: the upper name band slides the clip between its neighbours, the rest slips the content. | Fixed: was slip only, slide on Alt (Alt still swaps; tp spec8_6_trimToolNameBandSlides; `EditBehaviour::trimToolSlideInNameBar`) |
| 8.7 | Slip and slide are limited by media and neighbour lengths. | OK (eo slide*, tw slipInTrimTool) |
| 8.8 | , and . nudge the selected clips by one frame, or trim the selected edit point (roll if both sides; ripple in the trim tool). | Fixed: edit points could not be nudged (tp spec8_8_nudgeTrimsSelectedEdit) |
| 8.9 | Shift+, and Shift+. nudge / trim by several frames (5). | Fixed (new; tp spec8_8; `EditBehaviour::multiFrameNudge`) |
| 8.10 | Shift+[ / Shift+] trim the start / end of the clips under the playhead to the playhead and leave a gap; Ctrl+Shift+[ / Ctrl+Shift+] ripple-trim them (the gap closes, later clips move left, the playhead lands on the old clip start after a start trim). Selected clips, else the clips under the playhead on auto-select tracks; linked clips follow; locked tracks are untouched; one undo step. Ctrl+Shift+[ also works when the keyboard layout reports it as Ctrl+Shift+{ (US layouts under Wayland). | Fixed (alpha.2 feedback: Shift+[ / ] used to ripple through a setting, `edit/trimToPlayheadRipples`, now retired and removed at start-up; tp spec8_10_*; ek trimToPlayheadKeys) |
| 8.11 | E extends the selected edit point to the playhead. | Fixed (new; tp spec8_11_extendEdit) |
| 8.12 | With linked selection on, linked clips whose edge sits on the same frame trim / roll along. | OK for drags (tw), Fixed for keyboard trims (tp spec8_10) |
| 8.13 | Esc cancels a drag without leaving an undo step. | OK (tw escapeCancelsDrag) |
| 8.14 | Trims snap to edit points / playhead when snapping is on. | OK (tw moveSnapsToEdges uses the same snap path) |
| 8.15 | While trimming, the viewer shows a two-up of the frames on both sides of the edit: trim / roll: the outgoing clip's last frame and the incoming clip's first frame (black where there is a gap or the timeline edge); slip: the clip's new first and last frame; slide: the previous clip's new last frame and the next clip's new first frame. Names and timecodes under the pictures; the view closes when the drag ends. | Fixed (editpolish; ea spec8_15_*). Decision: the pictures are the clips' source frames (exact frames from the media cache, without effects or transforms), so the view does not compete with the program renderer. |
| 8.16 | U cycles the selected edit point between the outgoing side, the roll and the incoming side. | Fixed (new `trim.cycle_edit`; tp spec8_16_cycleEditPoint) |
| 8.17 | Dynamic trim (W): with an edit point selected, playback (J/K/L, Space) drags the edit point along with the playhead (roll; ripple in the trim tool); one undo step per run, closed when playback stops. | Fixed (new `trim.dynamic`; pe2 spec8_17). Decision: the edit follows the program playhead, no separate trim viewer (8.15). |
| 8.18 | A ripple trim shifts the clips of the trimmed track (and of the clips trimmed along), not other tracks. | Decision: matches the common behaviour without sync locks; documented in EditOps. |
| 8.19 | The two-up trim view can show each side with its clip's look: the clip alone (effects, transform, crop, masks, titles and generators; without its transitions, fades and the other tracks) rendered by the program compositor at the two-up's reduced size (at most 540 lines, through the proxies). | Decision (polish round 3; test_trimtwoup): opt-in (Preferences > Editing > Trimming, "Trim view shows effects and transforms"), source frames stay the default: the renders share the program viewer's single render thread with its still frames, and in the benchmark (a roll dragged one frame per 16 ms over 1080p) the program pictures were never current during the drag (the source frames were after 68 % of the steps) and needed about 0.2 s to catch up afterwards. GPU compositor only. |
| 8.20 | Trim tool with clips selected (no edit point): , and . (Shift: several frames) slip the clips by one frame as a one-frame drag of the clip's lower part to the right / left would; after a click in the name band they slide the clips instead; linked parts follow; one undo step "Slip Clip" / "Slide Clip". The selection tool still moves the clips. | Fixed (bughunt6: the keys moved the clips in the trim tool too; test_bughunt6edit spec8_20_trimToolKeysSlipAndSlide) |
| 8.21 | Dynamic trim (W) with clips selected instead of an edit point: playback slides the clips along with the playhead in the selection tool, slips them in the trim tool (slides them after a name band click); one undo step per playback run. | Fixed (bughunt6: dynamic trim needed an edit point; test_bughunt6edit spec8_21_dynamicTrimSlipsAndSlidesClips). Decision: playing forward acts as a drag to the right. |
| 8.22 | With an edit point selected, Up / Down move the selection to the previous / next edit of its track, keeping its side (outgoing, roll, incoming) where the new edit has it, else the side that is there; the playhead follows. Keyboard trimming thus goes V, U, , / ., Down, ... Without a selected edit point they move the playhead over the auto-select tracks' edits (2.3). | Fixed (bughunt6: the selection stayed behind; test_bughunt6edit spec8_22_upDownMoveTheSelectedEdit) |
| 8.23 | The timeline tool bar has a Dynamic Trim Mode button between Trim and Blade (Selection, Trim, Dynamic Trim, Blade), as in Resolve: it toggles dynamic trim like W and stays checked while the mode is on. Its icon is the Breeze "chronometer-start" glyph (no own icon art); without the Breeze theme the button shows its name. | Fixed (bughunt7: the mode had no tool bar button; test_bughunt7 spec8_23_dynamicTrimButtonBetweenTrimAndBlade) |
| 8.24 | Dynamic trim always trims with J / K / L while the mode is on; there is no preference to turn that off. | Decision (bughunt7: the unused setting `edit/dynamicTrimWithJkl` was removed; Resolve has no such option, the J / K / L trim is what the mode is for) |

## 9. Blade

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 9.1 | B selects the blade tool; clicking a clip cuts it (and its linked clips) at the frame under the pointer, snapping to the playhead when snapping is on; a hover line shows where. | OK (tw bladeSplitsLinkedClips) |
| 9.2 | Ctrl+\ splits at the playhead (Resolve's Windows default; Ctrl+B is not bound): the selected clips, or every auto-select track when nothing is selected. | Fixed: Ctrl+\ was missing and it cut every track (tp spec9_2_splitAtPlayheadUsesAutoSelect); resolveedit: Ctrl+B removed (owner feedback; tp spec1_keymapMatchesSpec) |
| 9.3 | Both parts keep effects, transforms and keyframes (keyframes follow the content). | OK (eo split tests) |
| 9.4 | Cutting exactly at a clip edge does nothing (no empty undo step). | OK (tc splitAtPlayheadAllOrSelected) |
| 9.5 | After cutting, the tool stays the blade until A or T is pressed. | OK |
| 9.6 | Join (Alt+\\) removes a through edit (same media, continuous source) at the selected edit point or between selected clips; linked parts join along. | Fixed (new `editops::joinClips`; pe2 spec9_6, er joinThroughEdit) |

## 10. Delete, ripple delete, gaps

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 10.1 | Backspace removes the selection and leaves a gap. | OK (tc deleteAndRippleDeleteWithLinks) |
| 10.2 | Delete (and Shift+Backspace) ripple-deletes the selected clips: the gap closes; one undo step. | OK (tc deleteAndRippleDeleteWithLinks, pe2 spec10_2) |
| 10.3 | Linked parts are removed together when linked selection is on; with it off only the selected part goes (sync may break, as in Resolve); locked tracks never move. | OK (tc, pe2 spec10_3) |
| 10.4 | Ripple delete closes the gap on the tracks of the deleted clips, and every other unlocked auto-select track follows for each removed range in which it is empty (keeps titles / music in sync); a track with content inside the range stays. | Fixed: only the tracks of the deleted clips moved (er rippleDeleteAllTracks*, pe2 spec10_2) |
| 10.5 | Clicking an empty range between clips selects that gap (highlighted); Backspace or Delete removes it; with nothing selected and no in/out range ripple delete does nothing. | Fixed: gaps could not be selected, and ripple delete without selection closed the gap under the playhead (still available: `EditBehaviour::rippleDeleteGapAtPlayhead`; tp spec10_5_gapSelectionAndDelete, pe2 spec10_5) |
| 10.6 | Removing a gap also shifts the other auto-select tracks by the same amount when they have room, keeping sync. | OK (eo closeGapAllTracksKeepsSync) |
| 10.7 | With no clip selected and in + out marks set, Backspace lifts the marked range on the auto-select tracks (clips are cut at the marks, a gap stays). | Fixed (new; pe2 spec10_7) |
| 10.8 | With no clip selected and in + out marks set, Delete extracts the range: removed on the auto-select tracks and closed up; the playhead goes to the in point and the marks are cleared. A clip selection wins over the marks. | Fixed (new; pe2 spec10_8) |
| 10.9 | Ctrl+Shift+X ripple cut: the selected clips (else the marked range) go to the clipboard and are ripple-deleted, in one undo step; Ctrl+Shift+V pastes them back in. | Fixed (new; pe2 spec10_9_*) |
| 10.10 | Edit > Delete Gaps removes every gap common to the auto-select tracks, keeping them in sync. | Fixed (new; pe2 spec10_10, er deleteGapsKeepsSync) |
| 10.11 | With a list or tree panel focused (the Effects library, the media pool, Edit Index, the bin list, the node library), Delete, Backspace, Ctrl+A, the arrow keys, Home / End and Page Up / Down act on that panel (arrows move its selection); the timeline's editing and navigation shortcuts act only while the timeline, a viewer or nothing in particular has the focus. Transport keys (Space, J / K / L) keep working from the panels. | Fixed (bughunt2: Delete with the Effects library focused ripple-deleted the clips selected on the timeline, the arrows stepped the playhead; one application filter, ListKeyGuard, claims these keys for every focused item view; test_bughuntkeys spec10_11_listPanelKeepsEditingKeys) |
| 10.12 | A focused combo box or spin box (Project Settings, Timeline Settings, the Deliver page, the Inspector's choice lists, Preferences) takes Up / Down and Page Up / Down to step its value (Alt+Down opens a combo box's list); the timeline's Up / Down (previous / next edit) act again once the focus is elsewhere. | Fixed (bughunt3: Up / Down jumped the playhead to the previous / next edit and the box never changed; ListKeyGuard also claims these keys for combo boxes and spin boxes; test_bughunt3 spec10_12_focusedComboAndSpinKeepArrowKeys) |
| 10.13 | Close Gap (context menu, empty track area) is offered only on a gap between clips, never after the last clip, and is greyed out when there is nothing to close (no gap, or the track is locked), so it never silently does nothing. | Fixed (new; er canCloseGapOnlyFalseWithoutGapOrOnLockedTrack, pe spec10_13_closeGapAvailability) |
| 10.14 | Delete, Backspace and Ripple Delete on a selected gap all remove it: later clips on that track and on the other unlocked auto-select tracks move left by the gap length (supersedes nothing; confirms 10.5 for both keys with a linked track). | Fixed (pe spec10_14_deleteKeysRemoveSelectedGapOnAllTracks) |
| 10.15 | Closing a gap (Close Gap, or Delete / Backspace / Ripple Delete on a selected gap) never fails because another track is in the way: other unlocked auto-select tracks with room left of the gap move with it (by the least room among them), a track with no room there (a clip across the gap) stays where it is, and the clicked track always closes. Supersedes 6.4 for this case; with nothing blocking it is the full all-track ripple as before. | Fixed (er closeGapBlockedTrackIsLeftAlone, closeGapAllTracksKeepsSync; pe spec10_15_blockedGapStillClosesOnItsOwnTrack) |
| 10.16 | Closing a gap never splits linked clips. When a moving clip is linked to a clip on a track that would stay, that track moves too if it has room; if it cannot (a clip across the gap, or locked), only the tracks whose moving clips have no stranded link are rippled, and when not even the clicked track qualifies nothing happens and the status line says "Close Gap would break sync with linked clips" (the item is greyed out in the context menu). Supersedes 10.15 for linked clips. | Fixed (er closeGapLinkedPartnerTrackMovesToo, closeGapLinkedPartnerBlockedKeepsClickedTrackStill; pe spec10_16_linkedPartnerTrackMovesWithTheGap, spec10_16_blockedLinkedPartnerStopsTheClose) |

## 11. Snapping

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 11.1 | N toggles snapping (toolbar button reflects it). | OK (tc toolsAndTracks) |
| 11.2 | Snap targets: clip starts/ends, playhead, in/out marks, markers, timeline start. | OK (eo snapCandidates) |
| 11.3 | The snap distance is in pixels (8 px), so it feels the same at every zoom. | OK |
| 11.4 | A snap line shows while snapped. | OK (painter) |
| 11.5 | Pressing N during a drag turns snapping off / on for the rest of the gesture. | OK (the drag reads the live state; shortcut works during a mouse grab) |
| 11.6 | A moved clip snaps with whichever of its edges is closer to a target. | OK (tw moveSnapsToEdges) |
| 11.7 | Dragged clips never snap to themselves. | OK (exclusion list) |
| 11.8 | Clip markers snap too, at their timeline frame (both ends of a ranged one), for every gesture that snaps to edit points (moves, trims, the playhead scrub, marker drags, the blade, drops); markers trimmed out of the clip's visible part and those of the dragged clips do not. | Fixed (owner request on 0.4.0; eo snappingToClipMarkers) |
| 11.9 | The playhead snaps onto keyframes, with the Snapping toggle (N, also during the drag, 11.5) and the 8 px distance of markers, through the same candidates: scrubbing the Keyframes panel's ruler (either view; before, that ruler snapped to nothing, now also to edges, marks and markers) and the timeline ruler. Which keys: the panel, the clips it shows; the timeline, the selected items (with their linked partner, as the panel shows them) and the clips with an open keyframe or curve lane, not every animated clip, whose keys would make the ruler sticky. Not the retime curve (15.8 marks) and not keys trimmed out of the clip. No modifier overrides snapping, as for markers. Clip moves, trims and the blade do not snap to keys. | Fixed (owner request; kp spec11_9_keySnapTargets, spec11_9_panelRulerSnapsToKeys; ke spec11_9_timelineScrubSnapsToKeys). Decision: Resolve's manual says clip edges, markers and the playhead snap to each other; nothing found says whether its playhead snaps to keys. |

| 12.13 | Clips of offline media (the file was moved, renamed or its drive unmounted) are drawn red with "Media Offline" instead of thumbnails or waveforms. The media pool keeps the offline set (checked on add, load, undo, relink and when the application becomes active; no file access while painting). | Fixed (polish round 2; p2 spec12_11_viewOptionsThumbnails, test_robustness offlineAndMovedMedia) |
| 12.14 | Timeline View Options > Show Source Timecode: media and nested clips show the source timecode of their first frame (the media's start timecode, or the Clip Attributes override, plus the source in) after the name in the name band, dimmed; the name keeps the room first. Off by default, stored in the settings. | Fixed (polish round 3; test_polish3 spec12_14). Decision: off by default, as Resolve shows only names on clips by default. |
| 12.15 | Double-clicking a track name in the header opens an inline editor with the name selected: Enter (or clicking away) renames the track in one undo step "Rename Track", Esc cancels. The header menu's Rename Track entry stays. | Fixed (bughunt2: only the menu entry existed; test_bughunt2 spec12_15_doubleClickRenamesTrack) |
| 12.16 | Clips on a locked track cannot be selected: a click on one, or on its edge, acts like a click on the empty track area (the selection clears; no edit point, no trim, no level-line drag), and a rubber band passes over them. | Fixed (bughunt6: locked clips were selected, their edges picked as edit points and shown in the Inspector, where edits still reached them; test_bughunt6edit spec12_16_lockedClipsAreNotSelectable) |
| 12.17 | A right-click on a clip on a locked track does not select it and shows the empty track area's menu (Paste, Select All, Add Track ...) instead of the clip menu; the selection stays. | Fixed (bughunt7: the clip was selected and its clip menu offered edits; test_bughunt7edit spec12_17_rightClickOnLockedClip) |

## 13. Transitions

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 13.1 | Standard transition: cross dissolve, 1 second. | OK (`EditBehaviour::transitionSeconds`, tp spec13_2) |
| 13.2 | Ctrl+T adds it at the selected edit point; with clips selected at both ends of each; otherwise at the edit nearest to the playhead on the auto-select video tracks. Existing transitions are kept. | Fixed: used only the nearest edge of the selection, ignored edit points (tp spec13_2_defaultTransitionPlacement) |
| 13.3 | Linked audio gets a matching cross fade. | OK (the mixer fades linked audio with the video transition) |
| 13.4 | At a cut the transition is centred on the cut. | OK (render TimelineEval) |
| 13.5 | Alignment can also be "ending at" or "starting at" the cut (clip context menu: Transition Alignment). | Fixed (`TransitionData::alignment`, saved; render, painter and audio fades follow; pe2 spec13_5) |
| 13.6 | At an edge without a neighbour it fades from / to black (transparent). | OK (render) |
| 13.7 | Without enough handles the user is asked: trim the clips (ripple trims at the cut until the media covers it), shorten the transition to the handles, or cancel. | Fixed (`HandleChoice`; pe2 spec13_7). Without a prompt (scripts, tests) the transition is added and missing frames repeat the edge frame. |
| 13.8 | Transition length editable in the inspector. | OK (in transitionTab) |
| 13.9 | Dragging a transition's outer edges in the timeline changes its length (centred: symmetrically), limited by the media handles; one undo step. | Fixed (drag mode TransitionEdge; pe2 spec13_9) |
| 13.10 | A transition is an item of its own: clicking its block selects it (outlined like a selected clip; clips, edit point and gap deselect), the Inspector shows its Transition tab, Delete / Backspace remove it (one undo step; not on a locked track), double-click opens it in the Inspector, the right-click menu offers Open in Inspector, Change Transition Duration, Transition Alignment and Delete Transition. Its outer edges keep the length drag of 13.9. | Fixed (alpha.2 feedback #6; `SelectionModel::transition()`; ts spec13_10_*, in spec13_10_selectedTransitionShowsTransitionTab) |
| 13.11 | The Effects library has an "Audio Transitions" category below Video Transitions (also in the Toolbox sidebar) with six items: Cross Fade 0 dB, Cross Fade +3 dB, Cross Fade -3 dB, Exponential, Logarithmic, S-Curve; each icon draws its two gain curves. | Fixed (0.4.1; `EffectCategory::AudioTransition`; test_effectslibrary spec13_11_audioTransitionsSection, sectionsAndSearch) |
| 13.12 | Audio transition gain curves (incoming rises 0 to 1, outgoing mirrors it): 0 dB linear (0.5 at the middle, an audible dip), +3 dB equal power sin / cos (0.707), -3 dB half linear half equal power (0.604), exponential (0.119), logarithmic (0.740), S-curve raised cosine (0.5). | Fixed (`core/AudioTransitions.h`; test_audiotransitions spec13_12_gainCurves, spec13_12_idsAndRegistry) |
| 13.13 | The mixer follows the audio transition type at cuts and at free ends (fade from / to silence). An audio clip without its own audio transition that follows a linked video clip's transition, or that holds a video transition id, gets the +3 dB equal-power crossfade. | Fixed (`AudioMixer::Fade::curve`; test_audiomixer spec13_13_crossfadeTypesBetweenTwoTones: two tones, 0 dB sits 3 dB below +3 dB at the cut) |
| 13.14 | Dropping an audio transition on an audio cut stores it on the incoming clip, centred, the standard drop length; on the free start or end of an audio clip it fades from / to silence. Audio transitions are refused on video clips and video transitions on audio clips (before 0.4.1 a video transition could be dropped on audio). | Fixed (`effectactions::transitionFitsTrack()`; test_timelinewidget spec13_14_dropAudioTransitionOnAudioCut) |
| 13.15 | Double-clicking an audio transition in the library adds it to the selected audio clip (the end nearer the playhead; with a linked video + audio pair selected, the audio clip). Ctrl+T and the Inspector's add buttons put Cross Fade +3 dB on audio clips (Cross Dissolve on video). The Inspector's Transition tab shows Type (audio transitions only, for audio clips), Alignment (Center / Start / End on Edit) and Duration. | Fixed (`effectactions::defaultTransitionId()`; test_effectslibrary spec13_15_audioTransitionActivation) |
| 13.16 | Audio transitions save and load with their type, length and alignment; older projects load unchanged (a video id such as cross_dissolve on an audio clip, or none, plays as +3 dB). No project format change. | Fixed (test_audiotransitions spec13_16_saveLoadRoundTrip) |
| 13.17 | Alt+drag a transition block onto a clip copies the transition to the clip edge nearer the pointer (at a cut: that cut); it replaces a transition already there, is shortened to fit the clips, and becomes the selected transition; one undo step "Copy Transition". | Fixed (owner request on 0.4.0; pe2 spec13_17_altDragCopiesATransition). Decision: the target is the clip under the pointer on a track of the source's type; no target outside clips or on a locked track. |
| 13.18 | Double-clicking a video or audio transition in the Effects library on the Edit page puts it on the selected edit point, or with nothing selected on the edit nearest the playhead on the auto-select tracks (the edges Ctrl+T uses, only tracks the type fits); an edit that already has a transition gets the new type and keeps its length and alignment (Ctrl+T still keeps it); one undo step. Selected clips keep 13.15 (the end nearer the playhead). | Fixed (bughunt6: without a selected clip nothing happened; test_bughunt6 spec13_18_libraryDoubleClickAddsTransitionAtEdit) |

| 14.2 | Stills are 5 s long. | OK (tc stillsAndGenerators; `EditBehaviour::stillSeconds`) |
| 14.3 | Title text and style are edited in the inspector, one undo step per typing burst. | OK (in titleClipTextIsOneUndoStepPerBurst) |
| 14.4 | Titles and generators can be trimmed to any length (no media limit). | OK (eo, sourceDurationUs = 0) |
| 14.5 | A new Text title (Effects > Titles > Text) reads "Basic Title", as in Resolve; titles of older projects keep their text. | Fixed (bughunt7: it read "Title"; test_bughunt7edit spec14_5_newTextTitleReadsBasicTitle) |
| 14.6 | Effects > Titles holds our own presets, as a Resolve user looks for them: Left Lower Third, Middle Lower Third, Right Lower Third (a name and a role low in the frame on a dark box), Scroll (rolling credits), Text and Text Box (text on a plain box). They are Title clips with the same parameters and only other defaults (our own simple design: system font, flat box); a preset clip is named after the preset, and the Inspector's Title tab shows one group named after it (text, font, size, colours, outline, background box, Position X / Y, Anchor, Scroll; reset goes back to the preset's defaults). Titles of different presets edit together when several are selected. | Fixed (bughunt10: the library had only Text; `EffectRegistry::registerTitles()`, core/TitlePresets.h, `ClipData::generator` of Title clips; test_bughunt10 spec14_6_titlePresetsInTheLibrary) |
| 14.7 | A title's Position X / Position Y place its text block (0 .. 1 of the frame from the left and from the bottom, 0.5 = centre, keyframable), and Anchor says which side of the block sits at Position X (a left lower third grows to the right). The default (0.5, 0.5, centre) is the centred block titles always had. GPU and CPU draw the same pixels. | Fixed (bughunt10: a title could only be moved with the Video tab's transform; `TitleStyle::posX / posY / anchor`; test_compositor spec14_7_titlePositionAndAnchor) |
| 14.8 | A title with Scroll on (the Scroll preset) rolls up through the frame over the clip's duration: the first frame has the text just below the bottom edge, the last frame just above the top, so a longer clip scrolls more slowly; Position Y is not used. GPU and CPU draw the same pixels. | Fixed (bughunt10: there was no scroll; `TitleStyle::scroll / scrollProgress`; test_compositor spec14_8_scrollRollsOverTheClip) |
| 14.9 | Selecting a title clip shows the Inspector's Title tab, where its text and look are, as Resolve shows a Text title's controls first; selecting a clip without a title afterwards goes back to the Video tab (as captions, 28.15). | Fixed (bughunt10: the Inspector stayed on the Video tab; `InspectorPanel::showClip()`; test_bughunt10 spec14_9_selectingATitleShowsTheTitleTab) |

## 15. Keyframes (inspector, keyframe editor, curve editor)

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 15.1 | A diamond per animatable parameter: dim (no keys), outlined (keys, none here), filled (key at the playhead). | OK (in keyframeButtonTogglesAtLocalFrame) |
| 15.2 | Clicking the diamond adds / removes a key at the playhead. | OK (in keyframeButtonTogglesAtLocalFrame) |
| 15.3 | Changing an animated value off a key adds a key at the playhead; on a key it updates it; a value without keys just changes. | OK (in scrubOnAnimatedParamSetsKey) |
| 15.4 | Arrows (and [ / ]) jump to the previous / next key. | OK (in, `clip.prev_keyframe/next_keyframe`) |
| 15.5 | The diamond is disabled when the playhead is outside the clip. | OK (in) |
| 15.6 | Keys interpolate linearly by default; the type (linear, ease in, ease out, ease in and out, smooth, static) can be changed per key from the right-click menu of the inspector diamond, the keyframe editor and the curve editor. | Fixed (in keyframeInterpolationMenu, tp spec15_6, ke) |
| 15.7 | Keys are clip-relative: they move with the clip, follow the source content on head trims and splits, and stay at their clip-relative frame on a slip. Keys trimmed out of the clip are kept (drawn faded beyond the clip edge in the editors) and come back when the clip is extended; the visible part still interpolates towards them. | Decision (ko spec15_7, eo) |
| 15.8 | Every animated clip shows a keyframe and a curve toggle at its bottom-right corner; each opens an editor lane below the clip (the track grows). One expanded clip per track; editor state is view state, not saved. Also `clip.toggle_keyframe_editor` / `clip.toggle_curve_editor` for the selected clip. Collapsed clips show their keys as small marks. | Fixed (ke spec15_8_*) |
| 15.9 | Keyframe editor: master row, a row per inspector group and per animated parameter. Click selects (Shift adds, Ctrl toggles, master / group diamonds take every key at that frame), rubber band, drag moves the selection in whole frames (snaps to playhead and clip edges with snapping on; clamped to the clip; a key landing on another replaces it), Delete / Backspace deletes, Ctrl+C / Ctrl+V copies and pastes at the playhead (while the lane has focus; also `clip.copy_keyframes` / `clip.paste_keyframes` / `clip.delete_keyframe`), double-click jumps the playhead, a row label selects the row. One undo step per gesture. | Fixed (ke spec15_9_*, ko) |
| 15.10 | Curve editor: value-over-time curves of the clip's animated parameters (toggle per parameter, each scaled to its own range, axis of the active one), keys drag in time and value (Shift locks the axis), double-click on empty space adds a key to the active curve, Fit resets the value zoom (Ctrl+Shift+wheel zooms it, middle drag pans). | Fixed (ke spec15_10_*) |
| 15.11 | Interpolation is exact everywhere: cubic Bezier segments from per-key in / out handles (linear, ease, smooth, free) plus static keys; compositor, playback, export and the audio gain envelope evaluate the same curve (audio between frames too). Projects saved with the version-1 per-segment model load converted. | Fixed (an spec15_11_*, am spec15_11_volumeCurvesExact, serialization spec15_11_loadsVersion1Keyframes) |
| 15.12 | Bezier handles of selected curved keys can be dragged; the opposite handle stays collinear (Alt breaks it); Flatten makes handles horizontal. | Fixed (ke spec15_12_curveHandleDrag, ko spec15_12_flattenAndHandles) |
| 15.13 | Each inspector section with animatable parameters (Transform, Cropping, Audio, Title) has a section diamond keying all of them at the playhead (filled when all have a key there). | Decision: Resolve's per-section keyframe controls are approximated by one diamond per section (in spec15_13_sectionKeyframeDiamond) |
| 15.14 | Keyframes panel (alpha.2 feedback #10, the cramped inline editor of alpha2-12.png): toggled from the interface bar (Keyframes, on the left after Edit Index, as in Resolve 20; spec 15.21) and View > Keyframes (`view.toggle_keyframes`, no default key: none is known for Resolve). Left a parameter tree grouped like the Inspector (Transform, Cropping, Title, Audio, each effect; animated parameters, Show All lists every animatable one), right a ruler, a dope sheet (master, item, group, parameter rows) and a large curve editor (all shown curves overlaid, colour per curve, value axis with labels, time and value grid), either or both (split). The header shows the item's name. | Fixed (kp spec15_14_showsAnimatedParams). Decision: Resolve's own keyframe window was not available to compare; the layout follows its Fusion / Color-page keyframe and spline editors. |
| 15.15 | The panel shows the selected items: a linked video + audio pair is one item (Transform / Cropping / effects of the video member, Audio of the audio member, spec 24.1); several items each get an item row (at most 8). | Fixed (kp spec15_15_linkedPairIsOneItem) |
| 15.16 | Tree rows: curve check box, previous / add-remove / next diamond with the Inspector's three states (disabled outside the clip), the value at the playhead editable inline (drag or type; keys at the playhead when animated, spec 15.3); a group diamond keys or unkeys all its parameters; a label click selects the row's keys. One undo step per gesture. | Fixed (kp spec15_16_*) |
| 15.17 | Dope sheet and curves edit exactly like the inline lanes (shared code): click / Shift / Ctrl / box select, drag in time (whole frames, snapping to playhead and clip edges) and value (Shift locks the axis), Bezier handles (Alt breaks), interpolation buttons Linear, Smooth, Ease In, Ease Out, Ease In and Out, Static, Flatten, right-click menu, copy / paste / delete (shared clipboard), double-click adds a key on the active curve. Normalised (each curve its own range) or one shared value axis; Fit All, Fit Selection; Ctrl+Shift+wheel zooms values, plain wheel pans them, middle drag pans both axes. | Fixed (kp spec15_17_*) |
| 15.18 | Time is the timeline's: one playhead (ruler scrub, double-click on a key, tree arrows; the panel follows the timeline), with the panel's own range: Fit Clip (default; refits on a new selection), Follow Timeline (same range as the timeline view), Free (Ctrl / Alt + wheel zooms at the pointer, Shift + wheel and the scroll bar scroll; page-scrolls when the playhead leaves the view). | 15.19 | Docks in the left column with the Media Pool, Effects and Edit Index (spec 15.21; before 0.3.4 a full-width row between the viewers and the timeline) or floats in its own window (title-bar button; e.g. a second monitor; shortcuts keep working there); closing the window hides the panel; dock state, window geometry, splitters, views and options persist with the page layout. | Fixed (kp spec15_19_detachAndRedockPersist) |
| 15.20 | A clip narrower than 160 px on screen shows "Open in Keyframes Panel" in its inline lane (the lane menus always have it); the inline curve editor's value labels are no longer cut at the lane edges. | Fixed (ke spec15_20_narrowClipOffersKeyframesPanel) |
| 15.21 | Keyframes panel placement (owner feedback on 0.3.2-alpha.1, "open on the left side"): its toggle sits in the left group of the interface bar (Resolve 20 puts the Keyframes button on the top-left toolbar), and docked it shares the left column above the timeline with the Media Pool, Effects and Edit Index panels. Normalised and Fit Selection show only with the curves. |---|---|---|

| 18.6 | Alt+M deletes the timeline marker at the playhead. | Fixed (new; pe2 spec18_6) |

Clicking a row (or moving through the rows with the keyboard) moves the playhead to the record in and selects the clip; selecting a clip in the timeline makes its row current. Search over every column. Follows every edit live; colours from the scheme. | Fixed (polish round 3; test_editindex). Decision: one row per clip (linked video and audio are separate events, as on the tracks); Notes is read-only here (clips have no note field). |
| 18.9 | Edit Index (Markers): every timeline and clip marker with colour, name, timecode, duration, notes and location (Timeline / clip name); a colour filter next to the search field; clicking a row moves the playhead to the marker (a clip marker also selects its clip); a marker's name or notes are edited in the table (double-click or F2), one undo step each ("Rename Marker", "Modify Marker"); clip markers on locked tracks are read-only. | Fixed (polish round 3; test_editindex). Decision (round 3): markers were single frames, so Duration showed one frame; since round 4 Duration shows the marker's length (18.11). |
| 18.10 | Edit Index column chooser: right-click on the table header lists the columns of the current mode as check boxes; unchecking hides the column ("#" always stays). The choice is kept per mode across sessions. | Fixed (polish round 4; p4 spec18_10_headerMenuTogglesColumns) |
| 18.11 | Ranged markers: a marker has a duration. Alt+drag a ruler marker (or drag the end of a ranged marker) to set where it ends, or type the Duration in the marker editor; one undo step "Change Marker Duration". The ruler draws a band in the marker's colour over its range, a clip marker a bar under the clip name; both ends are snap targets; the Edit Index shows the duration. Saved as an optional "duration" (project format 13; files of formats 1 - 12 load unchanged). | Fixed (polish round 4; p4 spec18_11_*). Decision: Shift+Up / Shift+Down stop at the start of a ranged marker only (like a single marker; the end is reached with snapping). Clip markers get their duration from the marker editor (no Alt+drag on the clip body, which moves clips). |
| 18.12 | File > Export > Timeline as EDL: CMX3600 of V1 ("V"), A1 ("A") and A2 ("A2"): one event per clip in record order, reels from the file names (8 characters; "AX" for titles, solids, generators and compound clips), source timecodes from the media's start timecode, a transition between touching clips as a CMX dissolve ("D nnn"), "M2" lines for constant speeds other than 100 % (reverse negative; ramps at their average speed with a comment), "* FROM CLIP NAME:" comments. | Fixed (polish round 4; test_edlexport). Decision: one video track per EDL (CMX3600 has one); the other tracks are not written. |
| 18.13 | File > Export > Timeline Markers to EDL (one event per marker, record out = its end, "* LOC: timecode COLOR name", "* COMMENT: notes") and to CSV (#, Name, Start, End, Duration, Color, Notes). Clip markers are not included. | Fixed (polish round 4; test_edlexport) |
| 18.14 | Dragging a ruler marker's flag moves it by whole frames (snapping), one undo step "Move Marker"; it never lands on another marker. A click on a flag without dragging moves the playhead to the marker. | Fixed (polish round 4 audit A9; p4 spec18_14_markersMoveButNeverOntoAnother) |
| 18.15 | Double-clicking a clip marker's diamond opens the marker editor; hovering a ruler or clip marker shows its name, timecode, duration and notes as a tool tip. | Fixed (polish round 4 audit A10 / A11; p4 spec18_15_clipMarkerDoubleClickOpensTheEditor) |
| 18.16 | M toggles a marker: on an existing marker it removes it instead of adding a second one (with a clip selected under the playhead the clip marker starting at the playhead, otherwise the timeline marker starting there; a ranged marker counts at its start frame); one undo step "Delete Marker". Ctrl+M still opens the editor, Alt+M is unchanged. | Fixed (owner request on 0.4.0, supersedes "M on an existing marker" in 18.5; pe2 spec18_16_mTogglesAMarker). The Source viewer has no markers, so nothing changes there. |
| 18.17 | The timeline tool bar's marker button has a drop-down (its arrow) of the sixteen marker colours, the current one checked: the colour picked there colours the timeline and clip markers added from then on (M, Ctrl+M, the button); kept in the settings (`timeline/markerColor`), Blue by default. | Fixed (bughunt6: new markers were always blue; test_bughunt6 spec18_17_markerButtonPicksTheColourOfNewMarkers) |
| 18.18 | A new marker (timeline or clip) is named "Marker N" with N one more than the highest "Marker N" in that list, so a name never repeats after a deletion (1, 2, 3, delete 2, add: "Marker 4"); renamed markers do not count. | Fixed (bughunt7: N was the count plus one, so names repeated; test_bughunt7edit spec18_18_markerNamesDoNotRepeat) |

Decision: the video clips of the selection move (if none, its audio clips); linked clips of the other kind keep their tracks. Key unsure (docs/keymap-resolve-windows.md). |

## 22. End-to-end flow (running app)

`tests/app/test_e2eflow.cpp` drives the real main window offscreen (program engine without a
sound device) through the widgets and actions a user touches:

1. import `h264_25fps.mp4` (10 s, 1280x720, AAC) into the media pool;
2. drop it from the pool onto V1 (linked V+A, 250 frames);
3. trim the tail by 50 frames with the selection tool (audio follows);
4. Ctrl+B at frame 100 (both tracks cut), drag the right half 25 frames later;
5. click the gap, Backspace: the gap closes on V1 and A1;
6. drop a Text title on V2 (125 frames), Ctrl+T near the cut (25-frame dissolve);
7. two opacity keys through the inspector diamond;
8. Space: the engine plays, the playhead advances, frames reach the timeline viewer;
9. Deliver page, YouTube 1080p preset, Add to Render Queue, Render All: the job finishes and
   the file probes as 1920x1080 with audio, 8.0 s long.

Result: passes. Integration bug found and fixed on the way: without a restored layout
(fresh start, `--screenshot` mode) the main window opened on the Media page and the Edit
page's widgets kept their unshown default sizes (`MainWindow` now selects Edit explicitly).

`tests/app/test_editkeys.cpp` (editfix branch, alpha.2 feedback "ripple delete / ripple cut
don't work") types the editing keys into the real main window with QTest after clicking a
clip: Backspace lifts, Delete / Shift+Backspace / Ctrl+Shift+X ripple, Ctrl+Shift+[ / ] and
Shift+[ / ] trim, also with the viewer focused, also as `{` / `}` (what a US layout reports
with Shift under Wayland); and it scans every action and QShortcut of the window for a key
bound twice (Qt's "ambiguous shortcut" runs neither). Findings: no duplicate bindings and the
keys reach the commands. The cause was that the owner's ripple keys Ctrl+Shift+[ / ] were not
mapped at all (Shift+[ / ] rippled instead). As a precaution `ShortcutFallback` maps a shifted
character that matched nothing (`Ctrl+Shift+{`) back to its unshifted shortcut, since that is
how some layouts report the key.
