| 23.13 | The menu bar reads like Resolve's on Windows: an application menu first, named after the application (About, About Qt, Keyboard Customization, Preferences, Quit), then File, Edit, Trim, Timeline, Clip, Mark, View, Playback, Audio, Workspace and Help. File > Import holds Media (Ctrl+I), Media Folder (our own) and Subtitle; Workspace (formerly "Settings") holds Full Screen Window and our Color Theme and Style; Help keeps Help for This (F1), Guide and Getting Started. | Fixed (bughunt8: Preferences, Keyboard Customization and Quit sat in Edit / File and About in Help, imports were top-level File entries and the look menu was called Settings; bh8 spec23_13_menuBarLikeResolve) |
| 23.14 | Keyboard Customization's profile list offers built-in presets of other editors after DaVinci Resolve (the default, listed first and marked "(default)"): Avid Media Composer, Final Cut Pro (Command becomes Ctrl, Option Alt, Control Meta), Kdenlive, Premiere Pro (Windows), Shotcut and Vegas Pro. Each maps the commands with a clear counterpart in that editor (playback, In / Out, split, ripple delete, insert / overwrite, trim, nudge, markers, zoom, undo / redo, tools, snapping, panels / pages); the others keep their Resolve key, or none where that editor uses the key otherwise. No preset binds one key twice in one scope (the dialog's conflict rules). Every key has a confidence (sure / likely / unsure) and every preset lists its gaps in docs/keymaps/. The Resolve keymap is unchanged. | Fixed (new, Reddit tester and owner request; generated by scripts/keymap-presets.py; test_keypresets everyPresetLoadsWithoutConflicts, presetsHaveTheirEditorsKeys) |
| 23.15 | With the Vegas Pro preset, S splits the clips at the playhead (the selected ones, else every clip there), as Vegas users cut; Ctrl+\\ no longer does. Shotcut's preset splits with S as well, Premiere's with Ctrl+K, Final Cut's with Ctrl+B, Kdenlive's with Shift+R. | Fixed (new; test_keypresets sSplitsWithVegasPro: a real S key press on the timeline) |
| 23.16 | Choosing a preset in Keyboard Customization activates it at once; the user's own changes on top of it are kept per preset (Reset returns to the preset's key); Cancel goes back to the profile and keys the dialog was opened with, OK keeps the preset. | Fixed (new; test_keypresets choosingAPresetAndCancelRestores) |
| 23.17 | Keyboard Customization > Import also reads a Premiere Pro keyboard file (.kys): a new user profile based on the Premiere Pro preset, where the commands the file names and Kinoklip recognises get the file's keys (a key they take leaves the preset's other command of that scope); unknown commands, unreadable keys and repeats are skipped, and the status line says "Imported 7 shortcuts from the Premiere Pro file; skipped 3 ...". A file that is not XML is refused with a message; nothing in it fails the import. Cancel removes the imported profile. | Fixed (new; test_keypresets premiereKysImport, commandNames; src/ui/common/ForeignKeymaps.cpp). Decision: the parser reads the file's general shape (an entry with a command name, a Windows virtual key or key name, modifiers) rather than a fixed schema, so differences between Premiere versions degrade to skipped entries. Unverified against files saved by Premiere itself. |
| 23.18 | Import also reads a Final Cut Pro command set (.commandset, an XML property list): based on the Final Cut Pro preset, Command becomes Ctrl, Option Alt, Control Meta; Mac "delete" is Backspace (ripple delete). A binary property list is refused with the hint to convert it (`plutil -convert xml1`). | Fixed (new; test_keypresets finalCutCommandSetImport). Unverified against files saved by Final Cut Pro itself. |
| 23.19 | The View menu is grouped into submenus instead of one long list (owner request: "this menu is a bit too big"): Switch to Page (the seven pages, Shift+2 .. Shift+8), Show Panel in Workspace (the panel toggles, Show Bin List, Video Scopes), Viewer (Single Viewer Mode, Toggle Source / Timeline Viewer, Multicam Viewer, Full-Screen Viewer, Zoom to Fit, the On-Screen Controls) and Viewer Overlays (Show Rulers, Guides, Safe Area, Show Grid, Grid Type). Every command keeps its id, key and check state, so keyboard profiles and Keyboard Customization (one section per submenu) still work; a panel toggle disabled on the current page stays in its submenu, disabled. | Decision (poolbins: the first two submenu names are Resolve's, from its Workspace menu; the commands stay in our View menu, which is where they were; test_viewmenu submenusHoldTheCommands, shortcutsAndStatesStay) |
| 23.20 | The section lists of Project Settings and Preferences (not resizable) are at least as wide as their widest section name ("Optimized Media and Render Cache"), measured with the actual font and style padding and with room for a vertical scroll bar; 200 px stays the minimum. No name is cut off and there is never a horizontal scroll bar under the list, also with a 1.5x larger application font, a translation or a font change while the dialog is open. | Fixed (textfit branch, owner report: the name was cut off and a horizontal scroll bar appeared; `textfit::fitListWidthToItems()`; tests/panels/test_dialogs.cpp spec23_20_sectionListsFitTheirNames) |
| 23.21 | The Color page's window list (width capped at 300 px) never scrolls sideways: a window name too long for it ends in "..." and shows in full as its tooltip. | Fixed (textfit branch; `textfit::elideListItems()`; tests/panels/test_textfit.cpp spec23_21_cappedListElidesWithFullTextTooltip, tests/app/test_colorpageui.cpp windowHandles) |
| 23.22 | Mixer strips keep their fixed width (as in Resolve); a long track or bus name in the input label, the name plate and the bus button ends in "..." and the tooltip starts with the full name. | Fixed (textfit branch: long names were clipped at both edges; `textfit::setElidedText()`; tests/panels/test_textfit.cpp spec23_22_fixedWidthLabelsAndButtonsElide, tests/app/test_audiopage.cpp spec23_22_longTrackNamesElideInTheStrip) |
| 23.23 | Fixed-width value fields (Keyframes editor, Color page qualifier and window fields) are at least as wide as their widest value with its unit, the range's negative end included; a Deliver preset tile elides each of its two name lines (also a single word longer than the tile), with the full name as the tooltip. | Fixed (textfit branch: values and long words were clipped; `ValueField::sizeHint()`; tests/panels/test_textfit.cpp spec23_23_valueFieldsFitTheirWidestValue, spec23_23_presetTileNamesElidePerLine, tests/app/test_keyframespanel.cpp spec15_16_treeEditsRoundTripThroughUndo, tests/app/test_colorpageui.cpp windowHandles) |

## 24. Inspector and viewers (editfix branch, alpha.2 feedback)

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 24.1 | A linked video + audio pair is one Inspector item: clicking either part shows the clip by name (no "2 clips selected"), the Video tab edits the video member, the Audio tab the audio member. Several unrelated items: the header says how many (link groups count once) and the last-clicked one is shown. | Fixed (owner report alpha2-13.png; in spec24_1_linkedPairIsOneItem). Decision (polish round 2): with several clips of the same kind selected (same clip kind and generator, same track type, not on a locked track), an Inspector change of the shown clip is copied onto all of them in the same undo step (typed values and drags alike): the whole parameter (value and keys, or the static) of the transform, audio, title and solid colour sets, and of effects matched by type and position among that type (the second blur matches the second blur). Fields whose values differ show "Mixed" (check boxes partly checked, combos empty) until the next edit. Adding, removing or reordering effects and masks, masks and transitions stay per clip. The header says "3 clips selected, edits apply to all" (or to how many of the kind). (in spec24_1_multiClipEditsApplyToAll, spec24_1_multiClipEffectParams) |
| 24.2 | The Inspector switches to the Audio tab when the audio part of a clip is clicked and to the Video tab for the video part, unless another tab (Effects, Title, Transition) is open. | Fixed (in spec24_2_tabFollowsClickedPart) |
| 24.3 | Clicking a clip's "fx" badge selects the clip and opens its Effects tab in the Inspector (showing the Inspector if hidden). | Fixed (ts spec24_3_fxBadgeOpensEffects) |
| 24.4 | Single / dual viewer: a button at the right end of both viewer headers (icon of the layout it switches to, tooltip with the shortcut if one is assigned) and View > Single Viewer Mode (checkable, kept in step) switch modes; one viewer gets the width of both. | Fixed (alpha.2 feedback #5; test_mainwindow spec24_4_singleViewerToggle). Resolve has no default key for it; none is assigned. |
| 24.5 | In single mode the viewer shows the timeline; opening a clip (media pool double-click, match frame) shows the source viewer in its place instead of leaving single mode; Q swaps back and forth; activating the timeline viewer (e.g. working in the timeline) shows the timeline. | Fixed (test_mainwindow spec24_4) |
| 24.6 | The viewer mode is saved with the page layout. | OK (test_mainwindow spec24_4) |
| 24.7 | Right-click on a viewer: Play / Pause, Play In to Out, Loop, Mark In / Out, Clear In and Out, Go to In / Out, Add Marker and Match Frame (timeline viewer), Single Viewer Mode, Source / Timeline, Full Screen Viewer and the Playback Resolution menu; the viewer becomes the active one first. | Fixed (polish round 2; vp mediaOfflineNotice) |
| 24.8 | A viewer showing offline media (the source clip, or the top visible clip at the timeline viewer's playhead) shows "Media Offline" with the clip name over the picture. | Fixed (polish round 2; vp mediaOfflineNotice) |
| 24.9 | On-screen Transform controls (the viewer's on-screen controls menu: Off / Transform / Mask, one at a time; View > On-Screen Transform Controls): the current clip's frame outline and centre over the picture; dragging in the viewer moves the clip (Position X / Y in timeline pixels, Shift for one axis; an animated position gets a key at the playhead); one undo step "Move Clip"; Esc cancels the drag. Titles are placed this way. | Fixed (polish round 2; vp onScreenTransformMovesTheClip). Scale, rotation, anchor point and crop handles followed in polish round 3 (24.11, 24.12). No default key (none known for Resolve). |
| 24.10 | Keyboard focus: shortcuts are window-scoped, so every window that can have focus carries the commands: the main window, the full screen viewer, the floating Keyframes window, the floating Audio page viewer and the EQ / Dynamics windows (Space plays the active viewer from any of them). | Fixed (polish round 2: the floating Audio viewer and the EQ / Dynamics windows had no shortcuts; ap pageActionsOnlyOnTheAudioPage) |
| 24.11 | On-screen Transform controls, complete: corner and edge handles scale (Zoom X / Y) around the anchor point, a knob above the top edge rotates around it, the anchor cross moves the anchor point while the picture stays where it is (Position compensates), dragging anywhere else moves the clip. Every drag is one named undo step ("Scale Clip", "Rotate Clip", "Move Anchor Point", "Move Clip") and writes the Inspector parameters; an animated parameter gets a key at the playhead, as an Inspector edit; Esc cancels. | Fixed (polish round 3; test_transformcontrols). Decision: corners keep the aspect while Zoom X / Y are linked (the Inspector default) and edges then scale both axes; Shift toggles the link for the drag (free corner / one-axis edge; a free scale of a linked zoom unlinks it), as Resolve's linked zoom behaves; Shift snaps a rotation to 15 degree steps and keeps a move on one axis. |
| 24.12 | On-screen Crop controls (the viewer's on-screen controls menu: Off / Transform / Crop / Mask, one at a time; View > On-Screen Crop Controls): the uncropped outline dashed, the crop rectangle with edge and corner handles setting Crop Left / Right / Top / Bottom in source pixels (corners two edges); an edge stops one pixel before the opposite one; one undo step "Crop Clip" per drag, keys at the playhead when animated. | Fixed (polish round 3; test_transformcontrols). No default key (none known for Resolve). |
| 24.13 | Default Edit page layout: the media pool / effects / Edit Index column takes about 24 % of the width (460 of 1920 px) and the Inspector column 25 % (480 px); the viewers share the rest. The proportions are re-applied whenever the window is resized until a column splitter is dragged, so a window that starts small and is then maximised keeps wide side columns. Layouts stored by 0.3.2 and older are reset to these widths once (stored layout version 2). | Fixed (owner feedback on 0.3.2-alpha.1; test_mainwindow sideColumnsKeepDefaultShare) |
| 24.14 | Live window resizing: the window content follows the frame while its edge or corner is dragged. Each size costs one layout pass of the Edit page (the default column widths are applied before the top row lays out), hidden pages do no work, the viewers draw their last frame scaled and re-render once the size has settled, and on Wayland buffer swaps do not wait for the compositor (swap interval 0), so a frame composed for the previous size is replaced at once. QT_LOGGING_RULES=kk.ui.resize.debug=true logs each step's time. | Improved, to be confirmed on a Plasma session with a GPU (owner screencast on 0.3.4; test_resizebench sweep, docs/resize-performance.md) |
| 24.15 | A command that selects a linked video + audio pair or many clips (an insert / overwrite / other source edit, a drop from the media pool, Paste, Paste Insert, Select All, Y / Ctrl+Y) leaves the Inspector on the Video tab: a video clip of the new selection is the current one. Only clicking an audio part shows the Audio tab (24.2). | Fixed (bughunt: every source edit and drop switched the Inspector to the Audio tab, because the audio clip came last; test_bughunt spec24_15_editResultKeepsVideoTab) |
| 24.16 | Transform Position Y and Anchor Y are shown positive up, as in Resolve: a positive Position Y moves the picture up. The Inspector, the Keyframes panel's value fields and both curve editors (axis labels included) show, take and draw the negated stored value; project files, rendering, the on-screen controls and Copy / Paste Attributes keep the stored +down pixels, so saved projects show the same picture (no format change). The Compositing page's Transform node Center already counts up. | Fixed (bughunt2: negative moved the picture up; ParamDescriptor::displayNegated; test_bughunt spec24_16_positionYIsPositiveUp). Mask Position Y in the Inspector's Masks still counts down (unchanged). |
| 24.17 | Double-clicking a media clip on the Edit page timeline opens its source in the source viewer, parked on the clicked frame with the part the clip uses marked (Match Frame at that frame; in single viewer mode the source viewer is shown). Compound clips still open in a tab (26.14), transitions in the Inspector (13.10); the Cut and Audio pages' timelines do not do it. | Fixed (bughunt2: a double-click acted as a second click; test_bughunt2 spec24_17_doubleClickOpensTheSource) |
| 24.18 | A focused Inspector value (after a click or a scrub) takes Up / Down (one step; Shift finer, Ctrl coarser; one undo step each) and Enter (type a value), as its tool tip says; the timeline's Up / Down (previous / next edit) act again once the focus is elsewhere. | Fixed (bughunt2: Up / Down jumped the playhead to the previous / next edit and the field never saw them; test_bughunt2 spec24_18_focusedValueFieldKeepsArrowKeys) |
| 24.19 | The Video tab has a Composite group after Cropping, as in Resolve: Composite Mode (Normal, Add, Subtract, Difference, Multiply, Screen, Overlay, Hard Light, Soft Light, Darken, Lighten, Color Dodge, Color Burn, Exclusion, Linear Burn, Linear Light, Vivid Light, Pin Light, Hard Mix) and Opacity (moved from Transform, same stored key, so projects load the same). The mode blends the clip with the tracks below with the Compositing page's Merge maths (which gains the same modes), with the same pixels on the GPU and the CPU fallback; over nothing the clip shows as is; in a transition the dominant clip's mode applies; adjustment clips composite normally. Paste Attributes > Transform, Cropping and Composite copies it. | Fixed (bughunt3: there was no composite mode; project format 17; test_bughunt3 spec24_19_inspectorCompositeGroup, test_compositemode spec24_19_compositeModesMatchMergeOnGpuAndCpu, spec24_19_absentModeIsNormal, spec24_19_modeOverNothingShowsTheClip) |
| 24.20 | Dynamic Zoom (Video tab, between Cropping and Composite, as in Resolve): an on/off switch in the section header, Dynamic Zoom Ease (Linear, Ease In, Ease Out, Ease In and Out), Swap (exchanges the start and end rectangles) and Reset. The clip moves from the start framing to the end framing over its length; each framing is a rectangle of the picture with the frame's aspect that fills the frame, applied before the Transform. The default is a slow push-in (the whole frame to a centred rectangle 1/1.2 of it). The viewer's on-screen controls menu has Dynamic Zoom after Crop: while it is chosen the viewer shows the whole picture (no Dynamic Zoom) with the start rectangle in green and the end rectangle in red; dragging inside one moves it, dragging a corner resizes it about the opposite corner; one undo step per drag, Esc cancels. GPU and CPU render the same pixels. Stored as transform statics (no format change). | Fixed (bughunt7; test_dynamiczoom, test_dynamiczoomcontrols spec24_20_onScreenDynamicZoomControls, test_bughunt7 spec24_20_inspectorDynamicZoom) |
| 24.21 | Speed Change and Retime and Scaling (Video tab of media and nested clips, after Composite, as in Resolve): Direction (Forward / Reverse), Freeze Frame, Speed (%), Frames per second and Duration (linked, as in the Change Clip Speed dialog, whose edit they run: one constant speed over the clip's material), Ripple Sequence (on by default; off keeps the clip length) and Pitch Correction; Retime Process (Project Settings / Nearest / Frame Blend / Optical Flow). Each change is one undo step and applies to the selected clips too. Motion Estimation is not offered (there is one optical flow method). | Fixed (bughunt7, the Inspector part of 29.16; test_bughunt7 spec24_21_inspectorSpeedChange) |
| 24.22 | Save (Ctrl+S) or Save As right after typing a title's or caption's text, before the typing pause closes its undo step, closes that step first: the text is saved and the project stays clean (no "•", no "save changes?" on closing). | Fixed (bughunt9 workflow: the step closed after the save and the project was modified again; `Project::aboutToSave`, `ParamRow::finishPendingEdit()`; bh9 spec24_22_saveRightAfterTypingIsClean) |
| 24.23 | Single viewer mode keeps the side columns of dual mode: the media pool / effects column and the Inspector have the widths they have with two viewers (by default about 24 % and 25 % of the width, so all five Inspector tabs fit) and the one viewer gets the middle, also when Kinoklip starts in single viewer mode. In windows too narrow for two viewers at their minimum width and the default columns (below about 1920 px) the columns give way by the same amount in both modes. Column widths nobody dragged stay proportional after a restart. Layouts stored by 1.1.4 and older (version 3) may hold the squeezed columns: their column widths are reset to the defaults once (stored layout version 4); their row heights are kept. | Fixed (owner report on 1.1.4: after a start in single mode the left column had about 320 px and the Inspector 190 px with its tabs behind scroll arrows, because the stored mode was applied before the first layout, when QSplitter::sizes() still reports the children's initial widths; test_mainwindow spec24_23_singleViewerKeepsSideColumns at 1869 x 1080 and 1920 x 1080, spec24_23_oldLayoutResetOnce) |
| 24.24 | Every panel size is remembered: the splitters of every page (side columns, viewers / timeline, Mixer, Media Pool / Effects / Edit Index / Keyframes in the left column, Inspector / Metadata) and inside panels (the Media Pool's bin list and clip area, the Effects library's sidebar and list, the Media page's browser tree and file list) are stored a second after a drag and on quitting, and come back on the next start. A page switch, single / dual viewer and hiding and showing panels keep them; dual mode gets back the split of the two viewers it had before single mode (stored too). | Fixed (owner request on 1.1.4: "make it remember the size of all panels"; test_mainwindow spec24_24_panelSizesPersist, spec24_24_everyPageKeepsItsSplitters) |
| 24.25 | Workspace > Reset UI Layout (as in Resolve) returns every page to its default panels and sizes: on the Edit page dual viewers, the Media Pool and the Inspector shown, Keyframes docked and hidden, the default column and row proportions for the current size, which follow window resizes again until a splitter is dragged. No default key. | Fixed (new; test_mainwindow spec24_25_resetUiLayout) |
| 24.26 | The default side columns keep a fixed 1065 px for the two viewers next to them (their real minimum with the Breeze style, about 1044 px, plus the splitter handles), so the columns match in both viewer modes; narrower windows take the difference from the columns. | Fixed (test_mainwindow spec24_23_singleViewerKeepsSideColumns; failed with the real Breeze style in the release container) |

## 25. Timelines and timeline tabs (compound branch, v2)

Tests: `cp` = `tests/core/test_compound.cpp`, `tt` = `tests/timeline/test_timelinetabs.cpp`,
`tn` = `tests/render/test_nested.cpp`.

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 25.1 | A project holds any number of timelines. Each one is an item in the media pool (its own icon), lives in a bin like media, and can be renamed there (renaming the item renames the timeline). | Fixed (new; cp createSwitchRenameDeleteTimelines, renameInMediaPoolRenamesTimeline) |
| 25.2 | File > New Timeline (Ctrl+N) asks for the name ("Timeline N" suggested), creates an empty timeline (V1, V2, A1, A2) in the media pool's current bin and opens it. | Fixed (new; tt tabsFollowProject). Decision: Ctrl+N was New Project before; Resolve uses it for New Timeline, New Project now has no key. |
| 25.3 | Duplicate Timeline copies all clips ("&lt;name&gt; copy"); Delete Timeline asks first (and says how many nested clips go offline), is one undo step, and refuses to delete the last timeline. | Fixed (new; cp createSwitchRenameDeleteTimelines) |
| 25.4 | Each timeline has its own playhead, in/out marks, markers, zoom and scroll; switching back restores them. | Fixed (new; tt zoomAndPlayheadPerTimeline) |
| 25.5 | Each timeline can follow the project resolution and frame rate (default) or have its own (Timeline > Timeline Settings…). Changing the project settings updates only the timelines that follow them. | Fixed (new; cp projectSettingsFollowedOnlyByFollowingTimelines). As before, a new frame rate does not rescale existing clips. |
| 25.6 | Timeline tabs are on by default: the open timelines are tabs above the timeline; double-clicking a timeline in the media pool opens (or shows) its tab; clicking a tab switches; tabs can be dragged to reorder and closed (button, context menu, Timeline > Close Timeline Tab); the last tab cannot be closed. The tab context menu also offers Close Other Tabs, Rename, Duplicate and Timeline Settings. | Fixed (new; tt tabsFollowProject, cp tabsOpenCloseMove) |
| 25.7 | The timeline viewer, the Inspector, the Keyframes panel, the Deliver page and playback always show the active tab's timeline; switching clears the clip selection. | Fixed (new: they share the one active Timeline object) |
| 25.8 | Preferences > Editing > Timelines turns tabs off: one timeline at a time, picked from a drop-down in the same place (compound clips are listed while open). | Fixed (new; tt dropDownWhenTabsAreOff). Decision: Resolve puts its timeline drop-down in the timeline viewer's header; ours sits above the timeline, where the tabs are, so the viewer chrome stays untouched. |
| 25.9 | The open tabs, their order, the active timeline and each timeline's playhead and zoom are saved with the project. | Fixed (new; cp saveLoadRoundTrip) |
| 25.10 | Undo covers every timeline: undoing a step that was made in another timeline first switches to that timeline. | Decision (Resolve keeps one undo history per timeline; ours is per project so cross-timeline steps like New Compound Clip stay one step; cp editsInOtherTimelineUndoWithSwitch) |
| 25.11 | Timeline > Next / Previous Timeline Tab cycle through the tabs. | Fixed (new; tt tabsFollowProject). No default keys (Resolve has none known). |
| 25.12 | New projects run at 24 fps and their timelines start at 01:00:00:00 (Resolve's defaults); Timeline Settings > Starting timecode (or the media pool's Timelines > Starting Timecode… on a timeline) changes a timeline's start (one undo step) and Project Settings > General Options > Timeline starting timecode the start of new timelines. The ruler, the timeline and source viewers (the source viewer counts from the media's own start timecode), Edit Index, marker tool tips, drag labels, the trim view, the Keyframes ruler, typed timecodes (a value below the start counts from it: "200" goes to 01:00:02:00), EDL and marker exports, timeline timecode burn-ins and the timecode track of renders count from it; subtitle files stay relative to the render. Projects saved before format 17 keep 00:00:00:00, also for timelines created later; a frame rate change keeps the HH:MM:SS:FF. | Fixed (bughunt3: every timeline counted from 00:00:00:00 and new projects ran at 25 fps; TimelineData::startTimecode, project format 17; test_bughunt3 spec25_12_newProjectsStartAtOneHourAt24Fps, spec25_12_olderProjectsKeepZeroStart, spec25_12_startingTimecodeSettingAndRateChange, spec25_12_displaysAndExportsUseTheStartingTimecode, spec25_12_mediaPoolTimelineMenuOffersStartingTimecode) |
| 25.13 | The frame rate of a timeline is fixed once it has clips (Project Settings > Timeline frame rate while a timeline following the project has clips, Timeline Settings > Timeline frame rate of a timeline with clips): the box is disabled with a tool tip saying why, as in Resolve. | Fixed (bughunt3: the rate could be changed under existing clips, which kept their frame positions, so the timeline played faster or slower and lost sync with its media; test_bughunt3 spec25_13_frameRateLockedOnceTimelineHasClips) |
| 25.14 | New timelines start with one video and one audio track, V1 and A1, as in Resolve (the first timeline of a new project, File > New Timeline, the media pool's New Timeline); dropping or editing above the top video track or below the last audio track adds tracks as before. Projects saved with more tracks keep them. Supersedes the track list in 25.2. | Fixed (bughunt4: new timelines had V1, V2, A1 and A2; `makeDefaultTimeline()`; test_bughunt4 spec25_14_newTimelinesStartWithOneVideoAndOneAudioTrack, test_timelinedrop emptySpaceAboveCreatesVideoTrack, emptySpaceBelowCreatesAudioTrack) |
| 25.15 | Timeline Settings of a timeline with clips that has its own frame rate does not offer "Use project settings" (disabled, with a tool tip) when the project runs at another rate, since following the project would change the rate (25.13). | Fixed (bughunt4: the box could be ticked and was then silently ignored; test_bughunt4 spec25_15_useProjectSettingsLockedWithClipsAtAnotherRate) |
| 25.16 | File > New Timeline (Ctrl+N, also the media pool's New Timeline) opens a Create New Timeline dialog as in Resolve: Start Timecode (the project's timeline start, 01:00:00:00 for new projects), Timeline Name ("Timeline N"), No. of Video Tracks and No. of Audio Tracks (1 and 1); Create makes that timeline in one undo step. Resolve's Audio Track Type, Empty Timeline and the format tab are not offered. | Fixed (bughunt4: only the name was asked; test_bughunt4 spec25_16_createNewTimelineDialog) |
| 25.17 | Timeline Settings (and Project Settings) offer Resolve's resolution presets: 1280 x 720 HD, 1920 x 1080 HD, 2560 x 1440 QHD, 3840 x 2160 Ultra HD, 4096 x 2160 4K DCI, 1080 x 1920 Vertical HD, 2160 x 3840 Vertical Ultra HD, 1080 x 1080 Square and Custom; a preset sets and locks the sizes, Custom unlocks them; a size that is no preset shows Custom. | Fixed (bughunt5: Timeline Settings had only the two size fields; `ProjectSettingsDialog::bindResolutionPresets()`; bh5 spec25_17_timelineSettingsResolutionPresets) |
| 25.18 | The project's first import (the media pool had no media files) whose first video runs at another frame rate than the project asks, as in Resolve, "Change the project frame rate to 25 fps to match them?" with Change (the default) and Don't Change; Change sets the project frame rate, and the timelines that follow it, in one undo step. Not asked for stills or audio, for later imports, for rates the project cannot use, or once a timeline that follows the project has clips (25.13). File > Import, the media pool, folder imports and drops all ask. | Fixed (bughunt9 workflow: 25 fps footage went into the 24 fps default timeline without a word, and the render was converted to 24 fps; `offerProjectFrameRate()` in ui/common/MediaImport; bh9 spec25_18_firstImportOffersTheClipFrameRate) |

## 26. Compound clips and nested timelines (compound branch, v2)

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 26.1 | Clip > New Compound Clip… (also in the clip context menu) asks for the name ("Compound Clip N") and collapses the selected clips (with their linked clips when linked selection is on) into one clip on the lowest selected track at the selection's first frame, as long as the selection. Linked audio becomes a linked audio part of the compound clip on the lowest selected audio track. One undo step; the new clip is selected. | Fixed (new; cp collapseKeepsTimingTracksAndContent, compoundClipInProject; tt compoundCommands) |
| 26.2 | Inside, the clips keep their relative timing and track order (lowest selected track = V1 / A1), effects, transitions, keyframes, markers and links. | Fixed (cp collapseKeepsTimingTracksAndContent) |
| 26.3 | If an unselected clip still sits in the selection's range on the lowest track, the compound clip goes to the next free track above (a new top track if needed); it never overwrites. Locked tracks in the selection refuse the command. | Decision (cp collapseAvoidsUnselectedClipInRange, collapseRefusesEmptyAndLocked) |
| 26.4 | The compound clip appears in the media pool (compound clip icon) and can be used again: dragged or edited (F9/F10/...) into any timeline, several times. | Fixed (tt timelineItemDropsAsNestedClipWithoutRecursion) |
| 26.5 | Open in Timeline (context menu, Clip menu, double-click in the media pool) opens the compound clip's timeline in its own tab; edits there change every instance (picture, sound, and the length the instances can be trimmed to). | Fixed (cp compoundClipInProject; tt compoundCommands) |
| 26.6 | Decompose in Place replaces the compound clip (and its linked part) with the clips it shows: only the visible part (cut at a trimmed edge), at the same timeline frames, nested track k on the clip's track + k or the next free track above. The compound clip's own transform and effects are dropped. Refused for speeds other than 100 % and a different frame rate. | Fixed (cp decomposeRestoresClipsExactly, decomposeTrimmedAndMovedInstance, decomposeRefusesSpeedAndForeignClip) |
| 26.7 | A compound clip trims, slips, splits, moves and changes speed like a media clip; its "media" is the nested timeline (it cannot be extended past it). | Fixed (cp decomposeTrimmedAndMovedInstance) |
| 26.8 | Inspector properties of a compound clip (transform, crop, opacity, effects, volume, pan) apply to the nested picture / mix as a whole, not to each clip inside. | Fixed (tn instanceTransformAppliesToWhole) |
| 26.9 | Nested timelines: dragging a timeline from the media pool into another timeline uses it as a clip (same machinery as compound clips). | Fixed (tt timelineItemDropsAsNestedClipWithoutRecursion) |
| 26.10 | A timeline can never contain itself, directly or through other nested timelines: such drops and edits are refused. | Fixed (cp recursionIsDetected; tt timelineItemDropsAsNestedClipWithoutRecursion) |
| 26.11 | Rendering, playback and export of nested content match the flattened clips (picture within 2/255 per channel, sound sample-exact), frame-accurately through several nesting levels. | Fixed (tn compoundMatchesFlattened, mediaThroughTwoLevels, audioMatchesFlattened) |
| 26.12 | Nested clips show thumbnails and waveforms of their content on the timeline (rendered in the background, cached until the nested timeline changes) and have their own clip colour. | Fixed (new) |
| 26.13 | Deleting a compound clip / timeline from the media pool asks first when clips use it; those clips become offline (draw and play nothing). | Fixed |
| 26.14 | Double-clicking the body of a compound clip / nested timeline on the timeline opens it in a tab (like Open in Timeline) with its playhead at the moment under the pointer (the inner frame the clip shows there, speed changes included). An offline compound clip does nothing; a double-click elsewhere behaves as before. | Fixed (owner request after 0.2.0-alpha.1; test_timelinetabs doubleClickOpensCompoundAtHoveredFrame) |

## 27. Masks (masks branch, roadmap v2)

Shape masks on the Edit page, from general knowledge of how NLE masks behave (Resolve's
Edit-page approach: shapes on a clip, shaped on-screen in the timeline viewer, keyframable).
Clean room: our own wording, icons and handle design.

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 27.1 | A clip has an ordered list of masks: rectangle, ellipse, polygon, bezier (pen: corner and smooth points) and freehand (a drawn stroke, simplified to a smooth bezier path). Each has enable, mode, invert, opacity, feather inside / outside, expand / contract and a transform (position, width / height for rectangles and ellipses, scale X / Y, rotation). | Fixed (test_masks; test_masksui inspector*) |
| 27.2 | Masks cut the clip's own picture: after its effects, before its transform, opacity and compositing, so they move, scale and rotate with the clip and the clip opacity applies on top. | Fixed (test_maskrender masksFollowTheClipTransform) |
| 27.3 | Modes combine with the masks above in the list: Add = union, Subtract = cut out, Intersect = keep the overlap, Difference = overlap removed. The matte starts empty for a first Add / Difference mask and full for a first Subtract / Intersect mask (one Subtract mask cuts a hole). Invert flips one mask's coverage before its mode, opacity scales it. | Fixed (test_maskrender modes, invertAndOpacity) |
| 27.4 | Feather softens the edge from feather-inside pixels within the shape to feather-outside pixels beyond it (timeline pixels, so the look does not change with preview quality); expand moves the edge out (negative: in). Hard edges are antialiased. | Fixed (test_maskrender featherIsMonotonic, expandContract, ellipseAdd) |
| 27.5 | Every numeric mask parameter and the shape (all points as one property) are keyframed with the normal keyframe system: diamonds in the Inspector (per row, per mask section, shape row), the Keyframes panel and the inline timeline lanes list them in a "Masks" group ("<mask>: <parameter>"), keys move / delete / copy / ease like other keys; shape keys interpolate every point and tangent. Rendering evaluates the exact frame. | Fixed (test_masks keyframeOps*, copyPasteShapeKeys; test_maskrender animatedMaskAtExactFrames; test_masksui inspectorEditsMasks) |
| 27.6 | Inspector, Video tab: a Masks section after Cropping with one add button per shape; one group per mask with enable, edit-in-viewer (active mask), move up / down, delete and the keyframe section diamond, then Mode, Invert, Opacity, Feather Inside / Outside, Expand, Position, Width / Height (rectangle, ellipse), Scale, Rotation and, for point masks, a Shape row (point count, shape keyframe). Every change is one undo step; a field drag is one step from press to release. | Fixed (test_masksui inspector*) |
| 27.7 | A new mask is the active one; the timeline viewer then shows its on-screen controls. The viewer's on-screen controls button (bottom left, "Off / Mask", View > On-Screen Mask Controls) shows every mask outline of the current clip without an active mask; clicking an outline makes that mask active; Esc deactivates. The overlay follows Fit / 50 / 100 / 200 % and the clip transform; it is hidden while the playhead is outside the clip. | Fixed (test_masksui overlayVisibility, overlayFollowsZoom) |
| 27.8 | Rectangle / ellipse on screen: drag inside = move (Shift: one axis); corner / edge squares = resize with the opposite side fixed (Alt: about the centre; Shift on a corner: keep the aspect ratio); the round handle above = rotate (Shift: 15 degree steps); the two triangles below the right edge = feather outside / inside (the dotted outlines show both). Point masks use the same handles on their bounding box; their corners scale about the mask centre. Each drag is one undo step. | Fixed (test_masksui overlayMovesResizesRotates, overlayFeatherHandles) |
| 27.9 | Drawing a polygon / bezier mask: click = corner point; press and drag (bezier) = smooth point with tangents along the drag; click the first point, double-click or press Enter = close; Backspace = remove the last point; Esc = close, or discard a mask with fewer points than a shape needs. Freehand: press and draw, releasing closes and simplifies the stroke. A closed path is recentred on its bounding box (rotation and scale act about its middle). Each point is one undo step. | Fixed (test_masksui overlayDrawsPolygon, overlayDrawsBezierAndFreehand, overlayDrawingCancel) |
| 27.10 | Editing points: drag = move (all selected points), Shift+click = add to the selection, drag a tangent end = turn it (a smooth point keeps the other side collinear with its length; Alt breaks the tangent), double-click a point = corner / smooth, click the outline = insert a point there (the curve keeps its shape), Delete / Backspace = remove the selected points (not below the shape's minimum). With focus in the viewer these keys go to the mask, not the timeline. | Fixed (test_masksui overlayDrawsPolygon, overlayDrawsBezierAndFreehand; test_masks insertPointKeepsTheOutline) |
| 27.11 | Masks render the same in the viewer, playback and export (the compositor), on clips inside compound clips / nested timelines and on compound (nested) clips themselves; the CPU fallback applies them as hard-edged shapes (modes and invert exact, feather / expand / partial opacity ignored). Timeline thumbnails show the source media, as before. | Fixed (test_maskrender cpuFallback). Decision on the fallback. |
| 27.12 | Project files store masks from format version 4; projects of versions 1 - 3 load unchanged and are saved in the current format (4 or later); a newer version is refused. | Fixed (test_masks serializationRoundTrip, formatVersion2Loads) |
| 27.13 | Mask tracking (point / planar) and mask presets are not implemented. | Deferred (roadmap v2 stretch goal) |

## 28. Adjustment clips, generators and subtitles (adjsubs branch, v2)

From general knowledge of how Resolve's Edit page treats adjustment clips, generators and its
subtitle track. Clean room: our own wording, icons and colours.

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 28.1 | Effects library > Effects > Adjustment Clip (double-click: at the playhead on the lowest free video track above the busy ones; or drag onto a video track) creates a clip without a picture of its own, as long as the generator duration in Preferences. It trims, moves, splits, ripples, copies and keyframes like any clip. | Fixed (test_generators everyGeneratorIsRegistered; test_subtitles serializationRoundTrip) |
| 28.2 | Its effects, transform, cropping, opacity and masks act on the composite of every video track below it within its time range: a blur blurs across clip edges, a transform moves the whole picture. Inside its footprint the processed picture replaces what is below; outside it (crop, masks, a zoomed-out frame) and in proportion to its opacity the tracks below stay visible. | Fixed (test_adjustment effectMatchesPerClipEffects, blurActsOnComposite, transformMatchesCompound, opacityAndMask). Decision: "replace in the footprint" (no ghost of the unprocessed picture where the composite is transparent). |
| 28.3 | Transitions on an adjustment clip blend its effect in and out (fade from / to the unprocessed picture); inside a compound clip / nested timeline it only affects that timeline's tracks; a disabled clip or hidden track does nothing. | Fixed (test_adjustment transitionFadesEffect, nestedScope, disabled) |
| 28.4 | The timeline shows adjustment clips in their own colour with diagonal hatching (no thumbnails); the inspector shows Transform, Cropping, Masks and the Effects tab, the header says "Adjustment clip". The CPU fallback applies transform, crop, opacity and masks, but no effects. | Fixed (test_adjustment identityIsInvisible cpu). Decision on the fallback (like every clip). |
| 28.5 | Generators: Solid Color, 4 Color Gradient, Linear Gradient, Radial Gradient, Color Bars (SMPTE-style 75 % bars with reverse band, -I / white / +Q / PLUGE row, or full-field 100 % bars), Grey Scale (steps or smooth ramp, horizontal / vertical), Noise (mono / colour, brightness, amount, grain size, seed, still or animated), Countdown (leader-style dial with sweep and number) and Timecode (timeline or clip timecode burnt in, with prefix, size, colour, background box and position). Numeric parameters are keyframeable in the inspector (Video tab) and the keyframe editor; colours and choices are static. | Fixed (test_generators deterministic, colorBars, greyScaleAndGradients, noise, countdownAndTimecode) |
| 28.6 | Generators render identically in the viewer, playback and export, deterministically (noise from an integer hash of the cell, seed and clip-local frame), on the GPU and in the CPU fallback (same formulas). | Fixed (test_generators deterministic) |
| 28.7 | Subtitle tracks sit above all video tracks (ST1 lowest). Header: "ST<n>" label (no source patch), eye (show / hide the captions in the viewer and in burnt-in exports), lock and auto-select. Timeline > Add Subtitle Track; right-click a subtitle track > Add Subtitle; Timeline > Add Subtitle adds a 3 s caption at the playhead on the current / first free subtitle track (a track is created when needed) and selects it. | Fixed (test_subtitles editOps). |
| 28.8 | Captions are clips: split, trim, ripple, roll, move (between subtitle tracks only), copy / paste (back onto subtitle tracks), join (same text), delete and ripple delete like other clips; an insert edit ripples subtitle tracks along with the others. They never go into compound clips. | Fixed (test_subtitles editOps, invariantsRejectWrongTracks) |
| 28.9 | Inspector > Captions tab for a caption: the text (multi-line), "Custom Style" (off: the caption follows its track's style; on: its own copy of the style), and the track's default style (font, size, bold, italic, colour, outline colour / width, drop shadow, background box colour / opacity, alignment, position bottom / top / middle, vertical and horizontal margins, line spacing). Every change is one undo step. | Fixed (test_generators burnInCaptions) |
| 28.10 | File > Import Subtitle (and Import Media / drops) reads SubRip (.srt) and WebVTT (.vtt) into the media pool: UTF-8 with or without BOM, CRLF, ',' or '.' before the milliseconds, numbered or unnumbered cues, multi-line text; markup (<i>, <font>, {\an8}, WebVTT classes / voices) is removed and entities decoded; NOTE / STYLE / REGION blocks are skipped. The item keeps its captions in the project. | Fixed (test_subtitles parseSrt, parseWebVtt, rejectsGarbage, projectFormatAndMigration) |
| 28.11 | Dragging a subtitle item onto the timeline creates the captions with the file's timing from the drop point (snapping like clips), on the subtitle track under the mouse or ST1; overlapping cues go to the tracks above; missing subtitle tracks are created. | Fixed (test_subtitles cuesToCaptions). Decision: the drop point is the file's zero (drop at the start for absolute timing). |
| 28.12 | Deliver > File > Subtitle Settings: Export subtitle (off by default), Format: Burn into video / As a separate file (Export as SRT or WebVTT, next to the render: "name.srt", or "name.<language or n>.srt" for several tracks) / As embedded captions (MP4 / QuickTime: timed text; MKV: SRT or ASS; WebM: WebVTT; one stream per enabled subtitle track, with language and title). Captions are clipped to the render range and timed from its first frame; hidden subtitle tracks are not exported. | Fixed (test_subtitleexport) |
| 28.13 | Project format 7 stores adjustment / generator clips, subtitle tracks and subtitle media items; versions 1 - 6 load unchanged (5 = retiming, 6 = the proxy branch). | Fixed (test_subtitles projectFormatAndMigration) |
| 28.14 | Subtitle styles per word (italic spans), karaoke timing, subtitle import from video containers and caption speed / reading-rate checks are not implemented. | Deferred |
| 28.15 | Selecting a caption shows the Inspector's Captions tab, where its text and style are; selecting a clip that has no title afterwards goes back to the Video tab. | Fixed (bughunt8: the Inspector stayed on the Video tab, which only says "see Captions"; bh8 spec28_15_captionShowsCaptionsTab) |
| 28.16 | Add Subtitle in a subtitle track's right-click menu (track header or empty track area) puts the caption on that track at the playhead (the next free unlocked track when it is locked or busy there). | Fixed (bughunt8: the caption always went to ST1 or the selected caption's track; bh8 spec28_16_addSubtitleOnTheClickedTrack) |
| 28.17 | A caption is named after its text (its first line): typing in the Captions tab renames it, so the Inspector header and the Edit Index show the caption, not "Subtitle". | Fixed (bughunt8: the name stayed "Subtitle" after the text was typed; bh8 spec28_17_captionNameFollowsItsText) |
| 28.18 | Subtitle files in UTF-16 (with or without BOM, as Windows tools write them) and 8-bit files that are not UTF-8 (Windows-1252, else Latin-1) import with their accented letters. | Fixed (bughunt8: UTF-16 files found no cues and Windows-1252 letters became replacement characters; test_subtitles parseOtherEncodings) |
| 28.19 | With several captions selected, typing caption text in the Captions tab changes only the caption shown (each caption has its own words; its name follows only its own text); the style settings still reach every selected caption (24.1). | Fixed (bughunt9: the text was written into every selected caption; `multiclip::propagate()` and `peerRef()` leave a caption's keys::Text out; bh9 spec28_19_captionTextIsPerCaption) |
| 28.20 | A cue without text (empty, or nothing left once markup is removed) would be an invisible caption: it is skipped on import, and the import says how many were skipped per file (Import Media and Import Media Folder); a file with only empty cues is not imported. | Fixed (bughunt9: empty cues became empty captions; `subtitles::parse(..., emptyCues)`, `PreparedMedia::note`, `ImportResult::notes`; bh9 spec28_20_emptyCuesSkippedAndCounted) |

## 29. Speed changes and retiming (retime branch, roadmap v2)

Speed points, speed ramps, reverse and freeze, written from general knowledge of how Resolve's
retime controls behave (clean room: our own wording and drawing). Model and maths:
`src/core/Retime.h`; edits: `src/core/RetimeOps.h`.

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 29.1 | A clip plays at a constant speed (100 % by default; negative = reverse, 0 = freeze) or follows a retime curve: source frame over clip time, whose keys are the speed points. Between two speed points the speed is constant (straight curve) or ramps (curved). The picture is continuous at every speed point. | Fixed (test_retime curveSegmentsAndExtrapolation, smoothRampIsMonotoneAndContinuous) |
| 29.2 | Ctrl+R shows / hides the retime controls of the selected clips (else the clips under the playhead on auto-select video tracks): a speed bar under the clip name with one field per speed segment (its speed, colour-coded: slower, faster, reverse, freeze; a menu arrow), a line through the clip at each speed point with a slide handle in the bar and a retime handle at the bottom, and a retime handle at the clip end. View state only (not undoable, not saved). | Fixed (test_retimebar ctrlRTogglesTheSpeedBar) |
| 29.3 | The speed menu (arrow of a field, or right-click on a field): Change Speed presets 10 % - 1000 %, Normal (100 %), Add Speed Point (at the playhead), Freeze Frame (a one-second freeze segment at the playhead), Reverse Segment, Reset Clip, Retime Curve, Retime Process (Project Settings / Nearest / Frame Blend / Optical Flow), Pitch Correction, Hide Retime Controls. Each command is one undo step. | Fixed (test_retimebar speedMenu*) |
| 29.4 | Dragging a speed point's retime handle (bottom) changes the speed of the segment before it: that segment keeps its source material and gets longer or shorter. Dragging the slide handle (top) moves where the speed changes, keeping both segments' speeds (the next segment covers less or more material). The clip end's handle retimes the last segment. Each drag is one undo step. Right-click a point: No Ramp / Smooth Ramp / Ease In and Out, Delete Speed Point. | Fixed (test_retimebar handleDragsRetimeAndSlide, speedPointMenuRampAndDelete; test_retime retimeAndSlideHandles) |
| 29.5 | Ripple: whenever a retime makes a clip longer or shorter, the later clips on its track move by the same amount (the linked audio clip and its track change the same way, so V and A stay in sync). The Change Clip Speed dialog can turn ripple off: the clip then keeps its length, limited by the media. | Fixed (test_retime speedPointThenSlowSegmentRipples, linkedAudioFollows, changeClipSpeedDialog) |
| 29.6 | Changing a segment's speed keeps its material: length = material / speed, rounded to whole frames, and the speed is then exact. Setting a freeze segment to a speed makes it play from its frame for the same length. Speed points stay where they are even when both sides end up at the same speed. | Fixed (test_retime) |
| 29.7 | Reverse Segment plays the segment backwards with the same length; the following material continues from where the reversed segment ends (the picture never jumps). Reverse speed in the dialog plays the whole clip backwards over the same material. | Fixed (test_retime reverseSegmentBounces, changeClipSpeedDialog). Decision: continuous curve (no jump back). |
| 29.8 | Shift+R (Freeze Frame) makes each target clip show the frame under the playhead (its first frame when the playhead is outside it) for its whole length. Freeze Frame in the speed menu inserts a one-second freeze at the playhead and ripples. | Fixed (test_retime freezeSegmentAndFreezeFrame; test_retimebar freezeFrameAndRetimeCurveActions) |
| 29.9 | R opens Change Clip Speed: Speed (%), Frames per second (speed x source rate) and Duration are linked; Reverse speed, Freeze frame, Ripple sequence (on), Pitch correction. Applying it replaces a retime curve with one speed over the clip's material. | Fixed (test_retimebar changeSpeedDialogLinksTheFields; test_retime changeClipSpeedDialog) |
| 29.10 | Keyframes of the other parameters stay at their clip time when a clip is retimed (attached to the timeline, not to the source), like Resolve. Trimming, splitting, slipping and joining a retimed clip keep the picture at every remaining frame; speed points move with the content like keyframes. | Fixed (test_retime trimSplitJoinKeepTheMapping). Decision. |
| 29.11 | The retime curve ("Retime / Retime Frame", source frame over clip time) is listed in the inline curve and keyframe editors and the Keyframes panel; its keys move, delete, copy and ease like other keys (Smooth = speed ramp). Ctrl+Shift+R (Retime Curve) opens the clip's curve editor showing only the retime curve. | Fixed (test_retimebar freezeFrameAndRetimeCurveActions) |
| 29.12 | Retime process per clip (speed menu) or the project default (Project Settings > Master Settings > Frame Interpolation): Nearest = the source frame at the exact source time; Frame Blend = the two neighbouring source frames mixed by the position between them; Optical Flow = motion-compensated in-between frames (GPU block-matching flow; falls back to the blend where motion cannot be matched). A clip at 100 % always shows exact frames. The CPU fallback renders Optical Flow as Frame Blend. | Fixed (test_retimerender frameBlendMixesNeighbours, opticalFlowMovesTheBox; test_retime projectDefaultProcessFollowsSettings) |
| 29.13 | Playback, export and the viewer pick the same source frame for every timeline frame (one mapping in core); thumbnails follow the retime curve and waveforms follow it too (freeze and reverse drawn silent). | Fixed (test_retimerender curveSelectsExactSourceFrames, reverseAndFreezeFrames) |
| 29.14 | Audio follows the speed: with Pitch Correction (default for new speed changes) it is time-stretched (pitch kept), without it resampled (pitch follows the speed). Freeze and reverse parts are muted. | Fixed (test_timestretch; test_audiomixer freezeAndReverseAreMuted, pitchCorrectedClipKeepsLength) |
| 29.15 | Project files store the retime curve, process and pitch correction from format version 5; the single speed of older projects loads as one constant segment with pitch correction off (they resampled their audio), so they play as before. | Fixed (test_retime serializationRoundTrip, formatThreeSpeedMigrates) |
| 29.16 | Motion estimation settings and a per-clip Inspector retime section are not implemented; the speed-change nudges followed in polish round 3 (29.17). | Deferred (nudges: Fixed, 29.17; the Inspector section: Fixed, 24.21 in bughunt7; motion estimation still Deferred) |
| 29.17 | Increase / Decrease Clip Speed by 10% (Clip menu, assignable in Keyboard Customization): the selected clips (else the clips under the playhead, with linked parts) at a constant forward speed change by 10 percentage points (100 % -> 110 % -> 120 %), at least 10 %; durations follow as with Change Clip Speed (later clips ripple); reverse, freeze and ramped clips are left alone; one undo step "Increase / Decrease Clip Speed". | Fixed (polish round 3; test_polish3 spec29_17). Decision: percentage points rather than a factor (two nudges up and two down return to 100 %); no default keys (none known for Resolve). |

## 30. Proxy media, optimized media and render cache (proxies branch, roadmap v2)

From general knowledge of how Resolve handles proxies, optimized media and its render cache
(clean room; Resolve was not used).
Tests: ml = test_medialinks, px = test_proxies, rc = test_rendercache, mm = test_mediamanagement.

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 30.1 | Media pool context menu (video items): Generate Optimized Media, Generate Proxy Media, Stop Generating, Link Proxy Media..., Unlink Proxy Media, Delete Proxy Media..., Delete Optimized Media.... They act on every selected item; audio files, stills and timelines are skipped (a note when nothing could be queued). | Fixed (mm endToEnd) |
| 30.2 | Generation runs in the background, one file at a time, at low priority; the tile shows a badge "P 42%" (proxy) / "O 42%" (optimized) while it runs, then "P" / "O"; the list view has Proxy and Optimized Media columns ("None", "960x540", "Generating 42%", "Stale (source changed)", "Missing"). Stop Generating cancels; a cancelled or failed job leaves no file and no link. | Fixed (px cancelLeavesNothing; mm endToEnd) |
| 30.3 | Proxy resolution: Choose automatically (default) / Original / Half / Quarter (Project Settings > Optimized Media and Render Cache). Automatic = the largest of the three that is at most 540 lines high (1080p and 4K sources: 960x540; 720p: 640x360; SD: original). | Decision (ml proxySizes) |
| 30.4 | Proxy format: H.264 fast decode (default: 12-frame GOP without B-frames, CAVLC, no deblocking; hardware encoded where possible), ProRes 422 Proxy / LT, DNxHR LB / SQ, in QuickTime with the source's sound as PCM. Sources with alpha get ProRes 4444. Optimized media: Original resolution, DNxHR LB by default. | Decision (measured, export/readme.md) |
| 30.5 | Proxies are stored in the proxy folder (Preferences > System > Media Storage > Proxy generation location; default <cache folder>/ProxyMedia) as "<name>_<8 hex digits of the source path hash>.mov" with a small JSON sidecar; optimized media in <cache folder>/OptimizedMedia. Regenerating replaces the file atomically. | Fixed (ml stableNames; px generateLinkAndPlay) |
| 30.6 | Links are saved with the project. On load and on import, proxies / optimized media with the stable name and a sidecar naming the source are linked automatically (also in other projects). Linking is not an edit: undo never unlinks. | Fixed (ml projectRoundTrip, linksSurviveUndo, sidecarAndAutoLink; mm endToEnd) |
| 30.7 | A proxy made from a source that has changed since (size or modification time) is stale: never used, badge "P!" in amber, column "Stale (source changed)". A missing proxy file is shown the same way ("Missing"). | Fixed (ml staleDetection; px generateLinkAndPlay) |
| 30.8 | Link Proxy Media... links any video file of (nearly) the same length by hand; Unlink keeps the file; Delete Proxy Media deletes generated files and unlinks, but only unlinks files outside the proxy folder. | Fixed (px generateLinkAndPlay; mm endToEnd) |
| 30.9 | Playback > Proxy Handling: Prefer Proxies (default), Prefer Camera Originals (the original while it is online; an offline original plays its proxy, picture and sound), Disable All Proxies. Playback > Use Proxy Media If Available toggles between Prefer Proxies and Disable All Proxies. Playback > Use Optimized Media If Available (default on). Settings are per project. | Fixed (px generateLinkAndPlay; mm endToEnd) |
| 30.10 | With a proxy the clip looks the same, only less detailed: same frame (frame-exact), same framing, crop in source pixels, same rotation and pixel aspect handling. Applies to the timeline viewer and the source viewer. | Fixed (px generateLinkAndPlay, rotatedSourceKeepsGeometry) |
| 30.11 | The viewer title bar shows "Proxy" (amber) while the shown timeline / clip uses proxy or optimized media. | Fixed (mm endToEnd: PlaybackEngine::proxiesInUse) |
| 30.12 | Export always renders the camera originals. Deliver > Video > "Use proxy media" / "Use optimized media" (off by default) render with them instead. | Fixed (px exportUsesOriginals) |
| 30.13 | Playback > Render Cache: None (default for new projects), Smart, User. Smart caches one-second sections with a transition, a composite (two or more video tracks with pictures), effects or masks, a compound / nested clip or a speed change. User caches clips marked Clip > Render Cache Clip Output (an undoable clip flag) plus, when enabled in Project Settings, transitions and composites. | Fixed (rc chunkGrid, userMode; mm endToEnd) |
| 30.14 | Background caching starts after the user has been idle for N seconds (Project Settings: "Enable background caching after N seconds", default 5); playback pauses it and abandons the section in progress. Sections render from the camera originals at timeline resolution in the render cache format (DNxHR LB default; DNxHR SQ, ProRes 422 Proxy, ProRes 422). | Fixed (rc pauseAbortsAndBackgroundStarts) |
| 30.15 | The timeline ruler shows a thin bar at its top: red over sections that need caching, blue over cached ones. | Fixed (new) |
| 30.16 | Playback shows cached sections from the cache; the picture matches the uncached render within the codec's loss (DNxHR LB on the test content: mean 2.9-4.2 of 255, luma PSNR 34-37 dB). Cached pictures are opaque (composited over black). | Fixed (rc backgroundCacheAndHit) |
| 30.17 | Invalidation: any change that can alter a section's pictures makes its cache unused (property / keyframe changes, trims, slips, moves, effects and their parameters, masks, transitions on either side of a cut, track enable, timeline size / rate, cache format, relinking or replacing a media file, edits inside nested timelines). Renaming, colour labels, markers, audio changes and the cache flag itself keep it. Undo returns to the cached state. | Fixed (rc *Invalidates*, irrelevantChangesKeepTheCache, transitionNeighbours, nestedTimelineInvalidates, backgroundCacheAndHit) |
| 30.18 | Playback > Delete Render Cache... deletes every cached file; the cache folder is kept under a size limit (Preferences > Media Storage, default 20 GB, oldest files first). Resolve's Delete Render Cache > Unused / Selected Clips are not offered. | Decision |

## 31. Audio level lines, fade handles, Clip Attributes, stacked timelines (attrs branch, v2)

From general knowledge of Resolve's Edit page (clean room: our own wording, drawing and
icons; Resolve was not used). Tests: lo = test_levelops, ll = test_levellines,
ca = test_clipattributes.

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 31.1 | Every audio clip (media or compound / nested) high enough for a waveform shows its volume as a level line over the waveform: linear in dB from +24 dB (top, the inspector's maximum) to -60 dB (bottom); the exact keyframe curve (eases included) is drawn. With the selection tool, dragging the line up / down changes the volume (Shift: a tenth of the speed, can be pressed during the drag); without keys the clip's constant volume changes, with keys the keys that shape the line under the pointer move (the two around it, the key itself, or the first / last key outside them). One undo step per drag; the clip is not moved. | Fixed (ll lineDragIsOneUndoStep; lo offsetWithoutKeys, keysAndSegments) |
| 31.2 | Alt+click on the line adds a volume key there with the line's value (one undo step) and selects it. Keys are diamonds on the line (selected ones highlighted); dragging one moves it in time (whole frames, kept inside the clip) and value together (one undo step); a key landing on another replaces it. Delete / Backspace (edit.delete / edit.ripple_delete) with only keys selected deletes the keys, not the clip; right-click on a key offers Delete Keyframe. They are the same `audio/volumeDb` keys the inspector, the keyframe / curve editors and the Keyframes panel edit. | Fixed (ll altClickAddsKeyAndKeyDrag; lo keysAndSegments) |
| 31.3 | The mixer plays the line: the volume envelope is evaluated per 64 samples at the exact fractional frame and ramped, so keyed volume changes are click-free. | Fixed (lo keysAndSegments: volumeDbAt is the mixer's evaluation; test_audiomixer volume keyframes) |
| 31.4 | Fade handles: hovering a clip (video, audio, titles, generators, compound clips; not captions) shows two small handles at its top corners; dragging one inward sets the clip's fade-in / fade-out length in frames (one undo step), drawn as a shaded wedge with the fade line; fade-in + fade-out never exceed the clip. Decision: fades are clip properties (`fadeInFrames` / `fadeOutFrames`), not keyframes, so they survive trims (the fade keeps its length), a split keeps the fade-in on the left part and the fade-out on the right part, joining keeps the outer fades, a swap keeps them with their clips. | Fixed (ll fadeHandles; lo fades) |
| 31.5 | Video fades ramp the opacity linearly (0 on the first frame, full after the fade-in; mirrored at the end), multiplied with the keyframed opacity and transitions, identically in the viewer, playback, export, the render cache and the CPU fallback. Audio fades are equal-power (sin / cos) gain ramps like the crossfades, multiplied with the volume. | Fixed (ca videoFadesRender gpu/cpu, audioFadesMix, fadeMaths) |
| 31.6 | Clip > Clip Attributes… (also in the media pool's and the timeline clip's context menus) edits how a media file is interpreted. Decision: the attributes belong to the media pool item and apply to every clip of it (the timeline entry edits the clip's item); several selected items get the same values; one undo step; the clips mirror the item (render snapshots need no media pool lookup). Timelines, compound clips and subtitle items have none. | Fixed (lo attributesMirrorAndUndo) |
| 31.7 | Video tab: Pixel aspect ratio (Auto = the file's, Square, NTSC / PAL 4:3 and 16:9 SD, anamorphic 1.33x / 1.5x / 1.8x / 2x), Data levels (Auto / Video / Full), Field dominance (Auto = upper first, Progressive, Upper, Lower) + Enable deinterlacing (keeps the first field and interpolates the other's lines), Rotation (0 / 90 / 180 / 270 clockwise, added to the file's own). Pixel aspect and rotation change the picture's shape and the fit into the frame (GPU and CPU renderers, the source viewer's frame); data levels and deinterlacing are GPU only (the software fallback ignores them, like effects; said in the tooltips). With proxy media the original's shape is kept, turned by the extra rotation. The Video tab is disabled for audio-only media. | Fixed (ca pixelAspectAndRotation gpu/cpu, dataLevels, deinterlace) |
| 31.8 | Audio tab: Format Default (the decoder's layout: mono on both sides, stereo as is, more channels downmixed) / Stereo (left output = chosen source channel, right output = chosen source channel; Swap button; Mute per side) / Mono (one source channel, or all channels mixed, on both sides). Applied by the decoder's channel matrix, so playback, scrubbing and export agree; changing it re-decodes from the exact sample. Timecode tab: a start timecode for the media (shown in the source viewer). Disabled tabs carry a tooltip. | Fixed (ca channelMatrixMaths, channelMappingMix) |
| 31.9 | View > Stacked Timelines shows a second timeline above the editor, each with its own tabs (the "+" button opens another timeline in the upper one), playhead (scrubbing the inactive one's ruler does not activate it) and zoom / scroll (Alt or Ctrl + wheel). The inactive one is drawn read-only and dimmed; a click in its tracks makes it the active timeline: it drives the viewer, the inspector and every command and key, the editor takes its place and the other timeline moves to the other pane (each keeps its tabs, zoom and playhead). Switching the editor's tab to the timeline the other pane shows puts the previous one there. | Fixed (ll stackedTimelines) |
| 31.10 | Clips are copied between stacked timelines by dragging: from the inactive pane onto the editor (ghost preview; Ctrl inserts), or out of the editor (a move dragged above / below the view) onto the inactive pane, which then becomes active with the copy. The copy keeps linked partners together, goes to the track under the pointer (video and audio on the same index), adds missing tracks, refuses compound clips that would contain themselves, and is one undo step in the receiving timeline. The source is never changed. | Fixed (ll stackedTimelines) |
| 31.11 | Project format 10 stores fades ("fadeIn" / "fadeOut") and Clip Attributes ("attributes" on media items and their clips); versions 1 - 9 load unchanged (no fades, no attributes). Stacked view state is per session (not saved). | Fixed (lo serialization, attributesMirrorAndUndo, olderFormatLoads) |
| 31.12 | Not implemented: per-clip (instead of per-item) attributes, audio track layouts with several mono tracks per clip (Resolve splits a stereo file into two mono clip tracks), fade curve shapes other than linear / equal-power, field-order detection from the stream, timecode from the file's metadata, deinterlacing and data levels in the CPU fallback, the stacked layout saved with the project. | Deferred |
| 31.13 | Audio clip waveforms are drawn like Resolve's: mirrored around the centre, linear in amplitude relative to full scale (0 dBFS = the full half height, -6 dBFS about half of it; never normalised per clip), so quiet passages are small and loud ones big. Each pixel column shows the largest peak of both channels over exactly the media time it covers and, lighter over it, the RMS body. Media clips read this from a multi-resolution summary (5 ms blocks, each level 4 times coarser), so zooming in shows detail (interpolated between blocks) and a quiet stretch stays small however far the view is zoomed out. A column keeps the largest peak it covers, so a heavily limited master keeps a nearly constant peak outline when zoomed far out; its level changes then show in the RMS body. Compound / nested clips use the same mapping and drawing with 10 ms blocks of their mix. The Edit, Cut and Audio page timelines share this drawing. | Fixed (waveforms branch; test_waveforms peaksAreLinearFullScale, rangeMatchesBruteForceAtEveryZoom, drawnHeightIsLinearAmplitude, zoomedOutDoesNotSaturate) |
| 31.14 | The waveform follows the clip volume (Inspector volume and the level line with its keys, eases included): -6 dB halves its height, +dB enlarges it up to the clip height (clipped there), a keyed fade changes it along the clip. Track faders and mute do not change it. | Fixed (waveforms branch; test_waveforms heightFollowsClipVolume) |
| 31.15 | Waveform cache files are format version 2 (16-bit min / max and RMS per channel per 5 ms); files of the older version 1 (8-bit min / max per 10 ms) are ignored and computed again, then rewritten. Not implemented: one waveform lane per channel inside the clip (Resolve draws a stereo clip as two stacked waveforms); Kinoklip draws both channels merged. | Fixed (waveforms branch; test_waveforms oldCacheVersionIsRebuilt; test_mediacache waveform) |

## 32. Multicam clips and sync (multicam branch, roadmap v2)

Multicam editing and clip sync, written from general public knowledge of how Resolve's
multicam clips and multicam viewer behave (clean room: our own wording, icons and drawing).
Model: `src/core/Multicam.h`; sync: `src/media/AudioSync.h`, `src/ui/mediapool/MulticamSync.h`;
viewer: `src/ui/viewer/ViewerMulticam.cpp`. Tests: `mc` = `tests/core/test_multicam.cpp`,
`as` = `tests/media/test_audiosync.cpp`, `mr` = `tests/render/test_multicamrender.cpp`,
`mu` = `tests/panels/test_multicamui.cpp`.

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 32.1 | Media pool, one or more video / audio clips selected: right-click > Create New Multicam Clip Using Selected Clips… (also Clip > New Multicam Clip…) asks for the name ("Multicam Clip N"), the angle sync (Timecode, Sound, In Points, Clip Start), the angle names (clip names or "Angle n") and whether to move the source clips into a new "Original Clips" bin; the last choices are remembered. The multicam clip appears in the current bin with its own icon. One undo step. | Fixed (new; mc projectCreatesItemAndBin; mu createFromMediaPool) |
| 32.2 | A multicam clip is a special nested timeline: angle k = video track k + audio track k (named after the angle), each source placed at its synced position, the earliest at frame 0; positions are exact to the microsecond (start rounded up to a frame, the sub-frame rest skipped at the head). Rate and picture size come from the first clip with video. | Fixed (mc buildPlacesSyncedSources). Decision: one audio track per angle (multichannel angles keep their channels in one track). |
| 32.3 | Sync by sound: the downmixed audio's onset envelope is cross-correlated (FFT, every offset at once, then refined to 1 ms); different gain, noise, lengths and partial overlaps are handled; the longest clip is the reference. Clips without common sound are reported with their confidence (and placed at the start if the user continues). | Fixed (as knownOffsets, unrelatedRecordingsAreRejected, longRecordingsAreFast; within 2 ms on synthetic scenes) |
| 32.4 | Sync by timecode uses the files' embedded start timecode (container / stream "timecode" metadata, drop frame understood); sync by in points lines up the clips' source in points (the source viewer's marks); clips missing a timecode are reported. | Fixed (mc timecodeParsing) |
| 32.5 | Sync and Place on Timeline (media pool context menu and Clip menu: Based on Waveform / Timecode / In Points) puts the synced clips on new tracks at the top of the current timeline, from the playhead, video and audio of each clip linked. One undo step. | Fixed (new, Kinoklip's command; mc placeSyncedStacksTracks; mu syncAndPlace) |
| 32.6 | A multicam clip is edited into a timeline like any clip (drag, F9/F10/...); on the timeline a multicam segment shows the active angle ("n  angle - clip"), and its thumbnails show that angle. | Fixed (screenshot target multicam) |
| 32.7 | Source viewer mode menu (Source / Multicam) or View > Multicam Viewer: the source viewer shows the angles of the multicam clip under the timeline playhead as a grid (2x2, 3x3 or 4x4 per page, automatic or chosen; page arrows when there are more angles), following the playhead live during playback. Opening a multicam clip from the media pool shows its grid (until the timeline playhead is moved again). Each cell is labelled with the angle number and name; the active angle is framed red (video and audio), blue (video only) or green (audio only). | Fixed (mu multicamViewerFollowsTimelineAndCuts). Decision: the grid is a private timeline played by the source player, so it uses the normal decode / render path. |
| 32.8 | Click an angle in the multicam viewer, or press 1-9 while it is shown: in Cut mode the multicam clip is cut at the playhead and continues on that angle; in Switch mode the current segment changes its angle. Video and Audio / Video Only / Audio Only choose the side (Clip > Multicam). Works while playing; each choice is one undo step. A cut on a segment's first frame switches it; choosing the angle it already shows does nothing. | Fixed (mc cutToAngle, switchAngle; mu multicamViewerFollowsTimelineAndCuts) |
| 32.9 | A video-only (or audio-only) cut cuts both sides at the playhead so video and audio segments stay pairwise linked; only the chosen side changes its angle. | Decision (mc cutToAngle) |
| 32.10 | Timeline right-click on a multicam clip: Switch Multicam Clip Angle > Video and Audio / Video / Audio > angle list; Open in Timeline (edit the angles: sync, add, trim; every instance follows); Flatten Multicam Clip (the segment becomes the clips of its active angle, cut to its window, video and audio linked). | Fixed (mc flatten; mu multicamViewerFollowsTimelineAndCuts: flatten). The context menu itself is untested. |
| 32.11 | Rendering, playback and export show only the active angle's picture and sound, exactly from the cut frame; locked tracks refuse cuts. | Fixed (mr activeAngleAndCuts, audioFollowsAudioAngle, gridShowsEveryAngle; mc cutRespectsLockedTracks) |
| 32.12 | Project files store multicam clips from format version 8; older projects load unchanged; newer versions are refused. | Fixed (mc saveLoadAndMigration, clipJsonRoundTrip) |
| 32.13 | Angle decoding at reduced size without proxies (FFmpeg lowres) for the grid (with proxies linked, the grid decodes the proxies like any viewer), sync by markers or out points, camera-metadata angle names, and adding audio-only angles to an existing multicam clip from the media pool are not implemented. | Deferred |

mo = tests/panels/test_mediaorganize.cpp.

|---|---|---|

| 33.2 | New Bin (File menu, Ctrl+Shift+N, title bar, context menus) creates a bin inside the current bin and starts renaming it; bins rename in place (F2 / double-click). Each is one undo step. | Fixed (mo binTreeDragAndUndo) |
| 33.3 | Dragging a bin onto another bin makes it a sub-bin; dropping it on the upper or lower edge of a row places it before / after that row. A bin cannot be dropped into itself or its own sub-bins; project bins and power bins stay in their own section. One undo step. | Fixed (bn moveBinReordersAndReparents; mo binTreeDragAndUndo) |
| 33.4 | Media items dragged onto a bin move there (one undo step). Files from the file manager dropped on a bin are imported into it. | Fixed (mo binTreeDragAndUndo) |
| 33.5 | Delete Bin removes the bin, its sub-bins and the media inside them (asks first when it is not empty and says how many timeline clips go offline). Timelines and compound clips inside are kept and move to Master. One undo step. | Decision (timelines are only deleted explicitly; bn deleteBinRemovesContents) |
| 33.6 | Each bin remembers its view mode (thumbnails / list) and sort order; they are saved with the project. | Fixed (bn projectSaveLoadAndBinViews; mo perBinViewState) |

A smart bin shows every matching item of the project, wherever it is, and updates live when items, rules or timelines change. Edit / rename / delete from the bin list menu (undoable). | Fixed (sb everyFieldAndOperator, matchAllAndAny; mo smartBinIsLive) |

| 33.9 | Power bins: bins shared by every project (stored in the application data folder, items by absolute path). Clips can be imported or dragged into them; using a power bin clip in a timeline or the source viewer adds it to the project's media pool. Power bin changes are saved at once and are not part of the project's undo. | Decision (Resolve keeps them in the database; bn powerBinsPersistAcrossProjects; mo powerBinsAcrossProjects) |
| 33.10 | Clip Color (item and timeline clip context menus): sixteen colours plus Clear Color. | 33.11 | Flags: sixteen flag colours, several per item, toggled from the item and timeline clip context menus (a timeline clip sets the flags of its source); shown as small markers on tiles and timeline clips. | Fixed (bn colorsFlagsMetadataUndo; mo colorsAndFlagsInViewsAndTimeline) |
| 33.12 | List view columns: right-click the header to choose columns (clip colour, flags, usage, file name, path, dates, description, comments, keywords, scene, shot, take, camera, angle, good take, rating, ...); every column sorts, numbers and dates numerically. | Fixed (mo columnsSortAndChooser) |
| 33.13 | Search covers name, path, clip colour and flag names, keywords, comments and the other metadata; the colour filter button shows one clip colour (or clips without one). | Fixed (mo searchAndColorFilter) |
| 33.14 | Metadata panel: Clip Details (name, file, codec, resolution, frame rate, duration, audio, clip colour, flags, usage; read-only) and editable Shot and Scene (scene, shot, take, camera, angle, good take) and Notes (description, comments, keywords, rating). Each edit is one undo step. | Fixed (mo metadataEditAndBatch) |
| 33.15 | With several items selected the metadata panel shows shared values (others empty with a "multiple values" hint); editing a field writes that field to all of them in one undo step. | Fixed (mo metadataEditAndBatch) |
| 33.16 | Usage: the Usage column counts the timeline uses of an item (every timeline, compound clips and nested timelines included; a linked video + audio pair is one use); tiles of used items show a small dot. | Fixed (bn usageCountsNestedAndCompound; mo usageCommands) |
| 33.17 | Select Clips in Timeline selects the clips of the active timeline that use the selected items. Remove Unused Clips removes every media file used in no timeline after a confirmation (one undo step; files stay on disk). | Fixed (mo usageCommands) |

| 33.20 | Relink: Relink Offline Media… (the pool menu, every offline item) and Relink Selected Clips… (the item menu, when a selected item is offline) ask for a folder and find the files by name in it and its subfolders (the shallowest match), then say how many were relinked and how many are still offline; one undo step. | Fixed (polish round 2: only the chosen folder itself was searched; test_robustness offlineAndMovedMedia) |
