No command was refused during playback, but: the playhead jumped on insert, overwrite, append, paste and ripple range deletes (`TimelineController::setPlayheadAfterEdit()`); the play range was fixed when playback started (`PlaybackEngine::followRange()`); the unheard 170 ms of old sound played out (`AudioRing::requestRewind()`, `AudioFeeder::remixUnheard()`); a parked seek lost its target frame; slow play kept the old picture until the next frame. Tested with fake sound devices only (a capturing device behind the real `RingPuller`, ring and clock), not on a real sound card, and without the 60 ms device buffer. Measured with solid clips: the new picture on the next frame; on decoded 1080p H.264 (software decoding, CPU compositor, load average 12 - 18): 4 to 10 frames when the edit needs a decoder that is not open yet, and 0 to 1 frame when it is open (the undo); so "a few frames" does not hold for the first opening of a decoder under load. The test prints these figures and does not limit them. Tests: ep spec48_7_everyCommandRunsDuringPlayback, spec48_7_sourceEditsKeepThePlayheadWhilePlaying, spec48_7_dragMoveDuringPlayback, spec48_7_rippleDeleteOfThePlayingClipPlaysWhatFollows, spec48_7_rippleDeleteOnDecodedMedia, spec48_7_soundFollowsTheEditInSync, spec48_7_soundArrangementOnlyLooksAtTheRing, spec48_7_uiOnlyChangesKeepTheFramesInFlight, spec48_7_theEndFollowsTheEdit, spec48_7_otherRatesAndLoops, spec48_7_ringRewind |
| 48.8 | Preferences > User > Playback Settings > "After an edit during playback": Keep playing (default) or Stop playback (key `playback/stopOnEdit`, program engine only: source players ignore it). Stop playback pauses on the current frame showing the new timeline; a command that moves the playhead when paused then moves it as usual. What counts as an edit: a finished undo step, or an undo or redo, that changes the arrangement of the timeline played (`render::sameArrangement()`): tracks added or removed or enabled; a clip's media, position, length, source range, speed, retime curve, enabled state or link group; a transition's type, length or alignment; a mouse gesture counts once, at its release. What does not: anything done in the Inspector, even if it changes a length (value drags, clip speed, transition alignment; `Project::markEditKeepsPlaying()`); Dynamic Trim; effects, grades, keyframes, mixer and automation; marks and markers (marking while playing is how one logs); colours, flags, names; a track's lock, mute, solo, height or auto select. | New option (`PlaybackBehaviour::stopOnEdit`, `PlaybackEngine::noteEditCommitted()`, connected in main.cpp from `Project::editCommitted`). Decision: the default is Keep playing, and it is unverified as a statement about Resolve in general. Search summaries quoting the Resolve 18.6 manual (the page itself was not read) describe trimming during playback (looped playback with the Comma and Period keys, and JKL dynamic trimming "during playback, while monitoring audio and watching the video"), which fits Keep playing, but it says nothing found about ripple delete or other commands while playing, and Resolve was not run (clean room). Tests: ep spec48_8_stopPlaybackAfterAnEdit, spec48_8_valueChangesDoNotStopPlayback, spec48_8_dynamicTrimIsNoEdit, spec48_8_preferenceIsStoredAndDefaultsToKeepPlaying, spec48_7_dragMoveDuringPlayback |

## 49. Bog fixes: Inspector, keyframes and panels (boginspector branch)

A YouTuber's list of nitpicks after editing in Resolve (owner request): each one checked against
Kinoklip. Where Resolve's own default is what the presenter disliked, Resolve's behaviour stays
the default and a Preference offers the alternative. Tests: `bi` = `tests/app/test_boginspector.cpp`.

| # | Expected behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 49.1 | Removing keyframes never resets a parameter to its default. Clicking a filled diamond (Inspector, section diamond, Keyframes panel tree) removes the key at the playhead; when it was the last key the parameter stays static at that key's value. Deleting several keys at once (Delete in the keyframe / curve editors and the Keyframes panel, `clip.delete_keyframe`) leaves a parameter that loses every key at the value it showed at the playhead before the deletion, whatever order the keys were selected in (before: the value of whichever key happened to be removed last). Only the Inspector's reset arrow returns a parameter to its default (and drops its keys). A key removed while other keys remain lets the curve interpolate between those, so the value at the playhead follows them: removing a custom key next to a key still at the default shows the default, because that key is what remains. | Already handled for the diamonds (`AnimatedValue::removeKey` copies the last key's value into the static value; bi spec49_1_inspectorDiamondOnTheLastKeyKeepsTheValue, ko spec15_9_deleteAndInterp); fixed for multi-key deletion (bi spec49_1_deletingAllKeysKeepsTheShownValue). Decision: no Preference. Unverified: public documentation found says the keyframe button deletes a key and the reset button removes all keys, but not what value remains, and the presenter reports Resolve resetting; Resolve's actual behaviour could not be checked (clean room). |
| 49.2 | Link Zoom (Inspector, Transform) changes only when its check box is clicked (or by Reset / Paste Attributes, which the user asks for). Shift on a viewer scale handle frees (or links) only that drag; the stored link is never written by a drag, so a multi-clip edit can no longer switch other clips' links either (before: a Shift corner drag unlinked the zoom, and every linked drag wrote the link onto the clip and, through the multi-clip propagation, onto the other selected clips). A linked Inspector edit of Zoom X or Y scales both axes by the same factor, so a ratio left by a free Shift scale is kept instead of Y snapping to X. Keyboard edits, undo / redo and selection changes never touch it. This replaces the "a free scale of a linked zoom unlinks it" part of 24.11. | Fixed (bi spec49_2_dragsNeverStoreTheLink, spec49_2_linkedEditKeepsTheRatio; test_transformcontrols scaleHandles) |
| 49.3 | Keyframes of every effect shown together: the Keyframes panel's dope sheet has a master row ("Keyframes", every key of the item), an item row and a row per group (Transform, Cropping, Masks, Title, Audio, each effect, Color, Retime) above its parameters; groups collapse and expand with their arrow (rows hide in the tree and the dope sheet alike); master and group diamonds select every key at their frame, so keys of different effects line up and move together (15.9, 15.14 - 15.17). The inline timeline lane has the same master and group rows, without collapsing (it opens the Keyframes panel for that). The marks on a clip and its keyframe / curve toggles now count every parameter except the retime curve, which has its own speed bar (before: mask, mask shape and Color page keys drew no marks, and a clip animated only by its grade offered no toggles). | Panel and lanes already handled (kp spec15_14_showsAnimatedParams; ke spec15_9_masterDiamondMovesAllKeysAtFrame); marks and toggles fixed (`keyframeops::clipMarkFrames`; bi spec49_3_clipMarksCoverEveryParameter). The painter and the toggle use that function; the drawn pixels are not tested. |
| 49.4 | Easing commands: Clip > Keyframe Interpolation > Keyframe Linear / Ease In / Ease Out / Ease In and Out (`clip.keyframe_linear`, `clip.keyframe_ease_in`, `clip.keyframe_ease_out`, `clip.keyframe_ease_in_out`) apply the preset to the selected keys, else to every key at the playhead of the selected clips; one undo step "Change Keyframe Interpolation". No default key in any keymap (Resolve, and none of the other editors' presets, has one); assignable in Keyboard Customization, and an assigned key is shown beside the matching entry of the keyframe and curve editors' context menus. | Fixed (new; bi spec49_4_easingCommandsHaveNoDefaultKey, spec49_4_easingCommandsActOnSelectionOrPlayhead) |
| 49.5 | Opening a panel from the keyboard: a panel toggle (View > Media Pool / Effects / Edit Index, or their keys) leaves the keyboard focus where it was. Preferences > Keyboard > "Focus the search field when a panel is opened from the keyboard" (off by default, QSettings `ui/focusSearchOnPanelKey`) makes a toggle's shortcut that opens the Media Pool, Effects or Edit Index put the cursor into its search field (text selected); a click on the interface bar or a menu entry does not. No "find in panel" command is added: Ctrl+F stays Viewer Full Screen. | Fixed (new option; bi spec49_5_panelShortcutFocusesTheSearchField). Unverified: no reliable public statement of what Resolve does was found, nor of a find-in-panel command; off is the conservative default. |
| 49.6 | Keyboard-only flows. Ripple delete (Delete / Shift+Backspace) acts on the selected clips, gap, transition or in / out range; Shift+V selects the clip nearest the playhead and X marks the clip under it, so a clip can be ripple deleted without the mouse. With nothing selected the default stays as in 10.5 (nothing happens); Preferences > Editing > "Delete the clips under the playhead on the selected tracks when nothing is selected" (off by default, `edit/rippleDeleteClipAtPlayhead`) ripple deletes the clips under the playhead on the auto-select tracks instead (one undo step). In the Effects library, Enter on the current entry applies it like a double-click (effects onto the selected clips, transitions at the edit, titles / generators at the playhead). | Fixed (new option; bi spec49_6_rippleDeleteClipAtPlayheadOption); Enter already handled (bi spec49_6_enterAppliesTheEffectsLibraryEntry, 5.3). Open: forum posts quoting Resolve's manual give the clip-under-the-playhead fallback for the Speed Editor's ripple delete key, and report it acting on the top track only in Resolve 20; whether the keyboard command does the same could not be confirmed, so 10.5 may need revisiting. A "select all clips under the playhead" command (forum posts name Alt+Shift+V) does not exist here. |
| 49.7 | Guides: View > Guides, rulers dragged out into guides, Ctrl-snapping of a dragged guide, Snap to Guides for on-screen transform drags (match cuts can align on them), presets, safe areas and grids exist on both viewers (section 41). | Already handled (gu dragOutMoveAndRemove, ctrlDragSnapsTheGuide, transformDragSnapsToGuides) |
| 49.8 | Shapes and blur without the Fusion or Color page: Generators > Solid Color (a coloured clip, cropped or masked into a rectangle), titles with a Background box (Text Box preset, 28.5 / title Background options), masks on any clip (section 27) and adjustment clips whose effects act through their masks on everything below (28.2). New: Effects > Region Blur places an adjustment clip with a Gaussian Blur (radius 40 px) and a centred rectangle mask (a third of the frame, 8 px soft edge) at the playhead (double-click, Enter or drag), one undo step; move, size and keyframe the mask in the viewer like any mask (faces, number plates). Once placed it is an ordinary adjustment clip. | Fixed (new; bi spec49_8_regionBlurIsAnAdjustmentClipWithBlurAndMask). Decision: a library entry built from existing pieces instead of a new effect type, so projects stay readable by older builds. |

## 50. Tracker (tracker branch, issue #3)

Tracking on the Compositing page: a point tracker node (50.1 - 50.7), then the planar tracker and linking other nodes to a track (50.8 - 50.13, version 1.2).

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 50.1 | A Tracker node (Transform folder, Select Node list) has an Input, an optional Tracking Source input (only analysed, never drawn or evaluated), Center X / Y (the tracked point), Pattern Size, Search Size (timeline px), Output (Match Move or Stabilize) and a Reference Frame. | Done (test_trackernode descriptor, trackingSourceIsNotEvaluated) |
| 50.2 | Match Move: the input is moved by the tracked point's position at the frame minus its position at the Reference Frame; Stabilize moves it the opposite way. Without a track the node changes nothing. GPU and CPU renders agree. | Done (test_trackernode matchMoveMatrix, matchMoveFollowsTheTrackInTheRender, stabilizeCancelsTheTrack) |
| 50.3 | With the Tracker selected the viewer shows the point, its pattern box and its larger search box; dragging the point or a box corner edits Center X / Y, Pattern Size, Search Size (one undo step per drag, a key at the frame when animated). | Done (viewer overlay; checked by hand, no pixel test) |
| 50.4 | The Inspector of a Tracker has Track Backward, Stop and Track Forward, a progress bar and a status line, and Clear. Tracking starts at the playhead, runs off the UI thread on the pictures of the tracking source (or the input) rendered by the playback engine, moves the playhead along, can be stopped, and stops where the pattern is lost or the clip ends. | Done (test_trackernode runnerFollowsMovingNoise, runnerTracksBackwardsAndCanBeStopped, runnerReportsAFlatPattern; the buttons by hand) |
| 50.5 | The tracked positions are stored as linear keyframes of Center X / Y in one undo step "Track <node>"; a later track replaces the keys it covers and keeps the Reference Frame; Clear removes the keys and leaves the point where it is. Opening another clip while tracking drops the result. | Done (test_trackernode storeTrackKeepsTheReferenceOfAnExistingTrack) |
| 50.6 | The track data survives saving and loading the project (it is ordinary keyframed node parameters). | Done (test_trackernode trackDataSurvivesSaveAndLoad) |
| 50.7 | The point tracker finds a pattern moved by a known sub-pixel offset to within 0.05 px (noise-free) and 0.15 px (noise), tolerates brightness changes, follows a steady movement over 40 frames within 0.4 px, finds a 30 px jump through the coarse levels, and reports a lost or detail-free pattern instead of a wrong position. | Done (test_pointtracker) |
| 50.8 | A Planar Tracker node (Transform folder) has an Input, an optional Tracking Source (analysed only), eight corner parameters (top-left, top-right, bottom-right, bottom-left; X / Y normalised like Center, default the middle half of the frame), Pattern Size (patch side), Search Size (box a patch may move in), Output (Corner Pin, Unsteady, Steady) and a Reference Frame. The viewer shows the quad with four corner handles and its diagonals; dragging a corner edits that corner, dragging inside moves the whole region (one undo step per drag). | Done (test_planartrackernode descriptor; test_compositingpage planarViewerCornersAreDragged) |
| 50.9 | The Planar Tracker's Inspector has the same Track Backward / Stop / Track Forward, progress and Clear as the Tracker. Tracking runs off the UI thread on the tracking source (or the input), moves the playhead along, stops where the surface is lost, and stores the four corners as linear keyframes in one undo step; a later track keeps the Reference Frame; Clear keeps the corners where they are. | Done (test_planartrackernode storeAndClearTheCorners, runnerFollowsATurningScalingPlane; test_compositingpage planarPanelTracksCornersIntoOneUndoStep) |
| 50.10 | Planar Tracker outputs: Corner Pin pins the input's four corners onto the tracked corners (perspective); Unsteady moves, turns and scales the input like the plane did since the Reference Frame (best-fit similarity of the four corners); Steady does the opposite. GPU and CPU renders agree. | Done (test_planartrackernode cornerPinMapsTheFrameOntoTheCorners, unsteadyFollowsAndSteadyCancelsTheMovement, cornerPinShowsTheInputInsideTheQuadOnly, cornerPinFollowsTheTrackedCorners, gpuAndCpuAgreeOnAPerspectivePin) |
| 50.11 | The plane is followed by a grid of patches matched with the point tracker's matcher, each patch warped by the previous frame's homography; RANSAC + normalised DLT turn the matches into a homography, then a second, stricter pass at a small radius refines it. Synthetic planes moved, turned, scaled and tilted by known homographies are recovered to 0.5 px (1 px with noise and a contrast change, 0.8 px with an occluder over a fifth of the region), forwards and backwards; a wrong picture loses the plane and a flat or degenerate region is refused, without crashing. | Done (test_homography, test_planartracker) |
| 50.12 | A node with a Center X / Y (Transform, Merge, Text, shape masks, ...; not the trackers) shows "Follow Track" in its Inspector: pick a Tracker or Planar Tracker that has a track and press Connect. The track (a planar one: the centre of its region) is copied into the node's Center keys at the track's frames in one undo step, with an offset that keeps the node where it is at the playhead; the node's old Center keys are replaced. There are no live links: re-connect after re-tracking. | Done (test_planartrackernode anotherNodeFollowsAPointTrack, aNodeFollowsThePlanarTrackCentre; test_compositingpage followTrackConnectsAnotherNodeInOneUndoStep) |
| 50.13 | Projects saved before the planar tracker load unchanged (the node and its keys are ordinary node data); a Planar Tracker's corners, mode and settings survive save and load. | Done (test_planartrackernode planarTrackDataSurvivesSaveAndLoad) |

## 51. OpenTimelineIO interchange (otio branch, issue #5)

File > Export > Timeline as OpenTimelineIO (.otio) and File > Import > Timeline write and read
the open interchange format of DaVinci Resolve and other editors. A .otio file is plain JSON;
no OpenTimelineIO library is used (`src/export/Otio.h`).

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 51.1 | File > Export > Timeline as OpenTimelineIO (.otio) writes the active timeline as Timeline.1 / Stack.1 / Track.1 (Video, Audio; V1 and A1 first; subtitle tracks are left out). Clips are Clip.2 (media_references with DEFAULT_MEDIA, active_media_reference_key) with source_range (source time, then length on the track), ExternalReference.1 (target_url "file:///...", available_range = the whole file, starting at the media's start timecode) or MissingReference.1 (titles, solids, generators, compound clips, media gone from the pool), enabled, clip markers as Marker.2; gaps are Gap.1; a transition between two touching clips is Transition.1 (SMPTE_Dissolve, in_offset / out_offset = frames before / after the cut; other types Custom_Transition with the Kinoklip type in metadata; fades from or to nothing are not written); a clip at another constant speed has LinearTimeWarp.1 (time_scalar), a freeze FreezeFrame.1, a ramp its average speed; timeline markers are Marker.2 on the Stack; global_start_time is the starting timecode. Times are RationalTime.1 at the timeline rate, fractions of a frame allowed. | Done (test_otio exportWritesTheDocumentedShape, roundTripKeepsEverything). Decision: source_range.duration is the length on the track and time_scalar scales the source consumed, so a clip at 200 % consumes twice its duration; not checked against a file written by Resolve. Clip.1 can be written with `ExportOptions::legacyClipSchema`; the menu writes Clip.2 like current OpenTimelineIO. |
| 51.2 | The importer reads Timeline.1 (the first one of a SerializableCollection.1), Stack.1, Track.1 (kind Video or Audio), Clip.1 (media_reference) and Clip.2 (media_references with active_media_reference_key), Gap.1, Transition.1, ExternalReference.1, MissingReference.1, RationalTime.1, TimeRange.1, Marker.1 / Marker.2 and LinearTimeWarp.1 / FreezeFrame.1, whatever the version number of a schema. Rates 23.976, 24, 25, 29.97, 30, 59.94 and others are read exactly (NTSC rates snap to n x 1000 / 1001); the timeline rate is the one of global_start_time, else of the gaps, else of the clips. A clip whose media rate differs from the timeline's (24 fps media on a 25 fps timeline) keeps its length in seconds. Source in is relative to the reference's available_range start. A transition after a gap or before one becomes a fade; between clips it goes on the incoming clip (alignment from in_offset / out_offset). | Done (test_otio clip1AtTwentyFourFrames, clip2AtTwentyFiveFramesWithSpeed, ntscAndThirtyFrameRates, mediaRateDiffersFromTimelineRate, fadeNextToAGap, markersOnTheStackAndOnClips, rateSnapping) |
| 51.3 | Everything else is skipped without failing the import and listed in the message after it, one line per kind with a count: other effects ("Effect.1 (2 times)"), tracks of another kind, nested stacks and unknown schemas (their length stays as a gap), image sequence and generator references (the clip is offline), transition types other than SMPTE_Dissolve (imported as cross dissolves), metadata of objects (Kinoklip's own "kinoklip" metadata key is not reported). A file that is not JSON or not a timeline is refused with a message and changes nothing. | Done (test_otio unknownThingsAreSkippedAndReported, metadataIsReported, notOtioIsRefused; test_timelineimport aFileThatIsNotOtioChangesNothing) |
| 51.4 | Round trip: a timeline with several video and audio tracks, gaps, a cross dissolve, clips at 50 %, reverse and freeze, a disabled clip, clip and timeline markers (their exact colours kept in the file's metadata), track names, a muted track and a starting timecode, exported and imported, has the same clip positions, lengths, source times (to the microsecond), speeds, names, transitions and markers at 25, 29.97 and 24 fps. | Done (test_otio roundTripKeepsEverything) |
| 51.5 | File > Import > Timeline asks for a .otio file and adds a timeline (the file's name when the file has none, numbered if the name is taken) in the bin the media pool shows, then opens it. The timeline has the project's picture size; it follows the project's frame rate, or keeps the file's rate when that differs. Files named by target_url (file:// URLs, plain or relative paths; relative ones against the .otio file's folder) go through the media pool import (a file already in the pool is reused, no second item); a missing file or a clip without a file becomes an offline pool item with the same path, so Relink Offline Media works as for a project. The timeline and the media it added are one undo step "Import Timeline". The message lists missing files and what was skipped (51.3). | Done (test_timelineimport importsExistingAndMissingMediaInOneUndoStep, aSecondImportWithTheSameNameGetsANumber, exportedTimelineImportsBack, theActionsExist). Decision: single-file projects get linked media from an import (no copy into the project file); remote (http) targets are offline. |

## 52. Clip audio effects (audio-clip-effects branch, owner request for 1.2)

Audio clips carry an effect stack like video clips do (`ClipData::effects`, the same
`EffectInstance` model and the same undo, save / load and Inspector code). This supersedes 37.33
for the effects listed here (reverb, delay); de-esser, noise reduction and pitch stay deferred.

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 52.1 | The Effects library has an Audio Effects section: Gain, Equalizer (low shelf, mid bell, high shelf), Compressor, Limiter, High-pass Filter, Low-pass Filter, Delay, Reverb. In the Inspector's Effects tab of an audio clip, Add Effect is enabled and lists these only (video clips keep listing video effects only). | Done (test_audioclipeffectsui inspectorEnablesAddEffectForAnAudioClip, test_audioclipeffects registryHasTheAudioEffects) |
| 52.2 | A linked video + audio pair shows two stacks in the Effects tab, "Video Effects" (the video clip) and "Audio Effects" (the audio clip), each with its own Add Effect. Double-clicking an audio effect in the library with the video clip of a pair selected puts it on the audio part. Dropping a library audio effect on an audio clip of the timeline or on the Inspector works as for video effects. | Done (test_audioclipeffectsui linkedPairHasSeparateVideoAndAudioStacks, libraryActivateReachesTheLinkedAudio) |
| 52.3 | Adding, editing a parameter, bypassing (the check box of the group), reordering and removing an audio effect are undoable steps, as for video effects; a video effect is refused by an audio clip and an audio effect by a video clip. Audio effect parameters are plain values (no keyframes). | Done (test_audioclipeffectsui applyUndoRedoAndRefusals, inspectorEditsBypassesReordersAndRemoves, linkedPairAudioStackEdits) |
| 52.4 | Processing order of an audio clip: source (retimed if it is) -> the clip's effect stack, in list order -> clip volume, fades, transitions and pan -> the track's channel strip (EQ, dynamics, pan, fader) -> buses -> master soft clipper. Playback, export and the nested preview all go through `AudioMixer::mix`, so they give the same samples; effect memory (filters, delay, reverb) restarts when a clip is not read contiguously (seek, loop, new export) and is cleared when an effect is bypassed. A reverb or delay tail ends with the clip. Gain -6 dB halves the amplitude, a high-pass removes DC, a bypassed effect leaves the signal bit for bit, mixing in blocks of any size equals one long mix. | Done (test_audioclipeffects gainMinus6dBHalvesTheAmplitude, highPassRemovesDc, bypassRestoresTheOriginal, clipEffectsRunBeforeTheClipVolume, stackOrderMatters, playbackBlocksGiveTheSamplesOfAnExport, aSeekRestartsTheEffectMemory, and one test per effect) |
| 52.5 | The stack is saved with the clip (the existing `effects` array, no format change; projects without it load as before) and loads back unchanged. Paste Attributes has an Effects switch under Audio Attributes that copies the stack onto audio clips (fresh effect ids). | Done (test_audioclipeffectsui stackSurvivesSaveAndLoad; test_parity_edit spec51_5_pasteAttributesCopiesAudioEffects) |

## 53. Command line and command layer (cli branch, version 1.2)

`kinoklip <command> --option value ...` drives Kinoklip without a window. The commands are one
small layer (`src/app/commands/`, described by name, typed parameters and a description, result
as JSON or an error); the command line (`src/app/CliRunner.h`) is one front end of it, and an
MCP server will be another. Nothing is sent anywhere.

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 53.1 | The first argument decides: `kinoklip commands`, `help`, or the first word of a command (`project`, `media`, `timeline`, `clip`, `marker`, `transition`, `export`, `import`, `render`, `version`) runs headless on Qt's offscreen platform (no display needed, whatever QT_QPA_PLATFORM says); anything else, including a project file name or an existing file called like a command, starts the window as before. `--help`, `--version`, `--render`, `--render-frame`, `--screenshot` and the other options are unchanged. | Done (test_cli versionAndExistingOptionsKeepWorking) |
| 53.2 | A command is "group verb" (`clip add`) or one word (`render`, `version`). Options are `--name value` or `--name=value`; a boolean is a bare flag (`--ripple`), or `--enabled false`; a list option is repeated (`--file a --file b`); a value may start with `-` (`--delta -8`). `--json` prints `{"ok":true,"result":{...}}` or `{"ok":false,"error":{"code","message"}}` on one line; without it a short sentence or table. Exit code 0 on success, 1 when the command failed, 2 for a wrong command line (unknown command or option, missing or bad value); the message is on stderr. Progress of a long command (render) goes to stderr. | Done (test_cli errorsExitNonZeroWithMessageOnStderr, projectMediaClipRoundTrip) |
| 53.3 | `kinoklip commands --json` lists every command with its description and parameters (name, type, description, required, default, choices) and whether it changes the project; `kinoklip help <command>` prints the usage of one. | Done (test_cli commandsJsonListsEveryCommand; test_commands registryListsEveryCommandWithParameters) |
| 53.4 | Commands work on the project file given with `--project`. A command that changes the project saves it there, or to `--output` leaving the original alone. The arguments are checked first (unknown option, missing required option, wrong type, value outside the listed choices), then the project is loaded; a missing or unreadable file is an error and nothing is written. `--timeline` (id or name) addresses another timeline for one call and does not change the project's active timeline; only `timeline select` does. | Done (test_commands argumentChecking, outputLeavesTheProjectUntouched, timelines) |
| 53.5 | Times are timeline frames ("125") or timecode ("00:00:05:10") counted from the start of the timeline, parsed with the timecode helpers of the timeline; a leading `-` makes a time negative. Text that is neither is the error `bad_time`. | Done (test_commands clipAddAndList, clipMoveTrimSplitDelete) |
| 53.6 | `project new` creates a project file (name, picture size, frame rate; 29.97 and the other NTSC rates become n x 1000 / 1001; V1 and A1; starting timecode 01:00:00:00 as in the application) and refuses to overwrite a file without `--force`. `project info` reports settings, timelines and media count. | Done (test_commands projectNewAndInfo) |
| 53.7 | `media import` adds files to the media pool in one undo step (a file that cannot be read or is already in the pool is a warning; if nothing was imported it is an error); `media list` shows id, name, path, duration and whether the file is offline; `media relink` searches a folder by file name (as Relink Offline Media) or points one item at a file. | Done (test_commands mediaImportListRelink) |
| 53.8 | `timeline list`, `timeline new` (name, number of video and audio tracks; not activated) and `timeline select` (saved as the project's active timeline). | Done (test_commands timelines) |
| 53.9 | `clip list` shows every clip with id, track (V1, A2), start and duration (frames and timecode), source in and out (microseconds), media id, speed, enabled, volume and transform. `clip add` places a media pool item (id, unique name or file path) on a track: after the last clip of the track, or at `--at` (overwriting; `--insert` pushes later clips right on every unlocked track, like the Edit page); a video track also receives the sound on the same-numbered audio track, linked; `--source-in` and `--source-out` pick part of the media. The clips come from the same function as dragging a file onto the timeline (`TimelineController::makeMediaClips`). | Done (test_commands clipAddAndList) |
| 53.10 | `clip move` (new start and/or a track of the same kind; linked clips follow in time), `clip trim` (head or tail by a signed number of frames, optional `--ripple`; linked clips with an edge on the same frame follow; refused with `out_of_range` when the edge cannot move, a change limited by the media or a neighbour is reported as clamped), `clip split` (one clip, strictly inside it, or every clip under the time on the auto-selected tracks), `clip delete` (several ids; the gap stays, `--ripple` closes it on all tracks) all use EditOps; `--unlinked` leaves the linked clips alone. Locked tracks refuse the change. Each is one undo step and keeps the timeline invariants. | Done (test_commands clipMoveTrimSplitDelete, clipSplitAndDelete) |
| 53.11 | `clip set` changes speed (0.01 to 100, linked clips follow, the clip length follows as Change Clip Speed does), volume in dB (-96 to 24), enabled, name, zoom, position x / y, rotation and opacity (0 to 100); a value outside its range is `out_of_range`, no option at all is `missing_parameter`. | Done (test_commands clipSet) |
| 53.12 | `marker add` (time, name, colour from the marker palette, note, length), `marker list`, `marker delete` work on the timeline markers, kept sorted; two markers on one frame are refused. `transition add` puts a video transition (default Cross Dissolve) or, on an audio clip, an audio transition (default Cross Fade +3 dB) at the head or tail of a clip, shortened to fit like the UI does; a transition of the wrong kind or an unknown type is refused. | Done (test_commands markers, transitions) |
| 53.13 | `export otio` and `export edl` write the same text as File > Export; `import otio` adds the file as a new timeline (and its media) in one undo step, as File > Import > Timeline. | Done (test_commands exportAndImportOtio) |
| 53.14 | `render` renders the timeline to a file with a Deliver preset (`--preset`, ids from resources/export/presets.json) or the settings of the file extension, through the pipeline of Deliver and `--render` (`renderProject()`); progress (fraction, frames, speed) goes to stderr about once a second and the result (frames, seconds, encoder, files) at the end. An empty timeline, an unknown preset or an unknown extension is an error. The project file is not changed. | Done (test_commands renderAudioAndVideo) |
| 53.15 | Single-file (packed) projects are not opened by the commands yet; `--project` takes a plain project file. | Decision |
| 53.16 | `effect add` puts a video effect on a video clip or an audio effect on an audio clip (ids from `effect list`, through the function behind the Effects library), with optional parameters (`--set key=value`, repeatable); `effect stack` lists the effects of a clip in order with instance id, state and parameter values; `effect set` changes parameters of one instance (`--key` and `--value`, and/or `--set`); `effect remove`; `effect enable --enabled false` switches one off. A value is checked against the descriptor of the parameter before anything is edited: a number inside its range (`out_of_range`), a whole number for an integer parameter, true / false, one of the choices, a colour (#rrggbb or a name), an existing file (`file_not_found`); unknown keys are `unknown_parameter` and list the valid ones. An effect of the wrong kind for the track, an unknown id or instance, or a locked track is refused and leaves the project as it was. Supersedes the last sentence of 55.7. | Done (test_commands effectsOnAClip) |
| 53.17 | `transition set` changes the transition at the head or tail of a clip: its length (clamped to what fits, the result says so), its alignment on the cut (`centre`, `start-on-cut`, `end-on-cut`) and its parameters by key; a tail that touches the next clip finds the cut's transition stored on that clip. `transition remove` removes it (the function behind the timeline's Remove Transition). No transition there is `no_transition`. | Done (test_commands transitionSetAndRemove) |
| 53.18 | `generator add` (solid colour, gradients, bars, adjustment clip, ... from `effect list --category generator`), `title add` (text, font, size, colour, bold, italic, alignment and text position; a title preset with `--preset`) and `title set` (the same options on an existing title clip) place a clip at `--at` for `--duration` on `--track` (V1, V2, ...; default the lowest track above everything in that range, a new one if needed; anything under it on a named track is overwritten) with the same defaults as double-clicking the entry in the Effects library (duration from Preferences > Editing). Parameters are checked as in 53.16 and applied before the clip is placed, so the creation is one undo step. A locked or missing track, a negative start or a zero duration is refused. | Done (test_commands generatorsAndTitles) |
| 53.19 | `keyframe set` puts a value at a time on an animatable number of a clip (`--param` a key such as opacity, posX, zoomX, rotation, cropL, volumeDb, pan; `--effect` the instance id for an effect's parameter; `--interp` linear, easeIn, easeOut, easeInOut, smooth or hold), adding the key or changing the one at that time, through ParamAccess like the Inspector's diamond. The time is a timeline time inside the clip. `keyframe list` shows the keyed parameters of a clip with time, value and interpolation (optionally one parameter); `keyframe delete` removes the key at a time (`no_keyframe` when there is none; the parameter keeps the value of the last key removed). A parameter that cannot be keyframed is `not_animatable`. | Done (test_commands keyframes) |
| 53.20 | `track list` shows every video and audio track with its state and clip count; `track add --type video` or `audio` adds an empty track on top; `track set` changes enabled, locked, muted, solo, auto-select and the name of one track (V1, A2). A track that does not exist is `unknown_track`. | Done (test_commands tracks) |
| 53.21 | `clip set` also takes `--crop-left`, `--crop-right`, `--crop-top`, `--crop-bottom` (pixels, 0 to 10000) and `--pan` (-1 to 1), with the ranges of the Inspector. | Done (test_commands clipSetCropAndPan) |
| 53.22 | `session info` says whether the commands run on project files (`file`) or on the project open in a window (`live`, 55.9) and which project that is. `project save` saves the open project through the window's own Save in live mode (a project that has no file yet is `no_file`); in file mode it saves a copy to `--output`. `--live` after any command on the command line runs it in the open window (exit 1 with the reason when no window accepts local control). | Done (test_livecontrol sessionInfoTellsTheMode, projectSaveUsesTheWindowsSave, theCommandLineForwardsWithLive) |

## 54. Video transition folders (transitions branch, owner request for 1.2)

The Effects library lists the video transitions in folders, as Resolve does: Dissolve, Iris,
Motion, Shape, Wipe. The pictures are drawn by the GPU compositor, which the viewer, playback and
export all use (one `Compositor`); the CPU fallback (no OpenGL) draws a plain cross dissolve for
every transition. (Section 52 belongs to another branch; this is the next free one.)

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 54.1 | Video Transitions has the folders Dissolve (Cross Dissolve, Dip to Color, Additive Dissolve, Blur Dissolve, Non-Additive Dissolve, Smooth Cut), Iris (Arrow, Cross, Diamond, Eye, Hexagon, Oval, Pentagon, Square, Triangle), Motion (Push, Barn Door, Slide, Split), Shape (Box, Heart, Star, Triangle Left, Triangle Right) and Wipe (Wipe, Band, Center, Clock, Radial, Spiral, Venetian Blind, X), sorted by name; ids and parameters are in `core/BuiltinTransitions.cpp`. They drag onto cuts and double-click like the older four. | Done (test_transitions registry, foldersOfTheTransitions) |
| 54.2 | Smooth Cut morphs between the outgoing and the incoming picture of every frame of the transition with the optical-flow interpolator of Retime (`GlRetime`, one implementation), at the transition's progress: a block that moved between the two pictures is drawn between its two places, not at both. At progress 0 it is the outgoing picture, at 1 the incoming one. Limits: motion of about 30 px at 640 px width (it scales with the picture); beyond that, and where a side is missing (fade at a track end) or the interpolator failed to build, it is a plain dissolve; no Faster mode; the CPU fallback draws a plain dissolve. | Done (test_transitions smoothCutFindsTheMovedBlock, cpuFallbackIsADissolve) |
| 54.3 | Additive Dissolve adds the pictures (each at full strength from its half on), Non-Additive Dissolve keeps the brighter of the two per channel, Blur Dissolve blurs both with a disc that is widest in the middle (Horizontal / Vertical Strength, % of the picture). All three have Start Ratio and End Ratio (share of the incoming picture at the start and the end of the transition, default 0 and 100). Cross Dissolve and Dip to Color keep their old parameters. | Done (test_transitions renders) |
| 54.4 | Iris, Shape and Wipe transitions share one shader: the edge sweeps across the picture, the outgoing picture on one side, the incoming on the other. Border Color and Border (px) draw a line along the edge, Feather (%) softens it, Reverse runs it the other way round (an iris closes). Shapes have Offset X / Y (% of the picture), Rotation, Aspect Ratio (Oval Ratio for the oval) and presets (Arrow Up/Down/Left/Right, Hexagon / Hexagon Vertical, Pentagon Up/Down, Square Flat/Point, Triangle Up/Bottom/Left/Right, Star 4/5/6 points); Box has eight modes (four corners, four edge middles). Wipes: Band (Horizontal, Vertical and their Bilinear forms, Bands), Center (Angle), Clock (Angle, Clockwise), Radial (half a turn round the bottom centre), Spiral, Venetian Blind (Repeat, Angle), X. The plain Wipe and Push keep their parameters. Border width is measured against the half diagonal, so it is approximate on a shape. | Done (test_transitions renders, shapesAlmostCoverTheFrameLate) |
| 54.5 | Motion: Barn Door (Vertical / Horizontal, Reverse closes it over the outgoing picture), Slide (Direction, Mode Slide In over the outgoing picture or Slide Out off the incoming one), Split (the two halves of the picture move apart in opposite directions, each followed by the incoming picture; Horizontal / Vertical, Reverse). Motion Blur (%) averages eight samples of the movement; it is nothing at the first and last frame. | Done (test_transitions renders) |
| 54.6 | Every new transition saves and loads with its settings; projects from older versions load unchanged (the old ids and parameters are untouched, an unknown id still renders as a cross dissolve). | Done (test_transitions saveAndLoad) |
| 54.7 | Fusion Transitions (designed from one-line descriptions, not from any application): Brightness Flash, Zoom In, Zoom In and Out, Crash Zoom, Rotate (half turn), Rotate 90, Noise Dissolve (Grain Size, Softness), Glitch, Camera Shake (Shake Speed, Strength), Circles (Rings), Flip 3D, Slice Push (Slices), Warp, Drop Warp, Tunnel of Light (Glow Gain, Glow Size); Resolve FX Transitions: Burn Away (Angle, Raggedness, Glow Brightness, Char). Each starts as the outgoing and ends as the incoming picture. Not done: Fall and Bounce, Film Strip, Foreground Wipe, Paint On, Round and Down, Pan and Slide x4 (Push / Slide with Motion Blur cover them), Burn Away's hotspot and path modes, DCTL Transition (proprietary). | Done (test_transitions renders) |
| 54.8 | Cross Dissolve, Dip to Color, Additive, Non-Additive and Blur Dissolve have Start Ratio, End Ratio and Reverse (Reverse plays the ratio curve from the other end: start and end become 100 minus end and 100 minus start). Wipe has Border Color, Border and Reverse; Push, Barn Door, Slide and Split have Border Color, Border and Feather (the border is drawn on the staying picture along the moving edge and swells and shrinks with the progress, so the first and last picture stay clean). New parameters default to what the transition always did: a project saved before them loads (they are simply absent) and renders the same. | Done (test_transitions oldProjectsRenderTheSame, dissolveRatios, borderColourShowsAtTheEdge, crossDissolveIsTheAverage) |
| 54.9 | The rest of the Fusion Transitions, designed from one-line descriptions: Slide Left / Right / Up / Down (the next clip slides in over the current one, with a soft shadow; Curve Linear / Easing / Custom, Custom Curve), Pan Left / Right / Up / Down (both pictures move as one strip with a motion blur that peaks in the middle; Motion Blur, Curve), Fall and Bounce (Fall Angle), Film Strip (Film Color, Frame Size, Curve), Foreground Wipe (Invert Wipe, Shadow Softness, Border Width, Border Color), Paint On (Strokes, Shadow Blend), Round and Down (Angle, Pivot X / Y, Curve). With them every transition of the reference list is in except DCTL Transition (a framework for the proprietary DCTL language, not a fixed effect) and Burn Away's hotspot and path modes; this supersedes the "Not done" list of 54.7. | Done (test_transitions registry, renders, shapesAlmostCoverTheFrameLate) |
| 54.10 | The GPU renderer builds a transition's shader program the first time the transition is drawn, and an effect's the first time it is used (on the first frame that uses it, a one-off hitch of a few milliseconds); at start only Cross Dissolve, the fallback, is built. A program that fails to build is reported once and the transition is drawn as the dissolve from then on (an effect is skipped), as before. | Done (test_transitions shadersBuildWhenFirstUsed) |
| 54.11 | Drawn directly at progress 0 a transition is the outgoing picture and at progress 1 the incoming one (mean difference below 1 of 255 per channel, no channel more than 40 off), flashes and glitches included. | Done (test_transitions endpointsAreExact) |
| 54.12 | OpenTimelineIO export writes every transition as a Transition (SMPTE_Dissolve for a Cross Dissolve, Custom_Transition for all others) with its id and settings in `metadata.kinoklip` (`type`, `params`); import reads them back. A transition from another editor, or one with an id Kinoklip does not know, is a cross dissolve and is reported. | Done (test_otio everyTransitionRoundTrips, unknownCustomTransitionStaysACrossDissolve) |
| 54.13 | `kk_transitionsheets` (tests/tools, built with the tests, not shipped) writes a contact sheet per folder of every transition at progress 0.25, 0.5 and 0.75 between two test pictures, for judging the look by eye. | Done (manual) |

## 55. MCP server (mcp branch, owner request for 1.2)

`kinoklip mcp` is a Model Context Protocol server on standard input and output, so an AI assistant
can control Kinoklip with the commands of section 53. It is local: no network, nothing
sent anywhere (a socket on this computer only when the owner switches on live control, 55.8). Code: `src/app/mcp/` and, for the open window, `src/app/live/`.

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 55.1 | `kinoklip mcp` reads one JSON-RPC 2.0 message per line on stdin and writes one line per reply on stdout, until its input closes (exit code 0). Standard output carries protocol messages only: descriptor 1 is pointed at standard error while serving and the protocol writes to a private copy of the real stdout. Log lines go to stderr. An unknown option is exit code 2; `kinoklip mcp --help` prints the usage. | Done (test_cli mcpServerAnswersOnStdio, mcpRejectsOptionsAndHasHelp) |
| 55.2 | `initialize` answers with the client's protocol version when it is 2025-06-18 or 2024-11-05, else with 2025-06-18; capabilities `tools`; server info (name `kinoklip`, version); short instructions for the model. `notifications/initialized` and other notifications get no reply, `ping` answers `{}`. Methods not implemented: error -32601; malformed JSON: -32700 with id null; a message that is not a JSON-RPC request: -32600; unknown tool or arguments that are not an object: -32602. A batch (2024-11-05) is answered with an array. | Done (test_mcp initializeNegotiatesTheVersion, protocolErrors) |
| 55.3 | `tools/list` has one tool per registered command, named like the command with `_` for `.` (`clip_add`), with its description, an `inputSchema` (object; every parameter a property with its JSON type and description, `required`, `enum` for choices, a Time is a string described as "frames or timecode", a list an array of strings, no additional properties; the universal parameters `project`, `output`, `timeline`, `force` included) and `readOnlyHint`. A new command is a new tool without a change in the server. | Done (test_mcp toolListCoversEveryCommandWithAValidSchema) |
| 55.4 | `tools/call` runs `CommandRegistry::execute()`. Success: content with the sentence and the JSON result as text, and `structuredContent` with the JSON (protocol 2025-06-18 only). A command error is a normal result with `isError: true`, the text `code: message` and the stable error code (as `{"ok":false,"error":{"code","message"}}` in `structuredContent`). Progress of a long command (render) goes out as `notifications/progress` (increasing `progress` of `total` 1) only when the request carried `_meta.progressToken`. | Done (test_mcp aCallBuildsAProjectAndReadsItBack, aFailingCallIsAnErrorResultWithTheCode, oldProtocolHasNoStructuredContent, progressGoesOutOnlyWithAToken) |
| 55.5 | `frame export` renders one timeline frame (`--at`, default 0) to a PNG file (`--file`, must end in .png; `--width` 16 to 8192 keeps the aspect ratio, default the timeline size) through the compositor of `--render-frame`; the project is not changed. A frame outside the timeline is `out_of_range`, an empty timeline `empty_timeline`. | Done (test_commands frameExportViewAndTheCatalogue) |
| 55.6 | `frame view` returns the same picture in `CommandResult::image` (width default 1024, never wider than the timeline); the command line prints its size only, MCP sends it as an `image` content item (base64 PNG, `image/png`) next to the text. | Done (test_commands frameExportViewAndTheCatalogue; test_mcp aFrameComesBackAsImageContent) |
| 55.7 | `transition list` and `effect list` (`--category video, audio, generator, title`) list the ids, names, categories and Effects library folders of the catalogue without a project; `--id` gives one entry with all its parameters (key, type, range, default, unit, choices). An id of another kind is `unknown_effect`. Applying effects to clips is not a command yet. | Done (test_commands frameExportViewAndTheCatalogue; test_mcp aCallBuildsAProjectAndReadsItBack) |
| 55.8 | Live control of the open window is a preference, **off by default** (Preferences > System > Local Control: "Let local tools control Kinoklip (MCP)", with a sentence saying that programs of this user can edit the open project, each change one undo step, nobody else can connect, nothing on the network). While it is off nothing listens and a connection is refused. Supersedes "no socket" in the introduction of this section: with the option on, the window listens on a local (Unix) socket, never on the network. | Done (test_livecontrol offByDefaultAndNothingListens, switchingTheOptionOnAndOff) |
| 55.9 | The socket is `kinoklip-control-<uid>.sock` in `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` (else the temp folder), created with `QLocalServer::UserAccessOption` (mode 0700, no access for group or others); a client refuses a socket file that another user owns and the window drops a connection whose peer user is not the current one (SO_PEERCRED). `KK_CONTROL_SOCKET` names another path (tests). A socket file left by a crashed run is replaced; a second window does not take over a socket that answers (its option stays on and it logs why). | Done (test_livecontrol theSocketIsOnlyForThisUser, aLeftoverSocketFileIsReplacedButALiveOneIsNot) |
| 55.10 | `kinoklip mcp` asks for the window at every tools/list and tools/call (a window opened or closed meanwhile is noticed): when one accepts, the tool calls are sent to it over the socket (a private protocol of JSON lines: hello with a protocol version; call with command and arguments; progress; result with data, text, picture, or error code and message) and `project` is optional in every tool schema; otherwise the server works on project files exactly as before. `kinoklip mcp --file` always works on files. A window that closes during a call is `live_disconnected` (the call may have happened). | Done (test_livecontrol mcpForwardsToTheWindowAndFileModeStillWorks) |
| 55.11 | A live call runs on the window's thread through `CommandRegistry::executeLive()`, the same handlers as in file mode, against the window's own project, so the edit shows at once and is **one undo step** ("Assistant: clip add"), whatever number of edits the command makes; Edit > Undo restores the project. A failing command is rolled back and leaves no step; a step inside the call that changes nothing does not cancel the steps before it. No file is loaded or saved per call (`project save` saves through the window's Save). `project`, when given, must be the open project (`wrong_project`, the message names the open one; an unsaved project is named as such); `output` is `live_unsupported`; a window in the middle of an edit of its own (a drag, a field) answers `window_busy` instead of swallowing it. `project new` still creates a file and leaves the window alone. | Done (test_livecontrol aCallChangesTheOpenProjectAsOneUndoStep, aNoOpInsideACallDoesNotUndoTheRestOfTheCall, aFailedCallLeavesNothing, aWrongProjectIsRefused, anUnsavedProjectIsNamedAndCannotBeSavedByTheTool, aBusyWindowRefusesInsteadOfSwallowingTheEdit) |
| 55.12 | `render`, `frame export` and `frame view` do not block the window in live mode: the window takes a copy of the project document on its own thread and the command runs on that copy in a worker thread, answering (and sending progress) when done; the open project is not touched and a render is no undo step. | Done (test_livecontrol aFrameIsRenderedOffTheGuiThread) |
| 55.13 | While a local tool is connected the interface bar shows "Assistant connected" (tooltip: it can change the project, each change is one undo step, switch it off in Preferences). | Done (test_livecontrol theIndicatorShowsWhileAClientIsConnected) |
| 55.14 | The tools `session_info` (mode and open project) and `project_save` are part of the list; the instructions sent to the model say to call `session_info` first. Only one window at a time offers local control. | Done (test_livecontrol sessionInfoTellsTheMode, mcpForwardsToTheWindowAndFileModeStillWorks) |

## 56. Generators, test patterns and effects of the 1.2 gap list (effects-a branch, owner request for 1.2)

The Effects library gains the generators, the Effects-section templates and the effects that were
missing against the usual library. All look and behaviour were written from public standards or from
one-line descriptions, not from any application. Generators and effects draw on the GPU compositor
(viewer, playback and export); the CPU fallback draws every new generator and the pointwise
effects (Despill, Gamut Limiter, False Color); the spatial effects have no CPU version, as before.
Tests: `tests/render/test_effectsgap.cpp`; contact sheets: `tests/render/test_effectsheets.cpp`.

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 56.1 | Toolbox > Generators has nine more entries: 100mV Steps, EBU Color Bar, BT.2111 Color Bar HLG Narrow / PQ Narrow / PQ Full, Window, YCbCr Ramp, Noise Gradient, Grid. Each has parameters that can be keyframed where a number can move (not the integer counts, seeds and choices) and saves and loads with the project. | Done (test_effectsgap generatorsAreRegistered, projectRoundTrip) |
| 56.2 | EBU Color Bar draws eight vertical bars, left to right white, yellow, cyan, green, magenta, red, blue, black: white at 100 % (255), the colours at 75 % (191), black 0 (EBU Tech 3213, ITU-R BT.471 "100/0/75/0"). | Done (test_effectsgap ebuBars) |
| 56.3 | 100mV Steps is a grey ramp in eight steps of 100 mV of a 700 mV signal: level n/7 of full scale for n = 0..7 (the Grey Scale formula with eight steps); Orientation horizontal or vertical. | Done (test_effectsgap steps100mV) |
| 56.4 | The BT.2111 generators draw the layout of ITU-R BT.2111-3 on any frame size (reference 1920 x 1080 scaled: side columns 240 wide, seven bars 206 / 204 wide, rows of 90, 540, 90, 90 and 270 pixels): 40 % grey sides; 100 % bars over 75 % (HLG) or 58 % (PQ) bars; the stair (-7 % and 0 to 109 % in narrow range, 0 to 100 % in PQ full range); the ramp with the widths of Tables 5 and 6; the black levels 0, -2, 0, +2, 0, +4 %; the 75 % / 58 % white block; the six Rec. 709 colour bars in the bottom corners. Code values are the 10-bit values of Tables 2, 3 and 4. A Kinoklip picture is full range 0 - 1, so narrow range codes are drawn as (code - 64) / 876 (721 = 75 %) and codes below 64 or above 940 clip to 0 and 1; PQ full range is code / 1023. | Done (test_effectsgap bt2111Tables, bt2111Levels, bt2111Layout, bt2111Renders) |
| 56.5 | Window draws a rectangle or an ellipse (Shape) of Width x Height timeline pixels around Center X / Y in the Window Color on the Background colour; Softness (px) turns the edge into a ramp of that width centred on the shape's edge. | Done (test_effectsgap windowGenerator) |
| 56.6 | YCbCr Ramp draws ramps of Y' (grey 0..1), Cb and Cr (-0.5..0.5 at Y' = 0.5) converted with the Rec. 709 coefficients (Kr 0.2126, Kb 0.0722, full range) and clipped to 0..1; Channel all (three bands stacked across the other axis), y, cb or cr; Orientation. | Done (test_effectsgap ycbcrRamp) |
| 56.7 | Noise Gradient is fractal value noise (Scale px, Detail 1 - 8 layers each twice as fine and half as strong, Seed) blended from Color 1 to Color 2 with Contrast (percent about the middle); Evolution is the third axis of the noise and can be keyframed. Smooth (neighbouring pixels differ little), deterministic, GPU and CPU agree. | Done (test_effectsgap noiseGradientGenerator, gpuMatchesCpu) |
| 56.8 | Grid draws lines every width / Columns and height / Rows pixels over the Background colour at Background Opacity (0 by default: see-through); Line Width and Line Color; every Major Line Every-th line Major Line Width wide (0 = none). Lines are centred on the cell boundaries with one pixel of anti-aliasing. | Done (test_effectsgap gridGenerator) |
| 56.9 | The GPU shader and the CPU raster of every new generator agree to within 1 - 3 levels (single pixels at pattern edges aside) and each draws the same picture twice. | Done (test_effectsgap gpuMatchesCpu) |
| 56.10 | Toolbox > Effects gains Colored Border, Binoculars, Night Vision and Composition Clip next to Adjustment Clip and Region Blur. The first three are adjustment clips that come with their parts: Colored Border with a Colored Border effect, Binoculars with a Levels effect that lowers the output white to 8 of 255, limited by a mask stack (the frame minus two overlapping circles, feathered), Night Vision with Duotone, Film Grain, Scanlines and Vignette; all editable afterwards. Composition Clip is a see-through Solid clip carrying an empty composition (the Compositing page opens it). | Done (test_effectsgap templatesMakeAdjustmentClips, templatesRender) |
| 56.11 | New folders in the effects library: Texture (Fast Noise) and Revival (none yet; reserved, shown once it has an effect). | Done (test_effectsgap effectsAreRegistered) |
| 56.12 | Despill (Color) limits the chosen cast channel (green, blue, red) to the mean or maximum of the other two by Strength; Gamut Limiter (Color) reads the picture as Rec. 709, DCI-P3 D65 or Rec. 2020 primaries with a power-law gamma, converts to the Limit To primaries in linear light and brings out-of-gamut colours inside by moving them toward their luminance (Desaturate) or clipping, leaving colours already inside untouched. GPU matches the CPU reference. | Done (test_effectsgap pointEffectsMatchReference, despillAndGamutMath) |
| 56.13 | Alpha Matte Shrink and Grow (Key) moves the alpha edge by Shrink / Grow px (negative shrinks) and softens it with Edge Blur; the colour is not smeared. Colored Border (Stylize) paints a border inside the picture edge: Color, Width, Corner Radius, Feather, Opacity. Transform (Transform) moves, scales and rotates the layer (Position, Scale %, Rotation clockwise, Pivot, Opacity). | Done (test_effectsgap alphaMatteShrinkGrow, coloredBorder, transformEffect) |
| 56.14 | Dent (Distort) bows the picture around a point: Round Bulge or Pinch, Horizontal or Vertical Bow, Square Bulge or Pinch; Strength, Size, Center, Edges transparent / repeat / mirrored. Fast Noise (Texture) lays Noise Gradient noise over the picture (Color 1 / 2, Scale, Detail, Contrast, Evolution, Drift Speed per second, Seed) with the blend mode Normal, Add, Multiply, Screen or Overlay at Blend percent. | Done (test_effectsgap dentEffect, fastNoiseEffect) |
| 56.15 | Contrast Pop (Color) boosts the difference between a pixel's luma and a wide blur of its surroundings (Strength, Radius), weighted toward the midtones, colour scaled with the luma; Chromatic Aberration Fix (Color) scales the red and blue channels about a centre by Red Scale / Blue Scale percent to line them up, and Fringe Removal pulls the colour toward grey along luma edges. False Color (Color) paints by brightness (exposure bands, thermal, rainbow); Dehaze (Color) uses the dark channel of the picture divided by the Haze Color to estimate and remove haze. | Done (test_effectsgap contrastPopEffect, chromaticAberrationFix, falseColorBands, dehazeEffect) |
| 56.16 | Every new effect leaves the picture unchanged with its neutral parameters (Amount / Strength 0, Opacity 0, Width 0, zero shifts). | Done (test_effectsgap neutralParametersLeavePictureAlone) |

Not in this section (waits for the owner): the colour-management effects (Color Space Transform,
ACES Transform, Gamut Mapping, Chromatic Adaptation, Color Compressor; they wait for the decision
about OpenColorIO) and anything that needs machine learning or DCTL.

## 57. Shader effects of the 1.2 gap list, second part (effects-b branch, owner request for 1.2)

Seventeen more effects for the Effects library, written from their names and from published
methods (bilateral filter, generalised Kuwahara filter, k-means, YCbCr distances), not from any
application. They draw on the GPU compositor (viewer, playback and export); like the other spatial
effects they have no CPU version. Parameters in pixels are timeline pixels (they scale with the
preview size). Anything random (dirt, scratches, tape noise, dither) is a hash of the pixel, the
Seed and the clip-local frame number, so the same frame renders the same way every time.
Tests: `tests/render/test_effectsb.cpp`; contact sheet: `tests/render/test_effectsheets.cpp`
(`effects-b.png`).

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 57.1 | The Effects library gains three folders, Sharpen (Sharpen Edges, Soften and Sharpen), Refine (Beauty) and, now that it has effects, Revival (Deband, Dead Pixel Fixer). Texture gains Texture Pop, Detail Recovery, Analog Damage, Film Damage and JPEG Damage; Stylize gains Watercolor and Stylize; Light gains Aperture Diffraction and Lens Reflections; Color gains Color Palette; Key gains 3D Keyer; Transform gains Video Collage. Every effect has a description, parameters inside their ranges and saves and loads with the project (animatable numbers keep their keyframes). The existing Sharpen and Unsharp Mask stay in Blur. | Done (test_effectsb effectsAreRegistered, projectRoundTrip; test_effectsui, test_videoeffects, test_mediapool pin the folders and the 90 video effects) |
| 57.2 | With neutral parameters (strength / amount / intensity 0, Quality 100, a 1 x 1 collage without spacing, ...) every new effect leaves the picture unchanged, and the sharpening, repair and Beauty effects leave a flat picture flat. Dead Pixel Fixer leaves a normal picture (colour bars) alone. | Done (test_effectsb neutralParametersLeavePictureAlone) |
| 57.3 | Sharpen Edges adds Strength times the difference between a pixel and a blur of Radius px, only where a neighbour at Radius px differs by Edge Threshold (full weight at the threshold, half weight at half of it), and never by more than Halo Limit of full scale. A 60 level step at 200 % overshoots on both sides by 12 to 64 levels; flat areas and a 2 % step are not touched. | Done (test_effectsb sharpenEdges) |
| 57.4 | Soften and Sharpen splits the picture into a blur (Radius) and the detail over it: detail smaller than Texture Limit is scaled by 1 - Soften, larger detail (edges) by 1 + Sharpen, with a smooth switch between the two (Texture Limit to twice it). Noise energy falls to under half at Soften 100 %; a Sharpen of 150 % overshoots an edge. | Done (test_effectsb softenAndSharpen) |
| 57.5 | Texture Pop adds Strength times the difference between the picture and a blur of Texture Size px (Brightness Only: to every channel alike; otherwise per channel), weighted down toward black and, by Highlight Protection, in the highlights. Strength 200 % raises the noise energy by more than half, a negative strength lowers it, and the same step gains less in the highlights than in the midtones. | Done (test_effectsb texturePop) |
| 57.6 | Detail Recovery blurs the picture (Radius), pulls the blur's brightness toward the middle grey where it is dark (Shadows) or bright (Highlights), and puts the detail (picture minus blur, times 1 + Detail) back on top. A window of 30 on 128 grey rises by more than 15 at Shadows 100 %, a window of 240 falls by more than 10 at Highlights 100 %, mid grey stays, and Detail alone sharpens a step without moving flat tones. | Done (test_effectsb detailRecovery) |
| 57.7 | Deband replaces a pixel by the average over Radius where it differs from it by less than Threshold (levels of 255; full replacement under half the threshold, none beyond it) and adds Dither: a hash noise of up to two levels, different per frame and equal for the same frame. The average is 9 x 9 tent-weighted taps, shrunk symmetrically near the frame edges, so a smooth ramp or gradient changes by at most one level at the defaults. A 16 step grey ramp (steps of 17 levels) has no step over 5 levels after Deband at Threshold 40 and Radius 20; a 60 level edge is kept. | Done (test_effectsb debandSmoothsSteps) |
| 57.8 | Dead Pixel Fixer compares each pixel with the ring of eight points Defect Size px away: a pixel brighter than all of them by Threshold (bright) or darker than all of them by Threshold (dark) is replaced by the ring's average; Detect limits it to one kind. A single stuck pixel is repaired (255 to under 116 on grey 100), its neighbours are not touched, a two-pixel blob is left alone at Defect Size 1 and repaired at 3. | Done (test_effectsb deadPixelFixer) |
| 57.9 | Beauty is a bilateral filter of 7 x 7 taps spread over Smoothing Size px: neighbours count by distance and by colour closeness (Edge Sensitivity sets how far apart colours may be), so blotches are averaged and edges are not crossed; Detail Recover gives back that share of the difference to the original; Smoothing is the mix with the original; Skin Tones Only limits it to skin-coloured pixels (a Cb / Cr range, no face detection). Noise energy falls under 35 %, Detail Recover 100 % gives the original back, a 60 level edge keeps all but a few levels, textured skin colour is smoothed and textured blue is not. | Done (test_effectsb beautySmoothsSkinKeepsEdges) |
| 57.10 | Analog Damage moves every scan line sideways (Line Jitter: a slow wobble plus rare torn lines, and a stronger pull in a Tracking Error band at the bottom, with white noise lines), smears the colour (not the brightness) Color Bleed px to the right, adds Noise to the brightness and a little to the colour, and darkens every other pair of lines by Scanlines. Edge positions vary per line; the same frame and seed repeat exactly, another frame or Seed differs; the tracking band moves lines more than twice as much as the top of the picture. | Done (test_effectsb analogDamage) |
| 57.11 | Film Damage adds specks of dirt (cells of 24 px, each possibly holding one speck, mostly dark and some bright, kept for two frames), up to five vertical scratches (kept for three frames), a Vignette that darkens toward the corners, a Gate Weave shift of the whole picture that differs per frame, a Flicker gain per frame and a Softness blur. All of it repeats for the same frame and Seed. | Done (test_effectsb filmDamage) |
| 57.12 | JPEG Damage works on blocks of Block Size px: the block average is quantized coarsely and the detail around it finely in brightness, and the colour is taken from blocks twice as wide and quantized harder; the quantizer grows as Quality falls (Quality 100 changes nothing). At Quality 10 a 320 px ramp of over 200 levels has under 50, constant inside each 8 px block, and 16 px blocks are twice as wide. | Done (test_effectsb jpegDamage) |
| 57.13 | Watercolor takes, for every pixel, the mean colour of the calmest of the four quadrants of a square of Brush Size px (generalised Kuwahara filter), darkens and saturates edges by Edge Darkening (colour times itself), changes Saturation and adds paper grain (fine fibres and broad blotches fixed to the picture, Paper Texture). Noise energy falls under a quarter; a 140 level edge stays; Paper 0 leaves a flat picture flat. | Done (test_effectsb watercolor) |
| 57.14 | Stylize has three styles over a blur of Detail Size px: Relief (the picture times a light from Light Angle, from the brightness gradient), Ink Outline (colours reduced to Levels per channel plus a black line where the gradient is strong) and Cutout (the same flat colours without lines); Strength mixes with the original. Turning the light by 180 degrees swaps bright and dark edges; flat areas stay. | Done (test_effectsb stylize) |
| 57.15 | Aperture Diffraction adds, for every light brighter than Threshold, Spikes straight rays of Length px with their brightest part at the light, fading quadratically; Rotation turns them, Intensity scales them, Tint colours them. Light is added, so spikes also show over see-through areas. Four spikes put light on the axes and none on the diagonals; Rotation 45 degrees swaps that. | Done (test_effectsb apertureDiffraction) |
| 57.16 | Lens Reflections adds up to eight copies of the lights brighter than Threshold, mirrored through the lens centre (Center X / Y) at the factors Spread times n / Reflections (a light 80 px right of the centre, Spread 100 %, one reflection: a copy 80 px left of it), softened by Softness, dimmer with n and tinted per reflection (hue cycling) and by Tint. Only lights count: a spot brighter than Threshold (in brightness, not per channel) and brighter than its surroundings; broad bright areas and mid-bright backgrounds make no reflection, and sources fade out toward the frame edges so no frame border shows. A mid-grey frame is unchanged. | Done (test_effectsb lensReflections) |
| 57.17 | Color Palette finds the Colors dominant colours (k-means on a 16 x 16 grid of the picture, farthest-point seeds, no randomness) and draws them as swatches in a strip (Bottom, Top, Left or Right, Strip Size percent, Gap px between swatches, Opacity), the biggest cluster first. The rest of the picture is untouched. | Done (test_effectsb colorPalette) |
| 57.18 | 3D Keyer makes a pixel transparent by its distance to the nearest of up to three picked colours (Color 1; Color 2 and 3 when their switches are on) in (Cb, Cr, sqrt(Brightness Weight) times Y') space: matte = smoothstep(Tolerance, Tolerance + Softness, distance) with Tolerance and Softness as percent of 0.5, then Clip Black / Clip White, Invert and the View (final, matte, status) like the other keyers. | Done (test_effectsb keyer3d) |
| 57.19 | Video Collage tiles the picture Columns x Rows times, each tile inset by half the Spacing, with rounded corners (Corner Radius), a shadow (Shadow percent, down and to the right) and a background (colour, Background Opacity); Tile Fit stretches the picture to the tile, fills it (crops) or fits it inside (empty at the sides). With 2 x 2 tiles every tile shows the whole picture at half size. | Done (test_effectsb videoCollage) |
| 57.20 | The contact sheet `effects-b.png` (KK_EFFECT_SHEETS) draws every effect of this section on a test picture. | Done (test_effectsheets effectsB) |

Not in this section: the colour-management effects (waiting for the owner, see 56), effects that need
machine learning or DCTL, the multi-frame effects and the tracking effects.

## 58. Stabilization (stabilize branch, owner request for 1.2)

Steadying a shaky video clip, in the Inspector's Video tab. Clean room: built from the public
description of the feature (a Stabilization section with Mode, Camera Lock, Zoom, Cropping Ratio,
Smooth, Strength and a Stabilize button); no code, text or assets of any other editor. The method
(frame-to-frame motion of patches over the whole picture, a robust fit, the chained camera path,
a smoothed path, the difference applied to every frame) is standard. Where the description leaves
room, this section says what Kinoklip does.

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 58.1 | The Inspector's Video tab of a clip of a video file has a Stabilization section between Speed Change and Retime and Scaling, with an enable switch in the header (like the other sections), Mode (Perspective, Similarity, Translation; default Perspective), Camera Lock, Zoom, Cropping Ratio (0.25 - 1, default 1), Smooth (0 - 1, default 0.25), Strength (-1 - 1, default 1; 0 = off, negative = the opposite way), a Stabilize button, Cancel, a progress bar and a status line. Other clips (solids, titles, generators) have no such section. The section has a help button and guide article. | Done (test_stabilizeui sectionSitsAfterSpeedChange, controlsAndEnableRules) |
| 58.2 | Camera Lock switches Smooth and Cropping Ratio off in the Inspector (they have nothing to do); Strength and Zoom stay usable. Every change of a control is one undo step (a drag of a field or slider is one step for the whole drag); values outside the ranges never reach the clip. | Done (test_stabilizeui controlsAndEnableRules; test_stabilization strengthScalesTheCorrection) |
| 58.3 | Stabilize measures the clip off the UI thread (the editor stays usable) with a progress bar and Cancel, then stores the result with the clip in one undo step "Stabilize" (undo removes the measurement, redo brings it back). Cancel, a failure or a clip that was removed meanwhile leave the clip untouched and add no undo step. Pressing Stabilize on a clip that cannot be measured (a freeze frame, a still, a title) shows why instead of starting. | Done (test_stabilizeui stabilizeRunsInTheBackgroundAsOneUndoStep, cancelLeavesTheClipUntouched, freezeFrameShowsAMessage; test_stabilizejob runnerReportsProgressAndFinishes, runnerCancel, refusals) |
| 58.4 | What needs a new measurement (pressing Stabilize again): the Mode, and nothing else. Smooth, Camera Lock, Strength, Zoom and Cropping Ratio are applied from the stored camera path when a frame is drawn, so they change the picture at once. After the Mode is changed the old measurement is not used (the clip renders uncorrected) and the section says "Press Stabilize again"; changing the Mode back uses it again. | Done (test_stabilizeui stabilizeRunsInTheBackgroundAsOneUndoStep; test_stabilization activeRules; test_stabilizerender strengthZeroAndInactiveStatesRenderAsBefore) |
| 58.5 | The measurement takes every pair of neighbouring source frames of the clip (at most 640 px wide), follows a grid of patches over the picture with the Planar Tracker's matcher, lets RANSAC discard patches that move on their own (people, cars), and fits the model of the Mode to the patches that agree: Translation the median move, Similarity a move, turn and scale (least squares), Perspective a homography. The pair motions are chained into the camera path (one entry per source frame). A pair that cannot be matched counts as no movement and is reported ("N of M frame pairs could not be matched"). | Done (test_stabilizer translationOfOnePair, similarityOfOnePair, movingObjectIsIgnored, unrelatedFramesAreNotMatched, analysisFollowsTheCameraPath, analysisOfRotationAndZoomJitter, failuresAreReported; test_stabilizerender measuredPathMatchesTheTruth) |
| 58.6 | The new camera path is the measured path low-pass filtered with a Gaussian whose standard deviation is Smooth x 2 seconds (a locally linear Gaussian fit: a slow pan stays, also near the ends of the clip); Smooth 0 follows the camera and corrects nothing. Camera Lock replaces it with the first frame's pose (a tripod). A frame is moved by the difference between the new path and the measured one. Translation removes jitter and keeps the pan (the corrected path is within 0.2 % of the picture width (rms) of the true pan on the test path, a third of the jitter or less); Similarity also removes rotation and zoom jitter; Camera Lock removes pan and jitter. | Done (test_stabilization translationRemovesJitterButKeepsThePan, smoothZeroCorrectsNothing, cameraLockRemovesEverything, similarityHandlesRotationAndZoomJitter; test_stabilizerender cameraLockAlignsTheFrames) |
| 58.7 | Strength scales the correction in the numbers of the model (the move, the turn and the scale; the homography entries): 1 full, 0.5 half, 0 none, -1 the opposite way. Strength 0, the switch off, no measurement or a Mode that differs from the measurement render exactly as a clip that never had stabilization (identical pixels). | Done (test_stabilization strengthScalesTheCorrection, activeRules; test_stabilizerender strengthZeroAndInactiveStatesRenderAsBefore) |
| 58.8 | Zoom on scales the picture about its centre by the smallest factor that keeps the picture covering its own frame in the worst corrected frame of the measured frames, never more than 1 + Cropping Ratio (so 2 at 1, 1.25 at 0.25; beyond that the edges show). Zoom off leaves the scale at 1 and the frame size alone, so the moving edges show. The factor is the same for every frame of the clip. | Done (test_stabilization zoomCoversTheWorstFrame; test_stabilizerender zoomHidesTheEdges, solidMovesByTheCorrection) |
| 58.9 | The correction is keyed by SOURCE frame (at the frame rate of the media): trimming, slipping, splitting, changing the speed, reversing or a retime curve keep every frame's own correction, and both halves of a split clip keep the measurement. Source frames outside the measured range are drawn uncorrected. Pressing Stabilize measures the source frames the clip shows at that moment. A freeze frame (one source frame), a still picture and clips that are not video files are refused with a message. | Done (test_stabilization thePathIsKeyedBySourceFrame, splitKeepsTheDataOfBothHalves; test_stabilizerender keysFollowTheSourceFrame; test_stabilizejob planCoversTheShownSourceFrames, refusals) |
| 58.10 | The correction is a transform of the clip's picture in the compositor, applied before Dynamic Zoom and before the clip's own Transform and Cropping (`render::clipTransform()` given the timeline's frame rate), in the GPU path and in the CPU path, in the viewer and in the export (one code path). Perspective mode uses a projective matrix: the GPU vertex shader keeps the third row in w for perspective-correct texture coordinates. The on-screen controls of Transform and Dynamic Zoom keep working on the uncorrected picture; the mask and Color page overlays follow the corrected picture. | Done (test_stabilizerender cameraLockAlignsTheFrames, perspectiveAgreesOnGpuAndCpu, solidMovesByTheCorrection; the overlays by hand) |
| 58.11 | The project file stores the settings and the camera path with the clip ("stabilization": the settings, and "data": mode, frame rate, first source frame, picture aspect, and the path as compressed little-endian float32 in base64). A clip that never had stabilization writes nothing, projects saved before load unchanged and render the same, damaged data is dropped and the settings kept. The format version is not changed. | Done (test_stabilization projectFileRoundTripAndOldFiles, undoRedoSaveLoad; test_stabilizerender oldProjectRendersTheSame) |
| 58.12 | `clip stabilize` (command layer, so the command line and the MCP server) measures a clip and stores settings and data in one undo step: `--clip`, `--mode`, `--smooth`, `--strength`, `--cropping-ratio`, `--zoom`, `--camera-lock`; options not given keep the clip's settings. Progress is reported through the command's progress callback (one call per frame). `--settings-only` changes the settings of the stored measurement without measuring again (`not_analysed` if there is none for the Mode). Errors: `unknown_clip`, `bad_value`, `out_of_range`, `cannot_stabilize`, `analysis_failed`, `track_locked`. In live mode the measurement runs on the window's thread. | Done (test_stabilizecommand) |
| 58.13 | `clip set --stabilization off` (or `on`) switches the stabilization of a clip off and on again, keeping the measurement; `clip list` shows a clip's stabilization settings and whether it was measured. | Done (test_stabilizecommand clipSetSwitchesItOff, stabilizesAClip) |
| 58.14 | No keyboard shortcut. | Decision |

## 59. Fade curves (fade-curves branch, owner request: "changeable fade curves on timeline clips, dragged with the mouse on the clip")

The fades of section 31 are no longer always straight: a dot in the middle of the fade line shapes the
fade from linear to a slow-start or a fast-start curve. Written from public descriptions of how
editors behave and from general knowledge, not from any application. Tests:
