`tests/timeline/test_fadecurves.cpp` (fc), `tests/render/test_clipattributes.cpp` (ca),
`tests/core/test_levelops.cpp` (lo), `tests/app/test_commands.cpp` (cmd).

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 59.1 | A fade that is at least 28 px wide on a clip at least 24 px tall shows a round curve handle (dot, 8 px) on the fade line at its middle (halfway along the fade, at the line's height there); it shows with the fade handles (hover of the clip, or while the clip has a fade) and has its own cursor (vertical split arrows). No handle on a clip without that fade, none on a fade narrower than 28 px (so it never touches the fade handle at the fade's end), none on captions (they have no fade handles). The grab area is the dot plus 2 px. Kinds covered: every kind that has fade handles (video, audio, titles, generators, compound clips; not captions), fade-in and fade-out separately. | Done (fc handleVisibilityRule, handleHitArea) |
| 59.2 | Dragging the dot up or down changes the fade's shape continuously: the middle of the fade follows the pointer's height (kept relative to where the dot was grabbed, no jump). Down = slow start (the level stays low first, then rises), up = fast start (rises first, then flattens). The value is -1 ... +1, 0 = linear; it is limited to that range, and values within 0.04 of 0 snap to exactly 0 so linear is easy to get back. A tooltip next to the pointer shows the value while dragging ("Fade curve: 0.50 (slow start)"). The fade handle keeps changing the length; the curve stays when the length changes. One undo step per drag ("Fade In Curve" / "Fade Out Curve"). Linked clips are not changed (the fade handles do not change them either). | Done (fc dragFadeIn, dragFadeOutOnVideo, dragLimitsAndSnap, linkedClipsKeepTheirOwnCurve; the tooltip is not tested) |
| 59.3 | Escape during the drag cancels it: the value and the undo stack are as before the press. | Done (fc escapeCancels) |
| 59.4 | A double-click on the dot sets that fade back to linear (one undo step, "Reset Fade Curve"); on an already linear fade it changes nothing and adds no undo step. Removing a fade (length 0) also removes its curve, so a new fade starts linear. | Done (fc doubleClickResets, removingTheFadeDropsTheCurve; lo fadeCurves) |
| 59.5 | One curve family for sound and picture: the fade's progress t (0 at the clip edge, 1 where the fade reaches full level) goes through t^(4^c) (c = the curve value); then the video factor is that shape, and the audio gain is sin(shape * pi / 2) (the equal-power fade of 31.5 with a bent progress). c = 0 is exactly the old maths (bit-identical: the shape is t itself); the shape is exactly 0 at the outer end and exactly 1 at the inner end for every c; c = 1 gives 0.0625 at the middle, c = -1 gives 0.84. | Done (ca fadeShapeMaths) |
| 59.6 | The mixer plays the curve: the gain is evaluated per 64 samples at the exact fractional frame and ramped linearly in between (the same granularity as all clip gains, 31.3), so the fade is click-free; exactly 0 at the clip edge sample and exactly the clip gain at the end of the fade. A linear fade sounds as before. Fade-in and fade-out have their own curves. | Done (ca audioFadeCurvesMix, audioFadeLinearMatchesOldMaths) |
| 59.7 | The compositor multiplies the clip's opacity by the curved factor, in the GPU and the CPU path, so viewer, playback, export and the render cache agree (alpha 0 on the first frame, full opacity after the fade; the fade-out is 0 on the last frame). | Done (ca videoFadeCurvesRender gpu/cpu) |
| 59.8 | The shaded wedge and the fade line on the clip are drawn as the curve (the shape of 59.5: the line's height is the shape, for audio too); a linear fade is drawn as the straight line it always was. | Done (fc overlayIsDrawnAsTheCurve) |
| 59.9 | The curves are stored on the clip (`ClipData::fadeInCurve` / `fadeOutCurve`) and saved in the project file as `fadeInCurve` / `fadeOutCurve` next to `fadeIn` / `fadeOut`; only written when the fade has a length and the curve is not 0; loaded values are limited to -1 ... +1. Projects from before load linear and sound and look the same. The project file version is unchanged (an older build ignores the curve and plays the fade linear). | Done (lo fadeCurveSerialization) |
| 59.10 | Copy / paste, duplicate and split carry the curve the way they carry the fade length (the left part of a split keeps the fade-in and its curve, the right part the fade-out and its curve; a joined clip keeps the outer fades). Paste Attributes does not carry fades (it never carried the fade lengths), so it leaves the curves alone. | Done (fc copyPasteSplitAndPasteAttributes; lo fadeCurves) |
| 59.11 | `clip set` (command layer, section 53) gets `fade-in`, `fade-out` (lengths: frames or timecode, 0 = none) and `fade-in-curve`, `fade-out-curve` (-1 ... 1); `clip list` / `clip set` show `fadeIn`, `fadeInCurve`, `fadeOut`, `fadeOutCurve` for clips that have the fade. A curve needs a fade (error `no_fade`); out-of-range values give `out_of_range`; only the named clip changes. The CLI and the MCP server get them as the command layer lists them. | Done (cmd clipSetFades) |
| 59.12 | Not changed on purpose: the Inspector shows no fade lengths, so it got no curve; the OpenTimelineIO export and import do not carry fades (the `metadata.kinoklip` of a clip has none), so they do not carry the curve. | Decision |
| 59.13 | A fade never outlasts its clip: fade-in >= 0, fade-out >= 0 and fade-in + fade-out <= the clip length, kept by `editops::clampFades()` wherever a clip gets shorter (split, trims of either edge with or without ripple, roll, slide, clear range / overwrite, razor on linked clips, range edits, Cut page smart edits, speed changes, compound windows) and on project load. After a split the right piece's fade-out becomes at most the piece's length (its end stays the clip end) and the left piece loses it, the mirror for the fade-in; when both still do not fit the fade at the cut edge is shortened first (fade-out on a tail cut, fade-in on a head cut). A fade that becomes 0 loses its curve. Transition lengths are held to twice the clip length. | Done (lo splitKeepsFadesInsideThePieces, trimsClampFades, loadClampsFades) |
| 59.14 | The timeline draws and hit-tests fade handles with the fade limited to the clip, so even a file with a longer fade shows both handles inside the clip where they can be grabbed. | Done (fc splitLeavesGrabbableHandle) |

## 60. Multi-frame effects of the 1.2 gap list (effects-5 branch, owner request for 1.2)

Nine more effects for the Effects library: the ones that need frames other than the current one
(Motion Trails, Smear, Motion Blur, Frame Replacer, Deflicker, Color Stabilizer, Automatic Dirt
Removal, Dust Buster) and Patch Replacer, which is in this batch by the list and reads only its
own frame. Written from the names, from published methods (weighted frame averages, temporal
median, block-matching motion estimation, mean-colour gain) and general knowledge, not from any
application. They draw on the GPU compositor (viewer, playback and export); like the other spatial
effects they have no CPU version. Tests: `tests/render/test_effects5.cpp` (e5); contact sheet:
`effects-5.png` written by its `contactSheet` case (KK_EFFECT_SHEETS).

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 60.1 | The Effects library gains the folder Temporal (Motion Trails, Smear, Motion Blur); Revival gains Frame Replacer, Deflicker, Automatic Dirt Removal, Dust Buster and Patch Replacer; Color gains Color Stabilizer. Every effect has a description, parameters inside their ranges, keyframable numbers (not the frame counts, which decide what is decoded) and saves and loads with the project. | Done (e5 effectsAreRegistered, projectRoundTrip; test_effectsui, test_videoeffects, test_mediapool pin the folders and the 99 video effects) |
| 60.2 | How neighbouring frames are supplied: an effect that needs other frames of its clip is listed in `timefx::framesRead()` (nearest frames first) and the renderer decodes those frames of the same clip before drawing and hands each to the effect as the clip's layer at that frame with the effects above the asking one applied (the mechanism Echo and Strobe already used, now general). The frames are the clip's own frame steps: local frame f - 1 and f + 1 go through the clip's trim, speed, reverse and freeze like any frame (at speed 2 the previous frame is two source frames back; reverse swaps them; a freeze frame has equal neighbours). | Done (e5 framesReadFollowTheEffect, neighboursFollowTrimSpeedAndReverse, frameReplacerReadsTheRightFrames) |
| 60.3 | Frames outside the media repeat the media's first or last frame (the source time is clamped); frames next to a trimmed edge are the real frames of the trimmed-away handle; a generated, title or nested clip, which has no handles, repeats its first or last frame. | Done (e5 frameReplacerReadsTheRightFrames "media start repeats" and "trimmed first frame, handle"; neutralParametersLeavePictureAlone for a generator) |
| 60.4 | The window is bounded: at most 6 distinct frames per clip and output frame over all effects of the clip (the number of decoders the pool opens per file, `DecoderPool::kMaxPerPath`; Echo and Strobe share the bound, which was 9 on paper before): Smear 1 - 2 frames per side, Deflicker 1 - 2, Dirt Removal and Dust Buster 1 - 2, Motion Trails 1 - 5 trails, Frame Replacer 1 - 2, Motion Blur 2, Color Stabilizer 1; stacked effects share and truncate to the nearest frames, and a neighbour past the bound is replaced as in 60.5. The neighbours are decoded in parallel on the renderer's decoder pool (a decoder per frame, each following playback forward), so a neighbour costs a decode, not a seek, during playback; the pool's per-file bound is shared by every clip of the same file in the frame. | Done (e5 framesReadFollowTheEffect) |
| 60.5 | A neighbour that cannot be rendered (not decoded, no history inside a Compositing page clip) is replaced by the nearest available frame in time, else by the current frame, so playback never stops for it and the effect degrades to a gentler result; nothing in the picture depends on timing: paused, playing and exporting give the same pixels for the same frame, whatever was rendered before. On an adjustment clip the picture below is not drawn at other frames, so these effects see the current frame there and change nothing. | Done (e5 missingNeighboursUseTheNearestAvailableFrame, sameFrameSamePixelsWhateverWasRenderedBefore; the adjustment clip case is a known limit) |
| 60.6 | The render cache needs no new key: a chunk's key already hashes the complete clip (effects with their parameters, trim, speed, retime) and the media key, which is everything the neighbours of a clip's frame depend on. | Done (no change; renderCacheChunks hashes whole clip JSON, 30.x) |
| 60.7 | Motion Trails (Temporal) lays the previous Trails frames (Frame Spacing apart) behind the picture: trail i fades to the factor Trail Opacity x (1 - Fade)^i. Lighten shows, per channel, the brighter of the picture and the trail frame mixed toward the picture by that factor; Average blends picture and trails with weights that sum to 1. Trails appear behind a moving object and never ahead of it, the first frame of a clip has none, and a picture that does not move is unchanged. | Done (e5 motionTrailsLeaveATrailBehindTheSquare, neutralParametersLeavePictureAlone, referenceArithmetic) |
| 60.8 | Smear (Temporal) averages the picture with the Length frames on each side (Direction Both, Behind or Ahead), a frame d away weighing (1 - Falloff)^d, weights normalised and mixed with the plain picture by Amount. With Falloff 0 and five frames each pixel is the mean of the five frames' pixels. | Done (e5 smearAveragesAroundTheFrame, referenceArithmetic) |
| 60.9 | Motion Blur (Temporal) estimates the motion of every pixel with the coarse-to-fine symmetric block matching of the retime code (GlRetime::estimateFlow(), luma pyramid, 3 x 3 sums of differences, the change penalised, at most 640 px wide): forward from this frame to the next, and backward from the previous frame to this one. The picture is averaged over Quality samples along that motion over the shutter, Shutter Angle / 360 of a frame centred on the frame (forward motion for the later half, backward for the earlier half). It is block matching, not machine learning: fast or tiny motion and uncovered background can smear or stay sharp. Pictures that do not move are not blurred; a brightness flicker is not motion. | Done (e5 motionBlurSmearsAlongTheMotion) |
| 60.10 | Frame Replacer (Revival) replaces the frame by the Previous Frame, the Next Frame or the Mix of Both, by the share Replace (keyframable: key it up at the damaged frame, or cut the clip so only that frame has the effect). At 100 % the result is the neighbour's picture. | Done (e5 frameReplacerMixesTheNeighbours, frameReplacerReadsTheRightFrames) |
| 60.11 | Deflicker (Revival) measures the mean colour of the picture area on the GPU in the current frame and in the Time Window frames on each side (a 2 x 2 box reduction to under 128 px, then the exact mean, no read-back), and multiplies the frame by target / current, where the target is the mean over the window including the frame: one brightness gain (Rec. 709 luma) or, with Correct Colour Too, one gain per channel; the gain is limited to 1 / (1 + Limit) ... 1 + Limit and mixed with 1 by Strength. The frame to frame spread of a flickering clip falls by more than half; the picture is not otherwise changed. | Done (e5 deflickerFlattensTheBrightness, referenceArithmetic) |
| 60.12 | Color Stabilizer (Color) measures the mean colour of a Region of the picture (Region X / Y shift its centre, Width / Height as percent of the picture) in the current frame and in the Reference Frame (clip-local frame number, default 0) and applies per channel gains: Exposure and White Balance (reference / current per channel), Exposure (the luma ratio for all channels) or White Balance (the per-channel gains scaled to keep the current frame's luma); limited by Limit (extra gain, up to 300 %) and mixed by Strength; the reference frame itself is unchanged. A colour that drifts by 25 % is back within 4 levels of the reference. | Done (e5 colorStabilizerHoldsTheReference, referenceArithmetic) |
| 60.13 | Automatic Dirt Removal (Revival) takes, per pixel and channel, the median of the neighbouring frames (the mean of the middle two of four; two neighbours: their mean). A pixel is dirt when it differs from that median by more than Dirt Contrast in some channel while the middle two neighbours agree within Motion Tolerance (so moving edges, where the neighbours disagree, are kept). The dirt mask grows by Grow px and the marked pixels are replaced by the median, mixed by Strength. With Time Window 1 (three frames) dust in one frame goes; with 2 (five frames) dust that stays two frames goes too. A one-frame white dot is removed while a moving square's edges are untouched; frames next to the dust frame are not changed. Limit: something thinner than its own movement per frame looks like dust. | Done (e5 dirtRemovalRemovesTheDotAndKeepsTheMovingEdge, dirtRemovalMatchesTheReferenceArithmetic, fiveFramesRemoveDustThatStaysTwoFrames, referenceArithmetic) |
| 60.14 | Dust Buster (Revival) does the same inside a region (Region Shape rectangle or ellipse, Center X / Y px from the middle, Width, Height, Feather px: the repair ramps in over that width inside the edge); outside nothing changes. Repair Whole Region replaces everything inside the region by the temporal median whether or not dirt is detected. The region is typed in; there is no on-screen handle yet. | Done (e5 dustBusterWorksInsideItsRegionOnly) |
| 60.15 | Patch Replacer (Revival) copies the patch around Source X / Y onto the target around Target X / Y (px from the middle of the picture), Patch Shape rectangle or ellipse of Width x Height px, the edge ramping in over Feather px inside it, mixed with the picture by Blend. Outside the target nothing changes. It reads only the current frame. | Done (e5 patchReplacerCopiesARegion) |
| 60.16 | With neutral parameters (Trail Opacity 0, Amount / Replace / Strength 0, Shutter Angle 0, Blend 0) every new effect leaves the picture unchanged (and costs nothing: it is skipped and reads no neighbour); a picture that does not change is unchanged by the others at any frame, the first and last included. | Done (e5 neutralParametersLeavePictureAlone) |
| 60.17 | The contact sheet `effects-5.png` (KK_EFFECT_SHEETS) shows before and after for each effect, four frames in a row for the temporal ones, on synthetic footage. | Done (e5 contactSheet) |

Not in this section: Match Move and Surface Tracker (tracking), the colour-management effects
(waiting for the owner, see 56) and anything that needs machine learning or DCTL. Not verified:
a real GPU (the tests ran on software rendering), real footage with sensor noise and compression
(Deflicker, Dirt Removal and Motion Blur were tuned on synthetic lossless footage), and the cost
of the extra decoders on 4K footage.

## 61. Import and export of all settings (settings-vim branch, owner request for 1.2: "Import and export all the app's settings with one click")

One file, `.kinoklip-settings`, holds everything Kinoklip stores outside projects, so a new computer or
a fresh install can be set up in one step. Tests: `tests/app/test_settingstransfer.cpp` (st),
`tests/app/test_vimkeys.cpp` (vk). Code: `src/app/SettingsTransfer.h` (no widgets),
`src/app/SettingsTransferDialog.h`, `src/app/commands/SettingsCommands.cpp`.

What the application stores outside projects (the inventory this section covers):

| Where | What | In the file? |
|---|---|---|
| QSettings (`~/.config/cutwise/Kinoklip.conf`) `ui/`, `project/`, `system/`, `playback/`, `edit/` | all pages of Preferences: colour scheme, widget style, UI scale, animations, autosave, start-up choice, cache sizes, render backend, hardware acceleration, playback resolution, pre/post-roll, editing defaults and switches, zoom anchor | Yes (the folders and the audio device are not, see below) |
| QSettings `control/localTools` | Preferences > System > Local Control | Yes, but a file can only switch it off |
| QSettings `keymap/`, `shortcuts/` | the active keyboard profile (`keymap/profile`), the custom shortcuts of each built-in profile (`shortcuts/<profile>/<action>`), the first-start choice flag | Yes |
| QSettings `mainWindow/`, `pages/`, `projectManager/`, `keyframes/`, `scopesWindow/` | window geometry and state, the current page, panel and splitter layout of every page, panel visibility, Project Manager and scopes window layout | Yes |
| QSettings `mediaPool/`, `timeline/`, `editIndex/`, `effects/`, `deliver/`, `import/`, `viewer/`, `colorPage/`, `dialogs/`, `multicam/` | Media Pool view, Timeline View Options, new marker and flag colours, Edit Index columns, Effects Live Preview, Deliver file-name options, folder-import options, viewer overlays and guides, Color page scope, remembered "do not ask again" choices | Yes |
| Data folder `~/.local/share/cutwise/Kinoklip/` | custom keyboard profiles `keymaps/*.json`, `guide-presets.json`, `power-bins.json`, `export_presets.json` (user render presets) | Yes (small JSON files, each checked) |
| Data folder | `autosave/`, project lock files, `LUT/` | No (see below) |
| QSettings `paths/` | last-used folders, the recent-projects list (`paths/recentProjects`) | No; the recent list only with a checkbox, default off |
| QSettings `project/libraryDirectory`, `system/cacheDirectory`, `system/proxyDirectory`, `system/audioOutputDevice`, `color/lutFolders`, `compositing/paramFile` | folders and devices of this computer | No |
| Effect favourites, title presets | Kinoklip has no stored favourites or title presets outside projects and the files above | Nothing to include |
| Secrets | Kinoklip stores no passwords, tokens or keys | Nothing to include |

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 61.1 | The file is JSON: a header `kinoklipSettings` (`format` number 1, `app`, `appVersion`, `created`), `settings` (QSettings key to value: flag, number, text, list of texts, or `{"bytes": base64}` for saved window states), `files` (path in the data folder to the file's text) and, only when asked, `recentProjects`. Keys are sorted and the file is indented so it can be read and diffed. | Done (st roundTripManyKinds) |
| 61.2 | Preferences > User > Import and Export has Export Settings... and Import Settings.... Export asks where to write (suggested name `kinoklip-<date>.kinoklip-settings`), says what the file will and will not contain and offers "Also include the list of recent projects" (off), then writes the file atomically. The result message names the file. | Done (st importDialogFlow exports; the file chooser and message boxes by hand) |
| 61.3 | `Export` writes exactly the allowed keys that are set and the existing user files; machine-specific values (folders, audio device) and the recent-projects list are not written unless asked (recent projects only); `autosave/`, locks and the LUT folder are never written. | Done (st roundTripManyKinds, recentProjectsOnlyOnRequest) |
| 61.4 | Import first checks the whole file, then shows what it contains per area ("Editing preferences: 14 settings", "Custom keyboard profiles: 2 files", ...), the number of unknown entries it ignores and the entries it refused, the sentence that importing replaces the current settings (settings the file does not mention stay as they are), the folder where the backup of the current settings will be written, that most settings apply after a restart, and the recent-projects checkbox (off; disabled when the file has no list). The button is "Replace My Settings"; Cancel changes nothing. A file with nothing usable cannot be imported. | Done (st importDialogFlow) |
| 61.5 | Before anything is changed a backup of all current settings (the recent list too) is written as a `.kinoklip-settings` file to `settings-backups/` next to the settings file (`~/.config/cutwise/settings-backups/settings-backup-<date-time>.kinoklip-settings`). If the backup cannot be written nothing is changed. The result message and the command's `backup` field name the file. | Done (st backupHoldsTheOldSettings, backupFailureChangesNothing) |
| 61.6 | Import applies the user files (atomic write, only the fixed names) and the settings in one step. A value in the file replaces the current value; a setting the file does not contain stays; the custom shortcuts of a profile that appears in the file are replaced as a whole. The recent list is taken only when asked for and present. | Done (st roundTripManyKinds, partialFileKeepsTheRest, keymapGroupIsReplacedWhole) |
| 61.7 | Honest about "restart to apply": the window keeps layout, keyboard and most options in memory, so after an import the result says "Restart Kinoklip to apply them", and the window does not write its own layout over the imported one when it closes. Local Control is re-read at once (an imported "off" stops the listener). | Done (st importPendingFlag, commandsRoundTrip; the window close and listener by hand) |
| 61.8 | Local Control is never switched on by a file: a file value of true is ignored (and the result says so); false switches it off; a value that is not a flag does nothing. This holds for the dialog and the command. | Done (st localControlNeverSwitchedOn, commandsRoundTrip) |
| 61.9 | The file is untrusted. Refused with a clear message: not JSON, no header, no or a non-whole format number, a format number above this version's ("made by a newer Kinoklip ... Update Kinoklip to import it"), more than 16 MiB, `settings` or `files` of the wrong type, more than 5000 entries. | Done (st hostileHeaders, tooManyKeysAndFiles) |
| 61.10 | Every setting must match a known rule: its key must be on the list of areas (61 inventory), must have the plain shape of a key (letters, digits and `_.@ +%-` in `/`-separated parts, no `.` or `..` part, at most 200 characters), and its value the right type and range (for example `project/autosaveMinutes` a whole number 0 - 120, `edit/zoomAnchor` one of `playhead` / `mouse`, `ui/animationSpeed` 0.1 - 10, texts without control characters and of limited length, saved states at most 512 KiB). Entries that fail are skipped and reported; entries no rule knows are ignored. | Done (st hostileValues) |
| 61.11 | A path in `files` may only be `guide-presets.json`, `power-bins.json`, `export_presets.json` or `keymaps/<name>.json` (name: letters, digits, `_.- `, not starting with a dot, 100 characters at most); the content must be valid JSON of at most 1 MiB; at most 200 files. Anything else (`..`, absolute paths, sub-folders, other extensions, `autosave/`) is skipped. The target is checked again to lie inside the data folder when it is written. | Done (st hostileFilesNeverEscape, tooManyKeysAndFiles) |
| 61.12 | Command layer: `settings export --file <file> [--recent-projects]` and `settings import --file <file> [--recent-projects]` (so the command line and the MCP server). They work without a project, apply the same checks, the same backup and the same Local Control rule, and report `settings`, `files`, `backup`, `restartNeeded` and `notes`. Errors: `bad_settings_file`, `import_failed`, `write_failed`. `settings import` is for the person at the keyboard only (`CommandSpec::remoteAllowed = false`): the MCP server neither lists nor runs it (file mode and live mode), and `executeLive()` (the local control socket, also `--live`) refuses it with `not_allowed_remotely`; the command line without `--live`, the dialog and the `:` line's refusal message are the ways in. `settings export` is available everywhere. | Done (st commandsRoundTrip; mcp settingsImportIsNotForAttachedTools; livecontrol settingsImportIsRefusedOnTheLiveSocket) |
| 61.13 | The Vim keyboard profile and custom shortcuts survive an export and import. | Done (vk survivesRestartAndSettingsTransfer) |
| 61.14 | Decision: the recent-projects list is left out by default and included only behind a checkbox, because its paths are only useful when the projects are in the same places. Decision: the window layout is included (a window restored on a smaller screen is clamped by Qt). Decision: an import is not undoable; the backup is the way back (import the backup file). |  Decision |

## 62. Vim keybindings (settings-vim branch, owner request for 1.2: "Vim keybinds; primary goal: make editing easy and intuitive for nvim users")

A keyboard preset "Vim" plus a small input layer that gives the timeline vim's keys: counts (`5l`),
two-key sequences (`gg`, `dd`, `yy`) and modes (NORMAL, VISUAL), which plain shortcuts cannot do.
Keys drive existing actions only. Key table and the list of keys left out: `docs/keymaps/vim.md`
(generated by `scripts/keymap-presets.py`, like the other presets). Code: `src/ui/common/VimInput.h`
(state machine and event filter), `src/ui/timeline/VimTimelineHost.h` (selection, playhead, target
track), `src/app/VimController.h` (mode indicator, `:` command line). Tests: `tests/app/test_vimkeys.cpp`
(vk), `tests/app/test_keypresets.cpp` (kp).

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 62.1 | "Vim" is a built-in profile like the other presets (Keyboard Customization > Keyboard profile, first-start chooser). It keeps Resolve's keys for every command that has a key with Ctrl, Alt or Meta, and for Space, the cursor keys, Home / End, Del / Backspace and the function keys; Redo is Ctrl+R (and Ctrl+Shift+Z); Shuttle is Alt+J / Alt+Shift+K / Alt+L. Every key typed without Ctrl / Alt / Meta (letters, digits, punctuation, with or without Shift) is taken by the layer, so the commands that had one move to Alt + the same key where that is free and are otherwise unbound (listed in `docs/keymaps/vim.md`). No key is bound twice. | Done (vk presetIsSelectable; kp everyPresetLoadsWithoutConflicts) |
| 62.2 | The layer exists only while the Vim profile is the active one (switching the profile turns it on and off at once, and the mode indicator appears or goes) and takes keys only while a timeline or a viewer has the keyboard focus. It never takes a key when the focus is a text field, a spin box, a text editor, an editable combo box, an embedded editor, or when a dialog or popup is open. Typing `dd` into a text field types two letters and changes nothing. | Done (vk textFieldsKeepTheirKeys, otherPresetsAreUnaffected, commandLineEscapeAndTyping) |
| 62.3 | Every other preset behaves exactly as before: the layer is inactive, shows nothing, and no key goes through it; the other presets' key files are unchanged by this work (regenerating them gives the same files); in Resolve's profile D still toggles the clip. | Done (vk otherPresetsAreUnaffected) |
| 62.4 | Counts: digits before a motion repeat it (`5l` five frames forward, `3w` three edit points, `12h`); a 0 after other digits is part of the count, a first 0 is a motion; counts above 999 are capped at 999. Counts also repeat `u` (undo), `>` / `<` (nudge) and `n` / `N`, set how many clips `dd` and `yy` take, and move the target track by that many. | Done (vk countedMotions, deleteYankAndEdits, ddIsOneUndoStep) |
| 62.5 | Motions: `h` / `l` previous / next frame; `H` / `L` one second back / forward; `w` / `b` next / previous edit point; `0` and `gg` the start of the timeline, `$` and `G` its end (decision: there is no action for the edges of the clip under the playhead, so `0` / `$` do what `gg` / `G` do); `n` / `N` next / previous marker; `m{a-z}` remembers the playhead, `'{a-z}` and `` `{a-z} `` jump to it (until the window closes; an unset mark does nothing); `zi` / `zo` / `zf` zoom in / out / fit. All are the transport and timeline actions, so they behave as the arrow keys and menu items. | Done (vk countedMotions, startEndMarkersZoom, marksAndUnboundKeys, motionsInTheWindow) |
| 62.6 | `j` / `k` move the target video track down / up by one (counts allowed; stops at the first and last track); this is the destination of source edits and of `dd` (tried first). | Done (vk deleteYankAndEdits with the fake host; the window's targeting by hand) |
| 62.7 | `x` is Ripple Delete of the selection (nothing happens without a selection and without the Ripple Delete preferences, as the key `Del`); `X` is Delete leaving a gap. `dd` selects the clip under the playhead (the target video track first, else the top-most video track, else audio) and runs Ripple Delete: one undo step, linked clips follow; `3dd` takes that clip and the next two of the track. With no clip under the playhead nothing happens. `d` followed by a motion is not supported: the keys are swallowed and nothing is deleted. | Done (vk ddIsOneUndoStep, xDeletesSelection, deleteYankAndEdits) |
| 62.8 | `yy` copies the clip under the playhead (selection as for `dd`); `p` pastes at the playhead inserting (Paste Insert, rippling); `P` pastes overwriting (Paste). Each paste is one undo step. | Done (vk yankAndPaste, deleteYankAndEdits) |
| 62.9 | `u` is Undo (`3u` three steps), Ctrl+R is Redo. `i` is Insert, `o` Overwrite, `a` Append at End (source edits as F9, F10, Shift+F12), `s` splits at the playhead, `>` / `<` nudge the selection a frame, `M` adds a marker. Each edit is the existing action and one undo step. | Done (vk deleteYankAndEdits, ddIsOneUndoStep) |
| 62.10 | `v` starts VISUAL: it selects the clips under the playhead on every track; each motion extends the selection from where `v` was typed to the new playhead (either direction). In VISUAL `d` / `x` Ripple Delete the selection (one undo step), `X` deletes leaving a gap, `y` copies; all go back to NORMAL (`y` also clears the selection). Esc or `v` leaves VISUAL and clears the selection. | Done (vk visualMode, modesAndEscape) |
| 62.11 | Esc drops half-typed keys and a count and leaves VISUAL; with nothing pending in NORMAL it is not taken (so it still cancels a drag). A key that is not a command (`q`, `r`, `.`, ...) is swallowed and cancels a pending sequence, like an unmapped key in vim. Ctrl / Alt / Meta combinations, Space, Return and the cursor keys are never taken. | Done (vk modesAndEscape, marksAndUnboundKeys) |
| 62.12 | The timeline's tool bar shows the mode and the keys typed so far (`NORMAL`, `VISUAL`, `NORMAL  3d`) while the profile is Vim, and nothing otherwise. | Done (vk motionsInTheWindow, pendingKeysShowInTheIndicator, otherPresetsAreUnaffected) |
| 62.13 | `:` opens a command line at the bottom of the window (Return runs, Esc closes, Up / Down walk the history of the session; no Tab completion, decision). `:w` saves, `:q` closes the window (the usual unsaved-changes question), `:wq` and `:x` save and close. Any other text is a command of the command registry on the open project, written as on the command line (`:marker add --at 7 --name Hi`), one undo step; the line shows the result or the error. Typing in the line never goes through the layer. | Done (vk commandLine, commandLineEscapeAndTyping; `:w` and `:q` by hand because they open dialogs) |
| 62.14 | Left unbound on purpose, with the reason in `docs/keymaps/vim.md`: `.` (no record of the last edit as a command), `/` (the timeline has no search), `d` / `y` with a motion, `c`, `r`, registers, macros, text objects, ranges. The preset survives a restart (the profile and the custom shortcuts are in the settings) and a settings export and import. | Done (vk survivesRestartAndSettingsTransfer, marksAndUnboundKeys) |

## 63. Menu and panel reference written by the application (menu-ref branch, owner request: "write down all the little things, what each one actually does, and all the menus and the panels")

`docs/menu-reference.md` is not written by hand: Kinoklip writes it from the tables its menus are
built from and from a description every panel carries, so it cannot go stale (the idea is taken from
[Picasso Suite](https://github.com/fabox-os/Picasso-Suite), whose program writes its own
`Menu-Status.md`). Code: `src/app/MenuReference.h` (the walk and the writer), `src/ui/common/ActionDescriptions.cpp`
(one line of words for every standard command), `src/ui/common/PanelInfo.h` (the description of a
panel). Tests: `tests/app/test_menureference.cpp` (mr), ctest `check_menu_reference`
(`scripts/check_menu_reference.py`). Regenerate with `scripts/menu-reference.sh`.

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 63.1 | `kinoklip --menu-reference <file>` builds the main window offscreen without a project, walks the menu bar (submenus too) and the panels of every page, writes the Markdown file and exits (0, or 1 with the reason on stderr). It needs no display (`QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen` is set by the switch). | Done (mr switchIsParsed, writesTheFile, processWritesTheSameFileWithIsolatedSettings) |
| 63.2 | The first line is "N menu entries in M menus; P panels". Then one `## <Menu>` section per menu in menu-bar order (the application menu, File, Edit, Trim, Timeline, Clip, Mark, View, Playback, Audio, Workspace, Help), each a table `Entry / Shortcut (default Resolve keymap) / What it does`. Submenu entries are written `Parent > Child`, separators are ignored, checkable entries end in "(toggle)" and entries of an exclusive group in "(choice)"; a menu that lists files or machine settings (Open Recent, the guide presets, Color Theme, Style) is one row. Commands that no menu shows would be listed in a last table "Keyboard-only commands" (none today). Context menus offer these same commands and are not listed again. No date, no machine-specific text; the order is the menus' own. | Done (mr generatorRunsOffscreen, layoutOfThePage) |
| 63.3 | "What it does" is the command's status tip (also shown when the menu entry is highlighted), written once in `ActionDescriptions.cpp` with `tr()` after reading the command's slot or its spec section; entries made elsewhere (submenus that list files, set in `MainWindow::buildMenus()`) carry their own. A command without words makes `--menu-reference` fail and name it, and the file is not written. Entries that are disabled because the feature is not built carry the property `kkPlanned` and are marked "(planned)" (no entry has it today). | Done (mr everyEntryAndPanelIsDescribed, undescribedCommandIsAnError) |
| 63.4 | `## Panels` has a table per page (Media, Cut, Edit, Compositing, Color, Audio, Deliver, in that order) with every panel and page area that has a name and a description set with `setPanelInfo()` (`PanelInfo.h`, `tr()` text, set where the panel is built); the rows are sorted by name and a panel shown twice on a page is listed once. A panel needs no change in the writer. | Done (mr layoutOfThePage, everyEntryAndPanelIsDescribed) |
| 63.5 | The file is the same on every run and on every machine: the switch runs with an empty private settings and data folder (`XDG_CONFIG_HOME` and the others point at a temporary folder removed on exit), so the user's keyboard profile and settings are neither read nor changed, and shortcuts are those of the Resolve keymap. | Done (mr stableAcrossRuns, processWritesTheSameFileWithIsolatedSettings) |
| 63.6 | ctest `check_menu_reference` runs the switch into a temporary file and fails, printing the difference, when it differs from the committed `docs/menu-reference.md`, so a new menu entry or panel without a regenerated document fails the tests. The page is linked from `readme.md` and `docs/readme.md`. | Done (check_menu_reference; run with a changed file by hand) |
| 63.7 | The description table has no line for a command that does not exist (`describeStandardActions()` returns the unknown ids and a test checks it is empty), and every registered standard command has a status tip. | Done (mr everyEntryAndPanelIsDescribed) |

## 64. Help > Export Log File (menu-ref branch, roadmap: "saves what the app did in this session as one text file to attach to a bug report")

The session's messages are kept in memory while Kinoklip runs and written only when the user asks.
Code: `src/core/SessionLog.h` (the ring buffer and the message handler), `src/app/LogExport.h` (the file's
text), `src/app/LogExportDialog.h` (the dialog), `src/app/commands/LogCommands.cpp` (`log.export`); the system
facts are `bugreport::collect()` of section 43, not a second copy. Tests: `tests/core/test_sessionlog.cpp` (sl),
`tests/app/test_logexport.cpp` (le), `tests/playback/test_playbackstatslog.cpp` (ps).

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 64.1 | Help > Export Log File... (`help.export_log`, last in the Help menu, after Join the Community, no default key) opens a dialog that says what the file contains, shows all of its text so it can be read before sending, and has Save Log File... and Cancel. Save Log File... opens the save dialog (suggested name `kinoklip-log-<date>.txt`, the last folder is remembered) and writes the text that was shown. Cancel writes nothing. Nothing is written to disk before that. | Done (le helpMenuHasTheEntry, dialogNamesTheSectionsAndShowsTheText, exportWritesTheFile; the file chooser by hand) |
| 64.2 | The dialog lists the contents: version, operating system, desktop (Wayland or X11), graphics card and OpenGL renderer; the decoder chosen for each media file; the playback statistics; the warnings and errors of the session; other messages (at most the last 20,000 lines). It says that the file names the media files used and that some messages contain folder paths, that paths under the home folder are written as `~/...` and the user and computer name are removed, that no project or media content and no settings are included, and that nothing is sent anywhere. The list is built from the same section titles the file uses. | Done (le dialogNamesTheSectionsAndShowsTheText) |
| 64.3 | The in-memory log is a ring buffer of the last 20,000 messages or 4 MiB of text, whichever limit is reached first; the oldest are dropped and counted, and the file says "N earlier lines dropped" in its header. A message longer than 4,096 characters is cut. | Done (sl ringBufferKeepsTheLastLines, ringBufferKeepsAtMostTheByteLimit, defaultLimitsAndLongMessages; le fileHasTheSectionsTheDialogNames) |
| 64.4 | A message handler, installed first thing in `main()` (every mode, also the command line), keeps every warning, error and fatal message of any category and the info messages of the `kk.*` and `kinoklip.*` categories (debug messages never), then calls the handler that was installed before, or Qt's own, so what reaches stderr is unchanged. Nothing is written to disk by it. | Done (sl wantedMessages, handlerKeepsAndChains, stderrOutputIsUnchanged) |
| 64.5 | The file is plain text: a header (version, time written, session length, what it is), then the sections System (the lines of the bug report's system information, including the desktop and the OpenGL renderer), Decoder used for each media file (`kk.media` "decoding ..." and "hardware decoding ..." lines), Playback statistics (`kk.playback` "stats" lines and summaries), Warnings and errors, Other messages, each line as `[seconds] type category: text`. Empty sections say so. Every line goes through the home-folder shortening (`/home/anna/Videos/a.mp4` becomes `~/Videos/a.mp4`; `/home/annabel` stays) and then the user and computer name are removed (`bugreport::scrub()`). | Done (le homeFolderIsShortened, fileHasTheSectionsTheDialogNames, emptySessionStillHasEverySection) |
| 64.6 | The per-second playback statistics lines and the summary at the end of each play are always collected for the log (decision: one formatted line a second while playing; the counters behind them were already kept every frame, so there is no extra work per frame), and printed to stderr only with `KK_PLAYBACK_STATS` as before. | Done (ps statsAreKeptButNotPrinted; playbackstats linesWhilePlayingAndSummaryOnStop unchanged) |
| 64.7 | The command `log.export --file <file>` (command line: `kinoklip log export --file F`) writes the same file for the process it runs in, atomically. Decision: it is not remote-allowed (`CommandSpec::remoteAllowed = false`), like `settings.import`: the file path is chosen by the caller and the log can name folders, so the MCP server neither lists nor runs it and the local control socket refuses it with `not_allowed_remotely`; the window's menu entry and the command line are the ways in. | Done (le commandWritesAndIsForThePersonAtTheKeyboard) |
| 64.8 | The bug report dialog says that Help > Export Log File... saves a log to attach for crashes and playback problems. The bug issue form has an optional "Log file" field asking for it. `docs/privacy.md` has a section on the log (in memory only, written when asked, names media files and folders, what is removed). Guide: "Send a log file with a bug report". | Done (le bugReportDialogMentionsTheLog; check_issue_forms) |

## 65. Template looks of the 1.2 gap list (looks branch, owner request for 1.2: every effect Resolve's library has)

CCTV, Digital Glitch, Drone Overlay, DSLR, DVE, Video Call and Video Camera are designed from scratch from the general idea of their names (the gap list gives names only). They draw on the GPU compositor (viewer, playback and export) and have no CPU version, like the other spatial effects. Tests: `tests/render/test_looks.cpp`; contact sheet: `tests/render/test_effectsheets.cpp` (looks). Pixel code: `src/render/GlTemplateLooks.cpp` (pictures), `src/render/LookHud.cpp` (text and marks); catalogue: `src/core/BuiltinVideoEffects6.cpp`.

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 65.1 | The Effects library gains seven looks, each two ways: a video effect in the Stylize folder (CCTV, Digital Glitch, Drone Overlay, DSLR, DVE, Video Call, Video Camera) and an adjustment clip with the same name in Toolbox > Effects that carries that effect. Every element they show is drawn by the program (text, marks, grids, icons); no image files are used. Their numbers can be keyframed (except Seed), and they save and load with the project. | Done (test_looks looksAreRegistered, templatesMakeAdjustmentClips, looksSurviveASaveAndLoad) |
| 65.2 | The overlay looks (CCTV, Drone Overlay, DSLR, Video Call, Video Camera) paint their text and marks with QPainter into a transparent picture the size of the layer, scaled to the picture height (1080 design units), and the GPU lays it over the picture. The picture is kept as a texture while nothing that shows in it changes (frame size, every parameter at the frame, the clock, the blink). Font (empty: the system fixed-width font, for Video Call the system font), Text Size (25 - 300 %), Text Shadow (0 - 100 %, default 60: a dark outline and soft shadow round every letter, mark line and battery icon; 0 draws none) and Overlay Opacity (0 - 100 %, 0 draws nothing) are the last settings of each; text parameters are plain text and can be edited. | Done (test_looks everyOverlayDrawsText, textIsEditable, textShadowDarkensAroundTheText, overlayOnlyLooksKeepThePicture) |
| 65.3 | CCTV washes the colours towards a grey-green (Desaturation), adds contrast, per-pixel noise that is different every frame and the same for the same frame (Noise), every second scanline of about two design pixels darker (Scanlines) and shaded corners (Vignette); over it: corner marks, the Camera Name at the top left, a blinking red dot and REC at the top right (Recording Dot), and the date and time at the bottom left. The time is Start Date and Time (yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss) plus the clip time, written with Time Format (a Qt date and time format; an invalid start reads as 2000-01-01 00:00:00, an empty format as yyyy-MM-dd  HH:mm:ss). The dot is lit for the first half of each second. With every amount and the overlay opacity at 0 the picture is unchanged. | Done (test_looks cctv, clockFollowsTheClipTime, blinkingDotsChangeThePicture) |
| 65.4 | Digital Glitch (Amount 0 - 100 %, 0 leaves the picture unchanged): a new pattern every 1 / Glitches per Second seconds, chosen by Seed and the pattern number only, so the same frame always renders the same. Horizontal bands (heights from 15 to 60 % of Block Size, at least 2 px) slide sideways by up to Band Shift, square blocks of Block Size jump up to three block sizes, red and blue are pulled apart by Color Split inside moved bands and blocks (and over the whole picture in rough patterns), and Digital Noise puts coloured speckles into jumped blocks and flicker on moved bands. Positions wrap round at the picture edge. More Amount changes more pixels. | Done (test_looks digitalGlitch) |
| 65.5 | Drone Overlay draws four corner framing marks, a compass bar (ticks every 5 degrees across 90 degrees of heading, N / E / S / W and numbers every 15, a marker in the middle), a horizon line broken around a small cross that moves with Gimbal Pitch (9 design pixels per degree), the recording time with a blinking dot at the top left, the pitch at the top right, readouts of Altitude, Speed and Distance to Home at the left (metres and metres per second, or feet and miles per hour with Units imperial), and Battery (icon and percent, red at 15 % or less), Satellites and a Home bearing at the bottom. Altitude, Speed, Distance to Home, Heading, Gimbal Pitch, Battery and Satellites are numbers that can be keyframed. Framing Marks and Compass Bar can be switched off. | Done (test_looks droneReadoutsFollowTheNumbers, readoutsFollowKeyframesOnTheGpu) |
| 65.6 | DSLR draws a Rule of Thirds, Fine Grid (6 x 4) or no grid, frame lines in the corners, eleven small focus points, one active focus frame at Focus Point X / Y (percent of half the frame, moved by up to 90 % of it) that is white and turns green with Focus Locked, and a dark information bar with Shutter Speed (text), Aperture (f/ number, one decimal), ISO, Shots Remaining in brackets, the focus mode (AF-S, AF-C, MF), a battery and an exposure scale from -3 to +3 whose marker follows Exposure Compensation (EV, kept inside the scale). | Done (test_looks dslrFocusPointMovesAndLocks) |
| 65.7 | DVE shrinks the picture to Size percent (100 = the whole picture) into a box centred on the picture plus Position X / Y (timeline pixels), turns it by Rotation degrees (clockwise) and tilts it about the horizontal and vertical axes by Tilt X / Y degrees with perspective (camera distance twice the larger frame side). The box has a Border of Border Width inside its edge in Border Color, rounded corners (Corner Radius, not scaled by Size), a drop shadow of Shadow Opacity, softened over Shadow Blur and moved by Shadow Offset X / Y, and an Opacity for the whole. Outside the box and its shadow the layer is transparent, so the clip below shows. Size 100 with no move, turn, tilt, border, rounding and with Opacity 100 leaves the picture unchanged. | Done (test_looks dve) |
| 65.8 | Video Call draws the picture scaled to cover a rounded tile inset by Margin on all sides (Corner Radius) on the Background colour at Background Opacity; over it a thin edge (or a thick glowing ring in the Speaking Ring Color while Speaking), a dark pill at the lower left with a microphone mark (red and crossed out while Muted) and the Name, connection bars at the top right (Connection Bars, 0 - 4 lit), and, with Call Timer, a chip at the top left with a red dot and the elapsed clip time as M:SS (H:MM:SS from an hour). | Done (test_looks videoCall, videoCallStates) |
| 65.9 | Video Camera draws, with Recording on, a red dot (blinking, or steady with Blink Recording Dot off) and REC, or STBY with Recording off; the Format Label and Card Remaining texts; a battery icon and percent; the timecode HH:MM:SS:FF at the bottom left, which is Start Timecode plus the clip frame count at the nominal frame rate (rounded fps; a start in drop-frame form with a semicolon reads the same); a Zoom bar from W to T; a focus frame in the middle (Focus Frame) and corner marks (Corner Marks). | Done (test_looks timecodeAndTimers, blinkingDotsChangeThePicture) |
| 65.10 | The seven looks do not change the existing 99 video effects: the catalogue now has 106 video effects and the Effects section (Toolbox) 13 entries. The guide page is camera-and-screen-looks.md; the Effects library status table lists the seven as works. | Done (test_mediapool, test_looks templatesRender) |

## 66. Media Pool Live Media Preview (hover-preview branch, roadmap: "hovering a clip thumbnail in the Media Pool previews it in the viewer, as in Resolve")

Resolve scrubs a clip when the pointer moves across its thumbnail and offers Live Media Preview as an option of the Media Pool. Kinoklip does the same in the thumbnail view and, on the Cut page, the strip view. Code: `src/ui/mediapool/MediaHoverPreview.cpp`, hover handling in `MediaPoolPanel::hoverAt()`; tests: `tests/panels/test_mediahover.cpp`. The Effects library's Live Preview (47) is separate and shows in the timeline viewer.

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 66.1 | Moving the pointer over the poster of a video or image clip in the thumbnail view (over the film strip in the strip view) asks the page's source viewer to show a frame: the pointer's horizontal place along the poster or strip, left edge to right edge, maps to 0 - 100 % of the clip's length (the last frame is at most 40 ms before the end). A still image shows its picture. The name line, folders, timelines and compound clips, multicam clips, audio-only clips, subtitles, offline clips and the list view start no preview (the viewer keeps what it shows). | Done (test_mediahover pointerPlaceMapsAlongThePoster, positionMapsToTheClipTime) |
| 66.2 | The viewer draws the frame as a temporary picture (as the Effects library does): its clip, playhead, marks and zoom are never changed. Leaving the thumbnail, pressing a mouse button, loading another clip into the viewer, or switching the option off puts the viewer's own picture back exactly. Clicking a clip loads it as before. | Done (test_mediahover viewerShowsTheFrameAndLeavingRestoresIt) |
| 66.3 | A playing viewer is left alone: a hover does nothing while it plays, and a preview shown when playback starts ends. The preview needs a visible viewer: on the Edit page in single viewer mode while the timeline is shown, and on the Cut page while the tape or the timeline is shown, nothing happens. Pages: Edit (source viewer), Media (viewer), Cut (the single clip viewer). | Done (test_mediahover aPlayingViewerIgnoresTheHover) |
| 66.4 | Frames are decoded by the media cache's decoder pool (`MediaCache::frame()`), never on the interface thread. One request is in flight at a time; moves meanwhile only change the wanted time, and when the frame arrives the newest wanted time is requested (latest wins). A frame already cached is shown at once. A request that sends no answer is given up after 1.5 s. | Done (test_mediahover fastMovesAskOnlyForTheNewestFrame) |
| 66.5 | The Media Pool's title bar has an options menu ("..."), with the checkable entry Live Media Preview: on by default, remembered in the settings (`mediapool/livePreview`, also in the settings export), and switching it off ends a preview in progress. Resolve's other place for it, its menu bar, has no entry here (it is a Media Pool option there). | Done (test_mediahover optionOffDoesNothing) |

## 67. Inspector Audio tab: Pitch and clip Equalizer (audio-inspector branch, roadmap: "Inspector Audio tab compared with every audio option Resolve has, Pitch first")

Resolve's Inspector > Audio tab for a timeline audio clip, from its public manual and general knowledge, against Kinoklip:

| Resolve control | Kinoklip | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Volume (dB, keyframable) | Volume | Done earlier |
| Pan (keyframable) | Pan | Done earlier |
| Pitch (semitones) and fine pitch (cents) | Pitch, Pitch Fine | Done (67.1 - 67.4) |
| Equalizer (switch, four bands with type, frequency, gain, Q) | Equalizer section: switch, four bands (low shelf, bell, bell, high shelf), frequency, gain, Q | Done (67.5 - 67.7); the band type is fixed per band, no band-type choice |
| Voice Isolation (Studio, machine learning) | none | Skipped on purpose: needs a model; the roadmap allows only optional, local, labelled AI tools |
| Dialogue Leveler (Studio, machine learning) | none | Skipped on purpose, as above (Clip > Normalize Audio Levels and the compressor in the audio effects cover the plain cases) |
| Audio effects on the clip (Fairlight FX / Effects tab) | Inspector > Effects tab, "Audio Effects" (spec 51) | Done earlier |
| Retime / Elastic Wave (Fairlight retiming of audio) | clip speed, retime curve and "Pitch correction" (spec 29) | Done earlier |
| Normalize Audio Levels, channel mapping (Clip Attributes) | Clip menu commands | Done earlier (spec 37) |
| Equalizer band types (high-pass, low-pass, notch) and more than four bands | none | Left: the mixer's strip EQ has them, the clip EQ does not offer them |
| Keyframes for Pitch and the Equalizer | none | Left on purpose: one value per clip |

Code: `src/core/ParamKeys.h` (keys, stored in `ClipData::audio`), `src/core/BuiltinEffects.cpp` (the two Inspector groups), `src/render/AudioMixer.cpp` (`pitchedSource()` and the clip EQ stage in `mixClip()`), `src/app/commands/ClipCommands.cpp` (`clip.set`). Tests: `tests/render/test_audiopitch.cpp`, `tests/panels/test_inspector.cpp` (spec67), `tests/app/test_commands.cpp` (clipSetPitchAndEqualizer).

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 67.1 | The Audio tab has Pitch (-24 to 24 semitones, step 1) and Pitch Fine (-100 to 100 cents) under Volume and Pan. The pitch is the sum, a frequency factor of 2^((semitones + cents / 100) / 12). Neither has a keyframe button. | Done (test_inspector spec67, test_audiopitch octaveUp, centsAdd) |
| 67.2 | A pitch shift keeps the clip's length and speed: +12 doubles the frequency of a sine, -12 halves it, the sound runs to the end of the clip and starts at its start (no delay). A pitch of 0 (including +1 semitone with -100 cents) leaves the samples bit for bit unchanged. | Done (test_audiopitch zeroLeaves..., octaveUp..., octaveDown..., centsAdd...) |
| 67.3 | How: the clip's source is time stretched by the factor with the same WSOLA as retimed audio with pitch correction (`render/TimeStretch.h`, 1024-sample grains, no new library; FFmpeg's rubberband filter and librubberband are not used), then read back at the factor with cubic interpolation. The stretch is random access, so playback, scrubbing and export give the same samples whatever the block size. Cost is about the pitch-corrected retime (about 12 ms of CPU per second of audio), so it is real time. Quality: good for up to a few semitones; large shifts (an octave or more) on speech sound processed; above +12 semitones the stretched signal is low-passed (33-tap windowed sinc at 0.45 of the new Nyquist) before the read so high frequencies do not fold back (test_audiopitch highPitchUp...); up to +12 there is no band limiting, so very bright material can fold a little. | Done (test_audiopitch blocksOfAnySize...) |
| 67.4 | Pitch applies to clips at normal speed. A clip with a speed change or retime keeps its own time handling and ignores the pitch value (the value is kept). The Inspector greys out Pitch and Pitch Fine on such a clip, with the tooltip "Pitch works on clips at normal speed; this clip has a speed change", and `clip.set --pitch` on it saves the value and returns a `warning` (data and text) saying it is ignored until the speed is normal. | Done (test_inspector spec67, test_commands clipSetPitchAndEqualizer)
| 67.5 | The Audio tab has an Equalizer section with a switch (off by default) and four bands: 1 low shelf (100 Hz), 2 bell (500 Hz), 3 bell (2 kHz), 4 high shelf (8 kHz). Each has Frequency (20 - 20000 Hz), Gain (-24 to 24 dB, 0 default) and Q (0.1 - 18; 0.707 for the shelves, 1 for the bells). Not keyframable. | Done (test_inspector spec67) |
| 67.6 | The clip EQ uses the mixer's Equalizer (`render/Equalizer.h`, RBJ biquads) on the clip's source after pitch, before the clip's audio effects, volume, pan and fades. Switched off it leaves the samples bit for bit unchanged; switched on, a band boosts or cuts its range (a +12 dB bell on a sine at its frequency gives about four times the level, a band far away changes nothing). | Done (test_audiopitch equalizerBoostsItsBand, lowShelfCutsALowTone) |
| 67.7 | Pitch and the Equalizer are saved in the project (`ClipData::audio`), each edit is one undo step, and the command line and MCP get them through `clip.set`: `--pitch` (semitones, -24 to 24, fractions are cents), `--eq on|off`, `--eq1-freq` ... `--eq4-freq`, `--eq1-gain` ... `--eq4-gain`, `--eq1-q` ... `--eq4-q`. Out-of-range values fail with `out_of_range`. `clip.list` shows `pitch`. | Done (test_commands clipSetPitchAndEqualizer, test_inspector spec67) |

## 68. Layers in the text title (title-layers branch, roadmap: "Titles: more options on the existing text title, mainly layers (several text and shape layers in one title); no new title type")

A Title clip holds an ordered list of layers, drawn from the bottom to the top: Text layers (everything the title had: font, size, colour, tracking, line spacing, alignment, outline, shadow, background box, position, anchor, scroll, and now rotation and opacity) and Shape layers (rectangle, rounded rectangle, ellipse, line). It is still the one title clip and the one Effects > Titles entry; no new title type. The title a project had before is one Text layer: it stays in `ClipData::title` (older readers, presets, keyframe paths and commands mean that layer), and the extra layers are `ClipData::titleLayers` plus `ClipData::titleBase` (where the first layer sits in the stack, whether it is shown, its name). Project files gain `layers` and `baseLayer` on a clip only when a title uses them, so the file version is unchanged. Code: `src/core/TitleLayers.h`, `src/render/TitleRenderer.cpp` (`TitleStyle::stack`), `src/ui/inspector/InspectorTitleLayers.cpp`, `title.layer_*` in `src/app/commands/GeneratorCommands.cpp`. Guide: add-layers-to-a-title.md. Tests: `tests/render/test_titlelayers.cpp`, `tests/panels/test_titlelayersui.cpp`.

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 68.1 | A title without layers renders exactly as before layers existed (the same code path, pixel for pixel: a stored picture made by the unchanged renderer is compared), and a project file without `layers` loads as that one Text layer. | Done (test_titlelayers goldenSingleText, spec68_1_oldTitleIsOneLayer) |
| 68.2 | Every title of the Effects library (Text, the three lower thirds, Scroll, Text Box) is a one-layer title that survives save and load unchanged and renders the same. | Done (test_titlelayers spec68_2_presetsRoundTripAsOneLayer) |
| 68.3 | Layers are drawn from the bottom of the list to the top; the same order in the viewer (GPU and CPU), playback and export, since all use one title image per frame. | Done (test_titlelayers spec68_3_layersDrawBottomToTop, test_compositor spec68_16_titleLayersDrawInOrder) |
| 68.4 | Layer kinds: Text (all the parameters of the title) and Shape. A Shape layer has a type (Rectangle, Rounded Rectangle, Ellipse, Line), Width, Height, Corner Radius (rounded rectangles), Position X / Y (0 - 1 of the frame from the left and from the bottom, as 14.7), Rotation, Fill Color and Fill Opacity, Stroke Color and Width (centred on the edge), and Opacity of the whole layer. A Line is a bar Width long and Height thick with round ends. Hidden layers are not drawn; a title whose layers are all hidden draws nothing. | Done (test_titlelayers spec68_4_twoTextLayersAndAShape, spec68_6_rotationAndOpacity) |
| 68.5 | Every layer has its own keyframes (Position, Size, Rotation, Opacity, colours' numbers ... every animatable parameter); keys are clip-local frames, follow head trims and splits, and join on a join. The Keyframes panel and `keyframe list/set/delete` (option `--layer`) show them under the layer's name. | Done (test_titlelayers spec68_5_keyframesPerLayer, test_commands spec68_13_titleLayers) |
| 68.6 | Text layers gain Rotation (degrees clockwise around the middle of the text block, box and shadow turn with it) and Opacity (percent of the whole layer); both are in the title's Inspector group, default 0 and 100, and change nothing for older titles. | Done (test_titlelayers spec68_6_rotationAndOpacity) |
| 68.7 | Layer operations: add Text or Shape on top, duplicate (the copy sits directly above the original), delete, move up / down, show / hide, rename. The first layer (the title the clip was made with) can be moved, hidden, renamed and duplicated but not deleted (decision: it lives in the fields older readers and the presets use). Layer ids are numbers local to the clip. | Done (test_titlelayers spec68_7_layerOperations) |
| 68.8 | Layers, their kinds, names, visibility, order, parameters with keyframes, and the first layer's place, name and visibility survive save and load. | Done (test_titlelayers spec68_8_projectRoundTrip) |
| 68.9 | The title's texture cache key and the render cache key follow the layer data: any change to a layer, its order, visibility or keys changes the picture's key; a title without layers keeps its old key. | Done (test_titlelayers spec68_9_cacheKeyFollowsLayers, test_rendercache titleLayersChangeTheKey) |
| 68.10 | Inspector > Title shows a Layers group above the controls: a list (top layer first, tick = shown, double-click renames), buttons Text, Shape (a menu of the four shapes), Duplicate, Move up, Move down, Delete. The controls below edit the selected layer (a Shape layer shows the Shape group, a Text layer the Text title's group named after the layer; the first layer shows its preset group as before). A title has no on-screen controls in the viewer, so the list is the only way to pick a layer. | Done (test_titlelayersui spec68_10_listShowsTheLayers, spec68_12_controlsEditTheSelectedLayer) |
| 68.11 | Every layer operation (add Text, add Shape, duplicate, move, hide, rename, delete) is one undo step that undo takes back and redo restores. | Done (test_titlelayersui spec68_11_everyOperationUndoes) |
| 68.12 | Editing several selected titles together changes their first layers only; the other layers are per clip. | Decision (MultiClipEdit.cpp: ParamScope::TitleLayer is never matched between clips) |
| 68.13 | Commands (CLI `title layer list|add|remove|move|set`, MCP `title_layer_*`): `title.layer_list`, `title.layer_add` (kind text or shape, shape, duplicate, name, text, set), `title.layer_remove`, `title.layer_move` (by), `title.layer_set` (layer 0 is the first layer; name, visible, text, font, size, color, set), and `--layer` on `keyframe.set/list/delete`. Errors: `unknown_layer`, `unknown_parameter`, `bad_value` (not a title clip, layer 0 removal), `out_of_range`, `track_locked`. | Done (test_commands spec68_13_titleLayers) |
| 68.14 | The render cache (Smart / User) chunk keys change for the chunks of a title when a layer changes. | Done (test_rendercache titleLayersChangeTheKey) |
| 68.15 | The command line takes `title layer add` for the command `title.layer_add` (three words), lists it as such, and the MCP server names it `title_layer_add`. | Done (test_cli spec68_15_titleLayerWords) |
| 68.16 | The compositor (viewer and playback) draws layers in order; GPU and CPU agree. | Done (test_compositor spec68_16_titleLayersDrawInOrder) |
| 68.17 | An exported frame shows the layers, with their keyframes, as the viewer does. | Done (test_titleexport spec68_17_layersExportLikeTheViewer) |

## 69. Colour emoji in titles and captions (title-layers branch, roadmap: "Titles: emoji in subtitles and titles, in colour, in the viewer and in exports")

Qt draws the stroke and shadow of a title from glyph outlines, and a colour emoji font has none, so before this section an emoji in a title or caption came out as an empty box or nothing. Now `renderTitle` cuts the emoji out of each line (`src/render/EmojiText.cpp`: one run per emoji sequence, with flags, zero width joiner sequences, skin tones, keycaps and the emoji selector) and draws them as glyphs with `QPainter::drawText`, in colour, on a layer of their own; the plain text around them keeps its outline path. Captions go through the same renderer, so they are covered with the same code. Fonts: the system's colour emoji font when there is one (Noto Color Emoji, Twemoji, Apple, Segoe); otherwise the Noto Color Emoji (SIL OFL 1.1, GPL-compatible) bundled in the AppImage under `usr/share/kinoklip/fonts`, registered at the first title render (`emoji::registerBundledFont()`). Tests: `tests/render/test_emoji.cpp`, `tests/export/test_titleexport.cpp`; they need a colour emoji font, found in the system or named by the environment variable `KK_EMOJI_FONT`, and skip without one. Guide: emoji-in-titles-and-captions.md.

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 69.1 | The text of a line is split into emoji and plain runs: one run per emoji (a flag of two regional indicators, a family joined by zero width joiners, a skin-tone modifier, a digit with the keycap mark, a symbol like the heart or the check mark that is text by default only with the emoji selector U+FE0F); the runs join back to the line. Plain text, digits, copyright signs and symbols without the selector stay plain. | Done (test_emoji spec69_1_splitsEmojiSequences) |
| 69.2 | An emoji in a title shows in colour (more than two distinct hues in the glyph box for a title of an apple, a green apple and a wave), not as a box or a black outline; the block is centred with the emoji's width counted. Text without emoji takes the unchanged code path (68.1). | Done (test_emoji spec69_2_titleEmojiAreInColour) |
| 69.3 | Outline, drop shadow, background box and rotation work around emoji: the outline is drawn from the path of the plain text only (emoji have no outline of their own), the shadow of an emoji is its shape in the shadow colour, the box covers the emoji, and a rotated layer turns its emoji. | Done (test_emoji spec69_3_outlineShadowAndBackground) |
| 69.4 | Emoji in a caption (the subtitle track's rendering) show in colour in the viewer. | Done (test_emoji spec69_4_captionEmojiAreInColour) |
| 69.5 | Emoji in a layer of a title with several layers draw in colour. | Done (test_emoji spec69_5_emojiInALayer) |
| 69.6 | An exported frame shows the emoji of the title and of a burnt-in caption in colour, equal to the viewer's frame. | Done (test_titleexport spec69_6_emojiExportLikeTheViewer) |
| 69.7 | The release bundle carries Noto Color Emoji (`usr/share/kinoklip/fonts/NotoColorEmoji.ttf`, listed in `bundled-libraries.txt` with its licence, attribution.md); it is used only when the host has no colour emoji font. Without either, the emoji cannot be drawn in colour (the font fallback draws what the host offers). | Done (scripts/package_linux.sh, packaging/linux/readme.md; not run here: the AppImage build) |

## 70. Tracking effects of the 1.2 gap list (trackers branch, owner request for 1.2)

The last three entries of the Effects library gap: Match Move, Surface Tracker (both Transform) and
Warper (Warp). Written from the names and the general idea only, no code, text or assets of any
other editor. They reuse the tracking maths of `src/compositing/tracking/` (PointTracker,
PlanarTracker, the Homography fit) and the clip's transform. Tests: `tests/render/test_trackers.cpp`
(tk).

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 70.1 | Match Move (Effects > Transform folder, `match_move`) follows a point or a region of the picture and moves its clip with it. Settings: Track (This Clip, or Footage Below), Tracking (Position, or Position, Scale and Rotation), Direction (Follow the Track, or Hold the Track in Place, which cancels the movement), Region X / Y / Size (percent of the picture, where the tracked thing is at the frame the track starts on), Strength (0 - 200 %), Offset X / Offset Y (px, keyframable). Footage Below tracks the video tracks under the clip's track, This Clip tracks the clip alone as the viewer shows it (its other effects and transform included, Match Move left out). | Done (tk descriptorIsRegistered, followsAMovingPatch, holdTheTrackInPlaceOnTheClipItself) |
| 70.2 | The Effects tab shows under the settings of a Match Move Track Backward, Track Forward, Clear Track, Cancel, a progress bar and a status line. Track Forward follows from the playhead to the end of the clip, Track Backward to its start; the region must sit on the feature at the playhead. A second run from the same frame (the other direction) adds to the stored track, a run from another frame replaces it. The tracking runs off the UI thread on pictures the playback engine renders from a private copy of the timeline (`PlaybackEngine::renderImageOf()`), and the result is stored with the effect in one undo step "Track". A cancelled run, a clip removed meanwhile or fewer than two found frames leave the clip untouched and add no undo step; a lost pattern keeps the frames found before it and says why. | Done (tk refusals; the buttons by hand) |
| 70.3 | The track is stored with the effect in a hidden parameter (`trackData`, a compressed table keyed by the clip's SOURCE time in seconds, `core/EffectTrack.h`): trimming, slipping, splitting, speed changes and reversing keep every frame's own value, both halves of a split clip keep the track, copy / paste and undo carry it. Values between keys are interpolated, outside the tracked range the nearest key is held. The project file format is unchanged (older builds ignore the parameter); damaged text is dropped. | Done (tk trackTableRoundTrip, trackTablesMerge, trackIsKeyedBySourceTimeAndStaleWhenTheRegionChanges, trackSurvivesTheProjectFile) |
| 70.4 | Only the tracker's inputs need a new track: Track (source), Tracking, and the region. A stored track whose inputs differ from the effect's settings is not used (the clip renders as without the effect) and the status line says "Track again"; changing them back uses it again. Strength, Direction and the offsets are applied when a frame is drawn. | Done (tk trackIsKeyedBySourceTimeAndStaleWhenTheRegionChanges) |
| 70.5 | The movement is the track's change since the frame the track started on (the reference): the clip stays where it is at that frame and follows from there. With Position the clip moves by the point's movement; with Scale and Rotation the four corners of the region (PlanarTracker) give the move, the turn and the scale (least squares about the region centre). Hold the Track in Place applies the inverse. Strength scales the move, the turn (degrees) and the scale (as a power); Offset is added in frame pixels afterwards. | Done (tk motionOfAPointAndARegion, holdTheTrackInPlaceOnTheClipItself) |
| 70.6 | The movement is part of the clip's transform in the compositor (`render::clipTransform()`, after the clip's own Transform and Dynamic Zoom, in frame pixels, so a scaled clip still sits on the tracked thing), in the GPU and the CPU path, in the viewer and in the export (one code path). On synthetic footage (a square moving 4 px per frame) a graphic follows the square within 1 px with Position and within 1.5 px with Scale and Rotation. | Done (tk followsAMovingPatch, planarVariantFollowsToo) |
| 70.7 | Refusals shown in the status line: the playhead outside the clip, a clip that is not on a video track, Footage Below on the lowest track. | Done (tk refusals) |
| 70.8 | Not done, on purpose: no command for tracking (the command line and the MCP server can set the parameters with `effect set`, but a track is made in the Inspector); moving the clip on the timeline against the footage below (or slipping it) makes the track stale in the footage's sense and needs a new track (the key is the clip's own source time); no keyboard shortcut; no on-screen box for the region in the viewer (the region is set with its three numbers). | Decision |
| 70.9 | Surface Tracker (Effects > Transform folder, `surface_tracker`) follows a surface that bends or turns with a square grid of points (Grid Density 2 - 8 per side, default 4) laid over a region (Region X / Y / Width / Height, percent of the picture). Each point is followed by its own pattern match; a perspective map is fitted to the points that matched, and the rest of every point's movement (the bend) is kept, smoothed against its neighbours by Smoothing (0 - 100 %). A point that was lost, or whose bend jumps away from its neighbours', is carried along by the others. The Track buttons, the stored track (one table of the grid points keyed by source time), the stale-track rule and the refusals are those of Match Move (70.2 - 70.4, 70.7); Track is This Clip or Footage Below. | Done (tk meshFollowsAPlanarMotion, meshFollowsABend, smoothingBlendsTowardsTheNeighbours, surfaceDescriptor) |
| 70.10 | Apply chooses what the track does. Pin to the Surface moves the clip by the perspective map that takes the reference grid to the grid now (applied in `clipTransform()` after Match Move, in frame pixels); Warp with the Surface bends the clip's own picture with the displacement of the grid (one GPU pass, Catmull-Rom between the grid points, held outside the grid). Direction (Follow, or Hold in Place, which uses the inverse map in Pin mode), Strength and Offset apply in Pin mode; Strength also scales the Warp. On synthetic footage a 4 x 4 mesh follows a moving textured plane within 1 px and a sinusoidal bend within 1.2 px, where one perspective map is off by more. | Done (tk meshFollowsABend, surfacePinMovesTheClip, surfaceWarpBendsThePicture) |
| 70.11 | Limits, on purpose: Warp assumes the clip's picture fills the frame (use Pin for a transformed clip); a surface that leaves the picture or is covered for long is lost (the frames found before are kept); the mesh drifts on very long bends, so track in parts. | Decision |
| 70.12 | Warper (Effects > Warp, `warper`) bends the picture with a grid of control points: Columns and Rows (2 - 4, default 3 x 3, not keyframable) and, for every point (row 1 at the top), a keyframable move in px on x and y (Point r, c X / Y, -2000 to 2000, 0 = in place). The picture follows the points smoothly (Catmull-Rom between the points, so a point moves what sits on it, and the part of the picture outside the grid takes the move of the nearest edge). With no point moved the pixels are untouched. Parameters of points outside the current Columns and Rows are kept and ignored. Same GPU pass as the Warp mode of Surface Tracker (70.10). | Done (tk warperDescriptorAndIdentity, warperMovesWhatSitsOnAPoint) |
| 70.13 | Not done, on purpose: no on-screen handles for the Warper points (the points are numbers in the Inspector, keyframable like any parameter); 4 x 4 points at most. | Decision |

## 71. Drag hover switches pages (drag-switch branch, owner request for 1.2)

Holding a drag over a button of the page bar for a moment opens that page, so files can be dragged
from a file manager (or clips from the Media Pool) to the Media, Edit or Cut page without letting
go. Research, public documentation and common behaviour only, no code of any other program:
spring-loaded folders in file managers, and tabs of browsers and panels that open while something
is dragged over them. Common to them: a short delay of about a second, and feedback that shows the
countdown. Chosen here: the button is highlighted and a fill sweeps across it from left to right
with a bar along its bottom edge; when the sweep is full the page opens. Tests:
`tests/app/test_pagespring.cpp` (ps).

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 71.1 | Every page button accepts every drag that enters it (file URLs from outside, the app's own media and clip drags), so the pointer never shows the forbidden cursor over the page bar. | Done (ps hoverSwitchesPage) |
| 71.2 | While a drag rests on the button of another page the button is highlighted and filled from left to right over `kSpringLoadDelayMs` (900 ms). When the fill is complete the page opens, exactly as if the button had been clicked; the drag goes on, so the files can be dropped on the new page. | Done (ps hoverSwitchesPage) |
| 71.3 | Leaving the button before the fill is complete cancels it: the feedback disappears and the page does not change. | Done (ps leavingEarlyCancels) |
| 71.4 | The button of the current page accepts the drag and shows nothing and does nothing. A disabled page button (a page not built yet) takes no drag. | Done (ps currentPageButtonDoesNothing) |
| 71.5 | A drop on a page button opens that page at once and is taken with no action (it does not pass on to the main window, which would import dropped files): nothing is imported. | Done (ps dropSwitchesAtOnceAndImportsNothing) |
| 71.6 | With the UI animations off (Preferences, the desktop's no-animation setting or `KK_NO_ANIMATIONS`) the button shows the plain highlight without the moving fill; the page still opens after the same delay. | Done |
| 71.7 | The helper is `kk::SpringLoad` (`src/ui/common/SpringLoad.h`), an event filter that any button can use with one line; the page bar installs it on its buttons. | Done |

## 72. Playing into an animated clip across a cut (kf-stutter branch, owner report for 1.3)

Report: several clips cut from one screen recording lie one after another; one has Transform
keyframes (zoom and rotation, from its first frame). Started on that clip, playback is smooth;
started in the clip before it and played across the cut, the viewer lags for about a second
while the keys animate (export is fine). Guarantee: animated Transform values never change how
a clip is prepared or presented; crossing a cut into a keyed clip loses no more frames than the
same cut into an unkeyed clip, than a cut between two files, or than starting on the clip.
