Measured with `tests/playback/test_keyframecut.cpp` (kc) and `tests/app/test_playheadload.cpp` (ph, whole main window) on a CPU-only machine (software
decoder, CPU compositor and llvmpipe OpenGL, 720p30, 1080p60): 0 frames lost in the first second
of the keyed clip in every case, longest hold one frame time; the lag of the report was not
reproduced there, so the test compares the cases with each other (generous margins) and
guards against the regression.

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 72.1 | Playing across a cut between two clips of the same file into a clip with Transform keys from frame 0 loses at most 3 frames more than the same cut without keys, than the same cut from another file, and than starting on the keyed clip, in the clip's first second. | Done (kc crossing) |
| 72.2 | The longest hold between two shown frames in that second is at most 80 ms above the unkeyed crossing's or the start-on-clip's. | Done (kc crossing) |
| 72.3 | The viewer's GUI thread (Inspector on the clip's Transform, Keyframes panel, on-screen Transform controls, all following the playhead) is no busier when playing across a cut into the selected keyed clip than into the unkeyed one: the longest event-loop stall measured is within 60 ms of the others (measured: ~20 ms in all three cases, 0 or 1 frames lost). | Done (ph crossingIntoSelectedKeyedClip) |
| 72.4 | A slow decoder open (a hardware decoder's session setup) does not make a same-file jump lose frames: the cut look-ahead starts 2 s before the cut, and 2 x the last preparation's measured cost (at most 4 s) when preparations were slow. Measured with a delay hook in the decoder pool (`DecoderPool::setOpenDelayMs`), playing from 2.5 s before the cut: open 300 / 800 / 1200 ms lost 0 / 2-3 / 11-15 frames with the old 1 s look-ahead, 0 / 0 / 0 now. | Done (kc slowOpen) |

## 73. Dialogs open big enough for their content (ui-sizes-drag branch, roadmap 1.3)

Owner report: Project Settings opened tiny (about 390 x 200); its section list and its page both
showed scroll bars and clipped text. Rule for every dialog and separate window: it opens at least
as big as its content needs, so no scroll bar shows when none is needed, and never bigger than
the screen. The size comes from the size hints of the content, not from numbers per dialog; one
helper does it: `src/ui/common/DialogSizing.h`. Tests: `tests/panels/test_dialogsizing.cpp` (ds), with
the screen set to 1366 x 768.

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 73.1 | `dialogsizing::fit(window, preferred)` makes the minimum size of the window the size its content asks for (size hint of its layout), and the opening size max(minimum, preferred); both are capped to the available screen less 60 px for the title bar and frame. | Done (ds sizeIsCappedToTheScreen) |
| 73.2 | The pages of Project Settings, Preferences and the Smart Bin rules sit in `PageScrollArea`, whose size hint is the size of its page: the dialog asks for the widest and tallest page, so every section shows without a scroll bar at the opening size (on a screen big enough). Where the screen is too small the page scrolls instead. | Done (ds projectSettingsEveryPage, preferencesEverySection) |
| 73.3 | Project Settings cannot be made smaller than its content, whatever size a window manager remembers; its section list is as wide as its longest section name (23.20). | Done (ds projectSettingsIsNotTiny) |
| 73.4 | These dialogs use the helper: Project Settings, Preferences, Keyboard Customization, History, Guide, Smart Bin, New Project, Bug Report, Export Log File, Export and Import Settings, Copy Link, Clip Attributes, Normalize Audio, Change Clip Speed, Project Manager. Each opens inside a 1366 x 768 screen with no scroll bar on its first page (lists, trees and text previews whose content can be long may scroll). | Done (ds, one test per dialog) |
| 73.5 | A saved window geometry (Project Manager) is used only when the restored window is not smaller than the minimum size; otherwise the window opens at its computed size (`restoreGeometryIfFits`). | Done (ds savedGeometryBelowMinimumIsIgnored) |
| 73.6 | Small question dialogs built inline (Multicam options, guide position) and the standard message and file dialogs size themselves from their layout and are not changed. The main window has its own minimum size (separate roadmap line). | Done |

## 74. Drag hover opens panels (ui-sizes-drag branch, roadmap 1.3)

The same hover as the page buttons (71) on the panel buttons of the interface bar: a drag resting on
the button of a closed panel that takes drops opens the panel, so the files can be dropped into it
without letting go. Panels that take drops: Media Pool, Inspector, Nodes. The other panels (Effects,
Edit Index, Keyframes, Gallery, Clips, Color Timeline, Scopes, Mixer, Metadata) take no drops, so their
buttons are plain buttons. Tests: `tests/app/test_pagespring.cpp` (ps).

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 74.1 | The button of a panel that takes drops behaves like a page button (71.1, 71.2, 71.3): it accepts the drag, shows the highlight and the sweeping fill over `kSpringLoadDelayMs`, and when the fill is complete the closed panel opens, as if the button had been clicked. | Done (ps hoverOpensClosedPanel) |
| 74.2 | If the panel is already open (the button is checked) the hover does nothing: nothing is shown and the panel is not closed. | Done (ps hoverOnOpenPanelDoesNothing) |
| 74.3 | The buttons of panels that take no drops are not spring-loaded and refuse drags. | Done (ps buttonOfPanelWithoutDropsIsNotSpringLoaded) |
| 74.4 | A drop on such a button opens the closed panel at once and is taken with no action (71.5): nothing is imported. | Done (ps dropOnPanelButtonOpensAndImportsNothing) |
| 74.5 | The table `kPanelToggles` in `MainWindow.cpp` has a `takesDrops` flag per panel; `InterfaceBar::enableSpringLoad` installs `SpringLoad` on that button. | Done |

## 75. Voice-over: record from the microphone onto an audio track (voice-over branch, roadmap 1.3)

Record a narration straight onto an audio track while the timeline plays, as Resolve's Voice
Over tool and the record arm of its Audio page do (public documentation only; nothing is
copied). The microphone sits behind a small interface (`playback/AudioInput.h`), so the tests feed
it a generated signal (a tone with a click at a known sample); the logic is in
`ui/audio/VoiceOverController`. Tests: `tests/app/test_voiceover.cpp` (vo). What cannot be tested
without a real microphone and sound card is listed in 75.24.

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 75.1 | Every audio track header has an arm button (R), on the Edit page and on the Audio page (where 37.3 had it disabled), and the Audio page's mixer strip has the same R button. Arming is a view state of the session: it is not saved in the project and is not an undo step. | Done (vo armButtonsOnEditAudioAndMixer) |
| 75.2 | Only one track is armed at a time: arming another track moves the arm; clicking the armed track's button disarms it. Video and subtitle tracks have no arm button. | Done (vo onlyOneTrackIsArmedAndTheInputIsMetered, armButtonsOnEditAudioAndMixer) |
| 75.3 | Right-clicking the R button of a header or a mixer strip opens the input menu: "Default (<name>)" and every system audio input (`QMediaDevices::audioInputs()`), the default device first, the track's current choice checked. The header's right-click menu also has Arm for Voice-Over and a Voice-Over Input submenu. The choice is per track and per session; "Default" follows Preferences > System > Audio I/O > Input device, which follows the system default. | Done (vo inputChoicePerTrack) |
| 75.4 | While a track is armed its input is open and metered: the header shows a thin level bar along its foot (dBFS scale, -48 dB at the left, yellow above -18 dB, red above -6 dB) and a red mark for 2 s after a sample reached full scale; the mixer strip shows the same bar under its buttons. The meter runs while armed, during the count-in and while recording. | Done (vo onlyOneTrackIsArmedAndTheInputIsMetered) |
| 75.5 | The microphone is never routed to the speakers (no monitoring), so there is no feedback; the preferences page says to use headphones for the timeline's sound. Monitoring while recording is not offered. | Done (by design) |
| 75.6 | A locked track cannot be armed or recorded onto, and a track that is deleted or locked while armed disarms. Switching the active timeline disarms. | Done (vo lockedTrackCannotBeArmed, removedTrackDisarms) |
| 75.7 | The Record command (Playback > Record Voice-Over, the red button in the viewer's transport bar, shortcut Ctrl+Alt+R, action `transport.record`; the key is free in every built-in keymap and kept by all of them) toggles: it starts the count-in and, pressed again, ends it. | Done (vo recordActionIsRegistered) |
| 75.8 | Count-in: Preferences > System > Audio I/O > "Voice-over count-in", 0 to 10 s, default 3 s, 0 = none. The viewer shows the seconds left (3, 2, 1) in large numbers over the picture; the recording and the playback start when it reaches 0. Sound arriving during the count-in is metered but not recorded. Space, K, or Stop during the count-in cancel it and make nothing (Space does not start playback then). | Done (vo countInTimingAndNoRecordingBeforeIt, cancellingTheCountInMakesNothing) |
| 75.9 | After the count-in the timeline plays from the playhead and the input is written to a WAV file. A red "REC" mark shows in the viewer's corner. Space or Stop ends the take, as does playback reaching the end of the timeline; the recording is tied to playback and stops where playback stops, so Record is refused when the playhead is at the end of the timeline (nothing would play) and while the timeline is already playing. | Done (refusals by code; playback itself see 75.24) |
| 75.10 | The take is a PCM WAV file with the device's sample rate (48 kHz when the device offers it) and channel count (mono stays mono), 24 bit by default or 16 bit (Preferences > Audio I/O > Voice-over WAV format). Its header is correct when the take ends; its length is exactly the samples received after the count-in. | Done (vo takeWavContentAndLength, sixteenBitStereoTake) |
| 75.11 | Where takes are written: a linked project writes `<track> take N.wav` into a "Voice Over" folder next to the project file (`<project folder>/Voice Over/`). A single-file project writes the take aside and copies it into the project file through the media store (39.19), exactly like an import, and removes the aside copy. A project that was never saved uses "Voice Over" in the application's data folder. A name that is not valid in a file name is cleaned (`/` becomes `_`). | Done (vo takeWavContentAndLength, singleFileProjectStoresTheTake, trackNameIsUsedInTheFileName) |
| 75.12 | Take numbering: N is one more than the highest number already used for that track name, counting the files in the folder and the media pool items, so numbers of deleted takes are not reused and each track counts on its own ("A1 take 1.wav", "A1 take 2.wav", "A2 take 1.wav"). The track name is its name in the header (A1, or the name the user gave it). | Done (vo takeIsImportedIntoTheVoiceOverBinAndNumbered) |
| 75.13 | The finished take is imported into the Media Pool, into a bin named "Voice Over" (created at the first take, reused after). Earlier takes stay in the pool. | Done (vo takeIsImportedIntoTheVoiceOverBinAndNumbered) |
| 75.14 | The take is placed as a clip named after the file on the armed track at the playhead frame where recording started, overwriting whatever is under it on that track (clips are cut at the edges, as in an Overwrite edit); other tracks do not change. | Done (vo clipIsPlacedAtTheStartFrameWithLatencyApplied, overwritesWhatIsUnderTheTake) |
| 75.15 | Importing the take, creating the bin and placing the clip are one undo step ("Voice Over"). Undo removes the clip, restores the overwritten clips and takes the take out of the Media Pool and the bin; the WAV file stays on disk. Redo puts all of it back. | Done (vo overwritesWhatIsUnderTheTake) |
| 75.16 | Latency: the sound reaches the file later than it was spoken. The total latency is the input's reported latency (for `QAudioSource`, the size of its capture buffer; 0 when the device reports none) plus Preferences > Audio I/O > "Voice-over extra latency" (0 to 1000 ms, default 0). The clip starts at the start frame and its source in point is the latency, so the head of the file (before the voice) is skipped and the voice lines up with the picture to the microsecond; the file itself keeps the whole recording, so Slip can reveal the head. The clip is as long as the rest of the file (whole frames, rounded down, at least one). | Done (vo clipIsPlacedAtTheStartFrameWithLatencyApplied, zeroLatencyClipStartsAtTheHeadOfTheFile) |
| 75.17 | No microphone (or permission denied): arming is refused, the R button stays off and a message names the problem ("No microphone or other audio input was found. Connect one and try again." or that the system does not let Kinoklip use the microphone); the tooltip of every R button shows the same text; Record is refused with "Arm an audio track first" and nothing is created. A device that cannot be opened is reported the same way. | Done (vo noDeviceRefusesWithMessage, deviceThatCannotOpenRefusesArm, armButtonWithoutMicrophoneDoesNotArm) |
| 75.18 | If the input breaks while recording (device unplugged, permission revoked) a message says so, what was recorded so far is kept as a take, and the track is disarmed. | Done (vo recordingNeedsAnArmedTrackAndStopsWhenTheInputBreaks) |
| 75.19 | A recording that received no samples makes no take (no file, no undo step) and says "Nothing was recorded". | Done (vo emptyTakeIsDiscarded) |
| 75.20 | Voice-over has no command-layer command (`app/commands`, MCP, CLI): recording needs a live microphone and a person speaking against the playback, which a command cannot supply. The finished takes are ordinary media and clips that the command layer can already edit. | Done (by design) |
| 75.21 | Preferences > System > Audio I/O gains Input device, Voice-over count-in, Voice-over extra latency and Voice-over WAV format; the input device id is machine specific and is not exported with the settings (`system/audioInputDevice`); the other three are (`voiceOver/*`). | Done |
| 75.22 | The record arm and the armed input stay open (the operating system may show its microphone indicator) until the track is disarmed. | Done (by design) |
| 75.23 | Limits: one microphone input per take (no multi-track recording); no punch-in/out region, no monitoring, no input gain control (use the system's mixer); WAV files are limited to about 4 GiB (about 6 hours of 48 kHz mono 24 bit); the capture format is whatever Qt Multimedia offers (8, 16 and 32 bit integer and float are converted, 24 bit devices arrive as 32 bit). | Done (documented) |
| 75.24 | Not tested without a real microphone and sound card: the Qt Multimedia capture itself (`QtAudioInput`: format negotiation, reported buffer size as latency, the permission check), the start of the real playback from the playhead and its stop with Space, the end of the timeline ending the take, and the true offset between the picture and the voice on a given computer (the extra latency setting exists for what the buffer size does not tell). The recorder logic, the files, the placement, the undo step and the interface are tested with a generated signal. | Documented |

## 76. Page turns during playback (issue2 branch, GitHub issue 2)

A 30-minute 4K 100 fps clip lost most frames after the timeline view page-scrolled during playback,
worse at 300 % speed. Measured with `tests/playback/test_pageturnplayback.cpp` (1080p 100 fps, GOP
600, three CPUs, filmstrip and waveform on, 300 % speed): the page scroll itself costs nothing (same
frames with "Clip Name Only", no GUI stall, the engine is not touched), but the tiles of the new page
did: the trickled thumbnail jobs (1.36) decoded from the last keyframe to the exact frame, hundreds of
frames each, next to the playback decoder (70 of 94 fps, 25 % of the frames lost; Qt's "lowest
priority" does nothing for normal Linux threads). The reporter's 4K hardware decoder and slow disk make
it worse; that part is checked on his hardware.

| # | Behaviour | Status |
|---|-----------|--------|
| 76.1 | A thumbnail job that starts while the program plays takes the keyframe at or before its time (one decoded frame, a short read) instead of the exact frame, so the filmstrip of a new page fills at once without loading the playback. | Done (`test_mediacache` trickledTilesAreKeyframesUntilPlaybackStops) |
| 76.2 | Such a job decodes in software, never on the hardware device the playback uses, and its thread runs in the idle scheduling class where the system has one (Linux `SCHED_IDLE`). | Done |
| 76.3 | Keyframe tiles live in memory only (never written to the disk cache). When playback stops they are dropped, `thumbnailReady` makes the views ask again, and the exact frames replace them. | Done (`test_mediacache` trickledTilesAreKeyframesUntilPlaybackStops) |
| 76.4 | Playback with the view following the playhead shows at least 97 % of the frames of the same playback with the view fixed, and loses at most 30 frames more. | Done (`test_pageturnplayback` pageTurnDoesNotHurtPlayback) |

## Summary

- 160 numbered items on the parity branch; the resolveedit branch added 25 (7.11-7.13, 9.6,
  10.7-10.10, 16.5, 18.6, 19.6, 19.7, 21.7, 23.1-23.7) and fixed 11 of the 13 deferred
  ones (4.11, 8.17, 13.5, 13.7, 13.9, 16.4, 18.4, 18.5, 21.6; 10.4 and 19.3 changed from
  Decision to Fixed).
- The resolveedit branch left 1.16, 4.14 and 8.15 deferred; the editpolish branch (roadmap v3,
  docs/archive/polish-v3.md) fixed them and 23.7 audio scrubbing, and added 2.10 (typed timecodes) and
  21.9 (drag offset label).
- The editfix branch (alpha.2 feedback) changed 8.10 (Shift+[ / ] leave a gap, Ctrl+Shift+[ / ]
  ripple), 23.5, and added 13.10 and 24.1 - 24.6.
- The keyframes branch fixed 15.8 and added 15.9 - 15.13 (keyframe editor, curve editor,
  Bezier interpolation, section diamonds).
- The retime branch (roadmap v2) added 29.1 - 29.16 (speed points, ramps, reverse / freeze,
  retime process, pitch-corrected audio) and updated 23.7.
- The masks branch (roadmap v2) added 27.1 - 27.13 (shape masks, on-screen mask controls).
- The proxies branch (roadmap v2) added 30.1 - 30.18 (proxy and optimized media, render cache).
- The kfwindow branch (alpha.2 feedback #10) added 15.14 - 15.20 (the Keyframes panel, docked
  or floating, sharing its editing code with the inline lanes).
- The adjsubs branch (v2) added 28.1 - 28.14 (adjustment clips, generators, subtitles).
- The compound branch (v2) added 25.1 - 25.11 (several timelines per project, timeline tabs on
  by default, drop-down mode) and 26.1 - 26.13 (compound clips, nested timelines).
- The multicam branch (v2) added 32.1 - 32.13 (multicam clips, the multicam viewer, sync by
  waveform / timecode / in points).
- The attrs branch (v2) added 31.1 - 31.12 (audio level lines, fade handles, Clip Attributes,
  stacked timelines).
- The bins branch (v2) added 33.1 - 33.19 (nested bins, smart bins, power bins, clip colours,
  flags, metadata editing, usage commands).
- The themecolors branch (alpha.3 feedback #6) added 34.1 - 34.5 (Kinoklip default scheme, scheme-driven editor colours).
- The cutpage branch (v2.5) added 35.1 - 35.25 (the Cut page: dual timeline, source tape, smart
  edits, strip view, fast review; 35.19 - 35.25 deferred).
- The animations branch (v2.2) added 36.1 - 36.12 (display-only timeline animations and their
  preference; 36.12 deferred).
- The audiopage branch (v2.5) added 37.1 - 37.33 (the Audio page: mixer with buses, EQ,
  dynamics, automation, meters and loudness; 37.20 - 37.33 deferred).
